# OmniPost — Consolidated Master Plan

> **Single source of planning and work.** It replaces and merges `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_ES.md` (feature spine → §2-4) + `PENDING_WORK_INVENTORY.md` (consolidation → §1/§5) — **`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_ES.md` retired 2026-06-29** (archived on 2026-10-06 and deleted from the tree on 2026-10-17; [its last version](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/2ec1562180ebf61835140dc4f81e70db3d646b89/docs/archive/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_ES.md)); **`PENDING_WORK_INVENTORY.md` frozen 2026-07-21** after its unique content was migrated (build-tool holds → `docs/security/SECURITY_CANON.md §Audited audit-ignores`; `FN-*` findings → Master Plan rows (via the detected-smells backlog, folded 2026-10-29)); it was in [`docs/archive/PENDING_WORK_INVENTORY.md`](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/cb4921bda93a39e300f1bd5203985edde8055258/docs/archive/PENDING_WORK_INVENTORY.md) until it was deleted from the repository on 2026-10-17, and git history keeps it. It rescues as living reference: `FEATURE_TRACE_MATRIX.md` (catalog + 2026 canon §9 + order §8.4, **KEPT**) + the discipline of `PLAN_MAESTRO §5.8/§5.9` (→ §0.3, retired). Its findings began in the 2026-06-29 assessment; on 2026-10-28 every open finding of that assessment's §2 and §3 became a row of this plan (most in §1 and §5.1; the commits of `workstream/cluster-b-mfa` under N-SEC-7), and the assessment left the tree ([its last version](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/b87b76ac80fbb971ac4c5733e94327d79c02d972/docs/audits/FULL_REPO_ASSESSMENT_2026-06-29.md)): this plan is the only record of open findings.
>
> ⚠️ **CAVEAT ABOUT THE ASSESSMENT (verified 2026-08-16, read it before citing it).** It was verified against `workstream/impl-revalidation @ 07c1faa5`, **not against `main`**. Its **§3** (new findings, adversarially verified) and **§4** (docs) still hold against `main @ 57374636` — 10 of 11 rechecked are still exact. But its **§2.1 ("13 FIXED — verified against HEAD… confirmed line by line") is NOT valid for `main`**: at least **4** of those fixes live only in `workstream/cluster-b-mfa` (`0c15280c`, `f5470dd4`, `bd968112`, `acb25880`) and their code is **absent from the tree**; of the 13 entries only 3 cite a commit and **none** cites file:line. One of the 4, `CACHE-XTENANT-HTTP`, was a cross-tenant leak, closed on 2026-10-17 by DEF-104 (#634, with its test in #635). **Re-audit the 13 against `main` before relying on any of them** → **N-SEC-7**. This is what motivated rule §0.2 **4.d**.
>
> **Status:** living document. **Owner:** Product Owner.
> **Code base:** `main @ 57374636` (2026-08-15, merge of PR #184). Last re-baseline of the plan: **2026-08-16**.
> **Hygiene resolved on 2026-10-06:** `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_ES.md`, which still carried live checkboxes (`F0-CLI-1`, `F0-WRK-2`) although §7 declared it retired on 2026-06-29, was archived by the Product Owner's decision (#433) in `docs/archive/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_ES.md`, with a header that declared it frozen, and deleted from the tree on 2026-10-17 ([its last version](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/2ec1562180ebf61835140dc4f81e70db3d646b89/docs/archive/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_ES.md)); this plan is the only authoritative roadmap (§7, §5.15).
> **Golden rule:** no features are built on a broken runtime — **Phase N (Leveling)** closes before the product resumes.
> **Closed items, collapsed on 2026-10-28:** a closed item is one line with its pull request and merge commit. The full text of every closed item, of §P, of the §1 campaign log, of the [IMAGE] card in §5.2, of the §6 notes and of §7, as it stood before this pruning, lives at [this file at `c8c62cbf`](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/c8c62cbf06c1a38031a0c5a22b7339f120f50497/docs/product/MASTER_PLAN_ES.md).

## §0 — Methodology (rescued, applies to the whole plan)

### 0.1 Tracking

- Status per task: `[ ]` pending · `[~]` in progress · `[x]` done · `[!]` blocked.
- **Stable ID** per task. Do not renumber; new tasks use the next free number of their series.
- **Size:** `[S]` ≤1 day · `[M]` 2-3 days. There is no `[L]`: if something is bigger, it is split.
- **Dependencies:** `🔗 dep:<ID>` (do not start until that one is `[x]`). `⛔ blocks:<area>` = an item whose delay holds others back.
- **DoD** = objective, verifiable criterion. Without the DoD met, `[x]` is not marked.
- **App tags:** API `apps/api` · WRK `apps/workers` · CLI `apps/client` · ADM `apps/admin` · PKG `packages/*` · INFRA `infra/prisma` · CI `.github/workflows`.
- **Provenance + confidence** per item: `verified` (cross-checked vs HEAD) · `confirmed-adversarial` (adversarially verified in the 06-29 assessment) · `pending-review` (audit finding not reconfirmed) · `stale-verify` (source >2 weeks old, re-audit before acting).

### 0.2 Re-validation bar (before marking any `[x]`)

Every task with code closes only if it passes all four:

1. **0-defect** — lint (`--max-warnings 0`) · tsc · **the fitness functions in force** (today **#1-#46**; the count "24 (→25)" went stale — verify against `CLAUDE.md §Automated Compliance Checks` + `.github/workflows/fitness.yml`, which are the source, instead of against a number written here) · tests, all green.
2. **Security** — the open findings this plan records for the item's area (§1 and §5) are addressed or explicitly deferred with a justification; since 2026-10-28 they include every open finding of the 2026-06-29 assessment's §2 and §3.
3. **Dependency-freshness (ADR-0018)** — every shared dep = its catalog pin (latest stable, exact); private ones freshened; `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` + `syncpack lint` green.
4. **Honest evidence** (new 2026-08-16, after reopening two items that were `[x]`) — the three rules below.

**4.a — A green fitness proves nothing if its regex never matches.** Verified: **#23** never fired — its regex sees only the 4 untyped parenthesized calls, all in excepted files, while raw-SQL sites exist (16 outside the exception list on 2026-08-16; 17 of 28 on 2026-10-28; N-AUTH-3 has the cause) (third instance of the class that N-CI-1 already names for #2/#3/#4). When closing an item that relies on a fitness, confirm that it has a **measured baseline**, not an assumed one.

**4.b — A concurrency test only certifies the interleaving it exercises.** Learned when reopening N-SEC-5: its fix closed the simultaneous window, its test used `Promise.all`, the staggered window stayed open and the item was marked `[x]`. If the DoD says "atomic", the test has to be the **staggered** one, not the simultaneous one.

**4.d — A verdict verified against a branch that never shipped is indistinguishable from a true one, and it silences the item.** The 2026-06-29 assessment ([§2.1 at its last version](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/b87b76ac80fbb971ac4c5733e94327d79c02d972/docs/audits/FULL_REPO_ASSESSMENT_2026-06-29.md#L43)) declares 13 findings "FIXED — verified against HEAD… confirmed line by line", but its HEAD was `workstream/impl-revalidation @ 07c1faa5`, not `main`: at least **4 of those fixes live only in `cluster-b-mfa`** and their code is absent from the tree (see N-SEC-7). One of them —`IDOR-ACCOUNTS`— was the CRITICAL that opened N-AUTH-1 (closed 2026-10-18), and it was silenced for seven weeks by appearing as closed. **Rule:** every "closed" verdict names **the ref it was verified against**; if it is not `main` (or the branch that is going to merge), it closes nothing — it only records that a fix exists somewhere. It is the same shape as 4.a, one level higher: green without having run against the tree that ships.

**4.c — Every documented guarantee needs executable backing** (the Maintainer's decision of 2026-08-16 — ADR pending, see §5.2). No normative statement in the canon or in a port's JSDoc is written in the present indicative without naming the test or the fitness that holds it up; without backing, it is written as an **intention**. Reason: six false statements in a single audit — `ARCHITECTURE_CANON §UoW:149` ("repositories **auto-detect** the active transaction" → 10 of 84), `MULTI_TENANT_GUARDS.md` §`AuditLog` design note, point 2 (calls "admin reader" the service that leaks to any customer), #23 above, the guard that does not cover nested `include`s without the doc mentioning it, `MfaUserRepositoryPort` (promises an atomic consumption that the adapter does not deliver) , `CustomerUserRepository:66-68` ("the existing hash is **preserved**" → it overwrites it) and —**seventh, created by this very commit**— `FEATURE_TRACE_MATRIX.md`, row `2FA / account security`, which states "**backup-codes CAS single-use**" ✅ while N-SEC-5 stays reopened precisely because of that. **People build on top, trusting it:** the CAS of the backup codes was written trusting the port's comment.

### 0.3 Audit/change discipline (from PLAN_MAESTRO §5.8/§5.9 — MANDATORY)

- **Direct reading = source of truth.** Greps locate or do a sanity cross-check; they are never the final truth for classifying code, deciding whether a middleware runs, or claiming "zero X" without having read the files.
- **No DELETE without the Maintainer's explicit validation.** The absence of consumers does NOT prove dead code. Mandatory taxonomy before any destructive action:
  - **PLANNED** — built for an intentional future feature.
  - **INFRASTRUCTURE_READY** — infra waiting for integration/wire-up.
  - **LEGACY** — it had consumers, they were removed, it remains for gradual migration.
  - **DEAD_CODE** — genuinely with no use and no plan, **confirmed by the Maintainer**.
  - Exception (markable without validation, still no auto-delete): `.bak/.old` backups, one-off debug scripts, superseded configs, commented-out blocks with git history.

### 0.4 2026 canon per feature

Each product task (§2-§4) references its **2026 implementation canon** (canonical approach + best practice + anti-pattern), rescued from `FEATURE_TRACE_MATRIX.md §9`. Re-verify the canon before starting each track (models/APIs change fast). The canon does NOT contradict `CLAUDE.md`: the new adapters reuse inbox/outbox + UoW + scheduler, they do not create parallel paths.

## §1 — PHASE N · LEVELING (cross-cutting blocker · P0)

> **It closes before the product resumes.** These items corrupt data, leak cross-tenant, break the publishing runtime (on which ALL the phases depend), or leave CI without a regression net. Source: 06-29 assessment (adversarially confirmed) + §2 open items of the inventory. Internal order: P0 security → core correctness → regression net → P1 docs.

**the Maintainer ratified option B**: the WRITERS first and the fail-closed reconciliation (`1c-1b`, applied and parked at `08391306`) at the end, after the last writer — the lesson that forced it is written down: a chain that turns a fail-open read into a fail-closed one must land the writer BEFORE the rejection, or every intermediate tip breaks the deployable, and `integration:saga-recovery` runs on EVERY tip since then. **5 of 13 closed** (orders 1 to 5): `1c-1a` the fail-closed load of the aggregate with the four dead loaders deleted under authorization; the full save REJECTS loudly what it is not going to write, because before it discarded it silently); `1c-1e` the CQRS command and its handler; `1c-2b` the admission of `/start` read from the record with typed 409s. **7 grandchildren remain**, with the **order-10 cycle** (`1c-2a` ⊗ `1c-3e`, mutually fail-closed) waiting for a decision on its shape. **The design was corrected twice by measurement, not by preference**: rev 3.6 (the full save does not write records, contrary to what it said) and **rev 3.7 (D9's admission contradicts itself: admitting a post WITHOUT a record breaks R7 for every post published before order 10 — a duplicate send; built narrower and convergent, with the original sentence left standing and corrected beside it)**. From that came a class that the order audit did not cover: it only asked whether a reader REJECTS on an absent record, never whether it ADMITS or WRITES — the rows that write owe that question before order 10. **Owned residuals born in 1c, SMELL-145 to SMELL-152,** are rows of the detected-smells backlog on the unmerged branches of this chain, never on `main`: they become rows of this plan when each branch lands, and the ids those branches reuse take new ids then (the §7 closure ledger's rule, decision F1 of §5.17). **Budget, measured**: the forecast for the WHOLE of PR 1c was CODE 3363 / EVIDENCE 4593 and it was already consumed with 5 of 13 units — the estimator undercounts ~1.9× in CODE and worse in evidence (one unit had a forecast of ZERO evidence for a wired handler). **Open decisions for the Maintainer**: `size:exception` of 1b2/1c-1a/1c-1b/1c-1c/1c-1d/1c-2b; the shape of the order-10 cycle; the payload of the 409s (the global handler sends `details` only in development, so `sagaId` and `fragments` do not reach the client) before 2b builds the panel; D9's equality rule against Slice 2's per-channel retry; and the pair "the bulk THROWS / the recurrence SKIPS" on a row without an account, which should be a single decision with its counters in T1c.18. The prerequisite `post-persistence-adapter-relocation` is **CLOSED** (card above), so N-COR-8's named dependency no longer blocks. **Owned residuals born in these two PRs, SMELL-132 to SMELL-137,** are rows of §5.1: PROVIDER-TEMPLATE-UNCOMPILED (SMELL-132), prov-classifier-403-drift-03 (SMELL-133), PROVIDER-ADAPTER-DUPLICATE (SMELL-134), TELEGRAM-TODO-FILE (SMELL-135) and SQUAWK-BIGINT-ADJUDICATION (SMELL-136); SMELL-137 is closed (§7 ledger). **Numbered successors N-COR-11..14** are the rows below, previously without a number.

### N.A — Security P0 (cross-tenant / DoS)

- [x] **N-SEC-1** + **N-SEC-1b** — the circuit-breaker cache leaked data across accounts, and one tenant's failures opened the breaker for all; closed 2026-07-19 by #124 (merge `bb38f5e7`).
- [~] **N-SEC-2 🔄 REOPENED 2026-08-16** `[M]` **CLI+ADM** — The Next proxies erase the real IP → the rate limit collapses. **The closure was PARTIAL:** its DoD covered the Next proxies and `resolveClientIp`, but `apps/api/src/admin/auth/adminAuthRoutes.ts:70` still puts the **raw** `request.ip` into the brute-force counter and into the per-IP lockout — on the most privileged surface of the system — and fitness **#29** does not catch it because it forbids _header_ reads, not the use of `request.ip`. The customer side does use the canonical resolver. **DoD of the remainder:** `adminAuthRoutes` derives the IP through `resolveClientIp`; a fitness that catches `request.ip` in security decisions (not only the headers). Evidence: `docs/architecture/ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` row A20. What was already closed is kept below. `confirmed-adversarial` (CRITICAL). **CLOSED** (`95b4ec66`; merged to `main` via **PR #125**, 2026-07-19): the helper `forwardedForHeaders` **relay-not-append** in the 4 egress points; `resolveClientIp` counting-from-the-right unchanged (OWASP canon verified — the spoofable leftmost is ignored); `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOP_COUNT` was later removed by ADR-0021, which replaced hop counting with `TRUSTED_PROXY_MODE` and `TRUSTED_PROXY_RANGES`. W-C2-1 (the admin routes `/admin/auth/{login,refresh}` under the AUTH preset, 5 per 15 minutes) did not land: `STANDARD_ROUTE_RULES` names no `/admin` route (`apps/api/src/security/httpRateLimitPreHandler.ts:89-95`), `/admin/auth/login` keeps its own limit of 15 per minute (`rateLimit(loginRateMax, 60000)`) and `/admin/auth/refresh` has no limit of its own, so it falls to the default of 100 per minute (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/adminAuthRoutes.ts:561-576`); both go to AUTH-RL-1 stage 1 as the `95b4ec66` residue of N-SEC-7. 31 tests. Prod precondition: ≥1 trusted proxy in front of Next. The proxies `[...path]/route.ts:69` (client and admin) + the auth server actions do not propagate `X-Forwarded-For`/`X-Real-IP` → the backend sees the Next server's IP for everyone; 5 anonymous requests block the login of the whole portal. **DoD:** append the real IP of the incoming request to `X-Forwarded-For` + the portal's address in `TRUSTED_PROXY_RANGES` (ADR-0021); replicated in admin; a test that verifies distinct per-IP buckets.
- [ ] **N-SEC-3** `[M]` **API+INFRA** — IDOR-TRACKEDLINK + a guard for `projectId`-only models. `confirmed-adversarial` (HIGH) + root cause `ARCH-PROJECT-SCOPED-GUARD-GAP`. `apps/api/src/links/linkRoutes.ts` get/stats/**delete** by `:id` without an accountId gate; `TrackedLink` has no `accountId` column → outside the guard. **DoD:** an ownership gate on the link routes; and a mechanism (a secondary guard or a denormalization of `accountId`) for the ~9 `projectId`-only models (ProjectMember, Post, **Channel** [encrypted credentials], TrackedLink, Campaign, ScheduledReport, ExternalNotificationConfig, GeneratedImage, RecurringPost) — prioritize Channel and ExternalNotificationConfig because they hold decryptable secrets. Verify that the compensating join pattern documented in `MULTI_TENANT_GUARDS.md §Transitively-scoped` is present in each adapter (the S2.1d audit did not evidence it). **🔄 IN PROGRESS (2026-07-28)** — sequential rollout under way, **Approach A** (denormalize `accountId` + guard + RLS, by construction; OWASP API1:2023). Classification of the 9 models done (`docs/features/project-scoped-tenant-guard/rollout-plan.md` + engram obs 260): **7 of 9 with a LIVE cross-tenant IDOR**. **Closed so far:** (a) the TrackedLink read/delete IDOR → PRs #109/#110 (adapter join + CQRS purge); (b) hotfix `DELETE /posts/:id` (Slice 0) → #112; (c) **reference impl** ExternalNotificationConfig to guard+RLS (Slice 1, one of the 2 prioritized credential-bearing ones) → #113; (d) ScheduledReport+Campaign to guard+RLS (Slice 2) → #114 — it includes the closure of the **untag join-table IDOR** (`campaignPost` without `accountId`: enrollment does not cover join-table mutations; an app-level fix with a guarded `findById`) and, collaterally, the CI fix for stacked branches (`workstream/**` triggers) + the fix of the Integration Tests boot, which was RED on every push to `main` since 2026-06-24. All with full SDD (8 phases) + adversarial gate + verify PASS + green CI; (e) RecurringPost+TrackedLink to guard+RLS (Slice 3, structural fold) → #116; (f) GeneratedImage to guard+RLS (Slice 4) → #118; (g) ProjectMember to guard+RLS (Slice 5, a forgotten feature enrolled defensively) → #119; (h) **Channel** to guard+RLS + tenant safety of the worker paths (Slice 7, the credential-bearing model of maximum blast: 4 AES-GCM columns decrypted by the credential resolver) — **PR1 #152** (`accountId` column + backfill + guard flip 57→58 + ownership in the two create paths + RLS pair through the guard↔RLS parity suite) + **PR2 #164** (explicit scoping by `accountId` + `setTenantGuc` in the 4 worker sites, `accountId` in the publish job payload with a fail-closed producer, two-tenant publish regression), **VERIFIED** (PASS 0C/5W/3S) **+ ARCHIVED** — it also closes the **worker-side credentials IDOR** (the paths that read `Channel` on the RAW client without tenant scope: `CredentialResolver`, `ChannelAuthFailureRecorder`, `mentionIngestWorker`, plus the owner lookup of the deploy fallback). **Structural enrollment: 8/9 models** (ExternalNotificationConfig, ScheduledReport, Campaign, RecurringPost, TrackedLink, GeneratedImage, ProjectMember, Channel) — only **Post** is missing. **Pre-auth context axis (propagation of the `TenantContext` at the edges, ADR-0020):** (i) `tenant-context-preauth-seams` (Slice 6.1, Class A — SSO SAML/OIDC, integration-auth Zapier/Make, billing/inbound webhooks, health, OAuth flow, index bootstrap) **VERIFIED** (PASS 0C/2W/3S) **+ ARCHIVED** → #120; the integration-auth seam landed as a **two-hook split** (`integrationAuthResolve` onRequest + `integrationAuthBind` preHandler), superior to the mutable holder the original plan described (reconciled in the archive). `tenant-context-coverage-harness` (Slice 6.0, coverage ratchet) has proposal+design (tasks phase pending). **Remaining:** slice **Post** (Slice 8 — the last model of the enrollment; structural closure of the app-level gate of `post-tenant-isolation`) · `tenant-context-coverage-harness` tasks+apply · audit of callers outside a context · `api-guarded-client-injection`: its swap landed (no container module imports the raw Prisma singleton, and `apps/api/src/index.ts` passes the guarded client to `createPrismaRepoAdapter`), but the gate it proposed against a raw client injected in `apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/` was never wired: fitness #21 excludes that directory, and today's #28 is the trust-proxy check ([design](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/e33fadb763be6e23dc79be89a7335869234a5afe/openspec/changes/api-guarded-client-injection/design.md)). **Execution findings (not seen in the assessment):** (1) the create path of each model accepts `projectId` from the client without an ownership check (engram obs 273) → a mandatory step of the recipe; (2) enrollment does NOT close routes that mutate or traverse join/child tables, nor those that skip the guarded `findById` — it applies to WRITEs and READs (engram obs 285) → each slice audits the full route surface and its two-tenant suite covers ALL of the model's routes. Backlog SMELL-54/55/56. **⬆️ STRUCTURAL SUCCESSOR IN PROGRESS (2026-09-07):** the closure of the Post slice and totality-by-construction now live in the full SDD `tenant-isolation-composite-fk` (decision A′: denormalized `accountId` + a composite FK over `UNIQUE(id, accountId)`), whose **phase 0 made RLS enforceable for the first time** — the founding finding was that layer 2 had been INERT since its migration (postgres SUPERUSER+BYPASSRLS+owner) — via PRs #219-#227 on `main` (a role without bypass, the pg-catalog gate, the URL split, the GUC bound per operation 177/177, fitness #40, the flip of ci.yml). **Slice 1 CLOSED 2026-09-09 (PRs #230/#232/#233): `Post` enrolled STRUCTURALLY — 9/9 models of this census**, with the trio `Post`/`PostContent`/`PostMedia` under `accountId NOT NULL` + composite FK + RLS and the guard on 61 models. What remains is the triage of the 65 remaining models (Slice 2, Post pattern decided) and the change's cross-cutting items.
- [ ] **N-SEC-3.b** `[M]` **API+INFRA** — **N-SEC-3's census covers one scoping shape out of four; its DoD must go from an enumeration to an obligation of TOTALITY.** `verified` 2026-08-16. The guard is an **allowlist that silently lets through everything that is absent** (`infra/prisma/src/extensions/tenantGuard.ts:91`, passthrough at `:195`). Outside the guard, besides the 9 `projectId`-only models that N-SEC-3 enumerates: **`approvalRequest`, `approvalReview`** (approval aggregate), **`postComment`** (scoped only by `postId`), **`notification`** (scoped by `recipientId`, `schema.prisma:411`), **`account`** (it is the tenant itself — its key is `id`, so **no guard can ever cover it**: there, authorization is the only possible control) and **`auditLog`** (excluded on purpose). Sequencing consequence: **§3 Phase 2 has 21 tasks waiting for N-SEC-3 to close, and that gate is going to read green with the class open.** **DoD:** every model of `Prisma.ModelName` declares its scope strategy in a **total** registry, and absence is a **compilation error**, not a passthrough; the `global` ones carry `reason` + the owning change in the entry itself; it turns the 3-step prose checklist of `SECURITY_CANON §Multi-Tenant Isolation` into something `tsc` verifies. Mechanism designed in `docs/features/principal-derived-authority/proposal.md §3.2`. 🔗 dep: N-AUTH-3.

The 2026-03 declaration is deleted with card C3 (2026-10-05, unit DC-3 of §5.13) without ever having been enforced; the intention stays here. **DoD:** a single source of the policy (per role, or `require2FA` if it survives N-SEC-6's decision on `SecuritySettings`); a SUPER_ADMIN without MFA gets no session until enrolling, with an enrollment path for the existing ones; a test, with the composed app, of the rejection and of the enrollment; the 2FA row of `FEATURE_TRACE_MATRIX.md` (`2FA / account security`) cites the real enforcement. 🔗 dep: N-SEC-6 (decides `SecuritySettings`).

- [ ] **N-SEC-6** `[M]` **[SECURITY, CRITICAL]** **API+PKG** — **Integrity of the credentials write path (new block 2026-08-16).** The plan had no owner for password reset/change nor for the write primitive underneath: its only credentials item was N-SEC-5 (MFA). Four confirmed defects ([`customer-credential-write-integrity/explore.md`](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/d3479d08493b2730765b48e65d66ae1021ba4d01/openspec/changes/customer-credential-write-integrity/explore.md)): **(D0)** the customer recovery flow is **dead** — the pre-identity routes (`customerAuthRoutes.ts:562`/`:577`, plus register and refresh) do not bind `withSystemContext` as login (`:164`) and MFA login (`:237`) do, so the guard throws and the error is laundered into a **400 "Invalid or expired reset token" with a valid token**; **(D1)** behind D0, the reset writes the new hash (`ResetPasswordUseCase.ts:67`) and **reverts** it with `save(user)` (`:74` → `PrismaCustomerUserRepository.ts:172,176,204`) returning "Password reset successfully" — the `Result` of that write is not even checked, while the one on the previous line is; **(D2)** the reset token is not **claimed**: it is TOCTOU, not sequential reuse, and the **admin** side is the only one reachable today; **(D3)** one token for N accounts against a `@unique` column, with the `Result` discarded → it resets an arbitrary account. Underneath all of it: `save()` writes **19 columns from a snapshot** —with 2 broken callers and 4 at risk of a lost update— and the repository **does not resolve the transactional client** (10 of the 84 files that write do). **DoD:** the hash reaches the database **only** through commands with an explicit argument (`create(user,hash)` / `claimPasswordReset(token,hash)`), so that omitting it is a **compilation error** and not a silent revert; the claim is **a single statement** whose `where` names the token (it closes D1+D2+expiry-outside-the-predicate at once, and it needs no transaction); `save()` is **deleted** in favor of a surface of commands by intention; context bound in the 4 pre-identity routes — **but never before** the fix of the revert, or the endpoint's first live behavior is the silent revert; parity with the admin side on the same axis (see `ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` §1A); **and session revocation goes in here, it does not stay as background debt** — `core-refresh-no-rotation-02` (§5.1, HIGH `confirmed-adversarial`) is still live: `RefreshCustomerTokenUseCase.ts:74` mints a new `sessionId` without invalidating the consumed one or detecting reuse, so **a repaired password reset would still leave every previous refresh token alive for 7 days** (PARITY-A24, the 3rd most exploitable of the ledger). A credential change that does not revoke sessions is not a credential change. ⚠️ **It carries the invite's ordering constraint** — see the warning at the end of §1.N.E. **Dormant trigger to close here:** `LoginCustomerUseCase.ts:191-194`+`:226` has the same revert shape in the transparent rehash; today it does not fire because `needsRehash` returns false for every existing hash, and **it arms itself the instant someone raises `ARGON2_PARAMS`** — which `passwordHashing.ts:27-28` recommends as the safe way to upgrade.

- [x] **N-COR-2** — in-flight sagas died on a deploy or restart, the timeout checker re-failed a terminal saga every 60 seconds, and a still-pending publish burned its three retries; its three DoD clauses hold since #184: (a) and (b) by `saga-tenant-scope-and-recovery`, #173 (merge `55d659af`) and #180 (merge `bd1adbb4`), and (c) by `saga-engine-terminal-hygiene`, #184 (merge `57374636`, 2026-08-15); closed 2026-10-28 by the Maintainer's decision, with the cross-replica row claim moved to N-COR-15.
- [ ] **N-COR-15** `[M]` **API(saga-runtime)+INFRA** — **Cross-replica saga row claim** (`saga-engine-terminal-hygiene`, phases 17 to 24; split from N-COR-2 on 2026-10-28 by the Maintainer's decision). `verified` 2026-10-28. Saga recovery takes no claim on the rows it advances: the boot load and the retry scan read and then dispatch, with no lease, no claim column and no `SKIP LOCKED` (the `SagaInstance` model carries no claim field and `apps/api/src/saga/SagaClaimService.ts` does not exist), so two replicas, or a rolling deploy whose draining process still advances rows the new one inherits, dispatch the same row (SMELL-73). The retry scan reads a fixed page (`take: 50`, `apps/api/src/saga/SagaManagerLifecycle.ts:1414`), so a slow head can starve the rows behind it. With no command-id dedupe on the bus (SMELL-71), a duplicated pre-pivot create mints a second post with a different publish dedupe key, which the BullMQ job-id dedupe does not absorb. The 2026-06-29 assessment's `SAGA-NO-INFLIGHT-GUARD` (the in-flight guard is opt-in per step) and `saga-inflight-race-05` (the recovery tick re-dispatches before `nextRetryAt` is cleared; MEDIUM while the deploy is single-pod, because the pivot's `jobId` dedupe absorbs a second enqueue) wait on this row. Until it lands, recovery is sound only in a single process, the constraint `docs/security/MULTI_TENANT_GUARDS.md` documents in §"Recovery ownership is PER-PROCESS". **DoD:** phases 17 to 24 of [`tasks.md`](../features/saga-engine-terminal-hygiene/tasks.md): lease-based row claims taken at selection by both readers (`SagaClaimService` inside `withSagaSystemClaim`), the durable park window (`SagaParkedWindow`), the claim's configuration closed at its seam, the ADR of saga row ownership, and the contention, claim-predicate and parked-window suites green; it lands before the API runs more than one replica. 🔗 related: SMELL-71 (DEF-166).

The editor also resets its content to `initialContent` whenever the prop changes (`packages/ui/src/components/business/useContentEditor.ts:438-446`), and the edit page passes the fetched `post.body` (`apps/client/app/[locale]/dashboard/posts/[id]/page.tsx:407`), so a refetch of the post replaces what the user typed since it loaded (assessment `client-editor-content-reset-03`, re-measured 2026-10-28).

- [~] **N-COR-8 🔄 IN PROGRESS 2026-09-21** `[L]` **PKG(core)+API(saga)+WORKERS+INFRA** — Partial publication failure without a per-channel record (`post-publish-partial-failure`, Q5 successor of N-COR-1). `confirmed` (HIGH). With N channels, if any of them fails the saga ends FAILED post-pivot and the post stays DRAFT (editable and re-publishable) while the other providers have the content live; the set of target channels **is never persisted** (`SchedulePostUseCase` validates and discards; `PostAggregate` with no collection of channels; `PublishLog` is what was observed, not what was attempted). **Signed (Q1–Q14, 2026-09-16):** a per-channel record INSIDE the Post aggregate as the only truth (targets + result + reason + attempts + `externalId` + `contentHash`), the post's status DERIVED (PUBLISHED = every channel complete · PARTIALLY_PUBLISHED = ≥1 complete and ≥1 failed · FAILED = none complete), an IMMEDIATE content lock at the first live channel, ALL-OR-NOTHING threads per channel (partial fragments are retracted → channel FAILED), retry only publish-now of the missing channels, an URGENT alert to the customer (in-app + email now; SMS = a change of its own; push with a mobile API) when the retraction is impossible. **The durable provider receipt (2026-10-28, the Maintainer's decision):** the per-channel record, which carries the provider's `externalId`, is the durable provider receipt, and the publish handler short-circuits on a channel whose record holds it, so the receipt of N-SEC-7's `eb295f40` folds in here and no separate `PublishLog.providerPostId` is built; the record narrows the window between the provider's acknowledgement and its own write without closing it, so this row also owns the provider-native idempotency that publishing exactly once needs (the 2026-06-29 assessment's Slice 7, Bluesky included). **Status (2026-09-17): IN PROGRESS** under `feature-branch-chain` — planning complete (explore/research/propose/spec, design at rev 3 after two fresh gates, tasks) and recorded in the tracker `feature/post-publish-partial-failure` (`4443ba7c`). **PR 1b3 (thread contract) DONE** in `workstream/ncor8-1b3`: `74c206dd` (`publishThread` reports the fragments that went out on every error path) + `a8eff3c5` (the worker keeps the failure report when it cannot write the live-fragment row), with the gate passed. **PR 1b work unit A DONE** in `workstream/ncor8-1b`: `3fdbd6dc` (table `PostChannelPublication`, migration `20260917093257`, enrolled in the tenant guard and without a writer yet); 7 grandchildren remain and the order-10 cycle waits for a decision on its shape. ### N.E — Principal authorization (P0, blocking)

> **New block (2026-08-16).** The plan lacked the **authorization** axis as a category —its security model was _isolation_ (which rows a caller can see → N-SEC-3) + _credentials_ (N-SEC-5), without the concept of an **actor** ("who acts, may they?") nor that of **declared authority** ("what does this route require")— but **that is not the main reason why these defects are still alive, and the real reason is worse.**
>
> **⚠️ THE PLAN BELIEVED THIS AXIS CLOSED, BECAUSE ITS EVIDENCE COMPANION VERIFIED IT ON A BRANCH THAT NEVER SHIPPED.** [The 2026-06-29 assessment's §2.1](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/b87b76ac80fbb971ac4c5733e94327d79c02d972/docs/audits/FULL_REPO_ASSESSMENT_2026-06-29.md#L43) lists **`IDOR-ACCOUNTS` (+ quota-tamper closed)** among the "13 FIXED — verified against HEAD", under the sentence _"Confirmed line by line that they are closed"_. And it is true — but its HEAD was `workstream/impl-revalidation @ 07c1faa5` (declared at [its line 4](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/b87b76ac80fbb971ac4c5733e94327d79c02d972/docs/audits/FULL_REPO_ASSESSMENT_2026-06-29.md#L4)), **not `main`**. The fix exists: commit `0c15280c` _"fix(security): close cross-tenant IDOR read+write across accounts/comments/notifications/recurring (CWE-639)"_ (2026-06-24), with `assertOwnAccount`, the missing `where` and six isolation suites — and it **is not an ancestor of `main`** (verified: `git merge-base --is-ancestor 0c15280c main` → no; only `workstream/cluster-b-mfa`, which is **53 commits ahead**). None of that is in the tree: `rg -c assertOwnAccount apps/api/src/accounts/accountRoutes.ts` = **0**.
>
> A consequence for the method, more valuable than the bug: **a green verdict verified against a branch that did not ship is indistinguishable from a true verdict, and it silences the item in the plan** — it is rule §0.2 **4.d** one level higher, and it is the one that would have caught this in July instead of August.
>
> **Closure (2026-10-18):** N-AUTH-1 closed `IDOR-ACCOUNTS` in `workstream/fix-n-auth-1-account-authority`, reimplemented on the current tree without extracting `0c15280c`; N-AUTH-2, N-AUTH-3 and N-SEC-7 are still open.
>
> Common root cause of the defects below: **authority is derived from client-controlled input** (query, body, params) instead of from the authenticated principal. Adversarially verified evidence (31 claims refuted): `docs/features/principal-derived-authority/explore.md`.

- [x] **N-AUTH-1** — the account IDOR (`IDOR-ACCOUNTS`); done 2026-10-18 by #657 (merge `ad810deb`), with DEF-106; findings DEF-113 to DEF-116 (§5.11).
- [ ] **N-AUTH-2** `[M]` **[SECURITY, CRITICAL]** **API** — `GET /activity-feed` returns the complete `AuditLog` to any authenticated customer. `confirmed-adversarial`. `activityFeedRoutes.ts:79` registers after `requireClientAuth` and the file **never** references `request.customerUser`; the base predicate is `{ success: true }` (`activityFeedService.ts:148-149`) and `auditLog` is outside the guard on purpose. **The exploit is `GET /activity-feed` with no parameters.** It leaks UUIDs of other tenants' accounts/projects/posts, the taxonomy of actions, and —via `include:{user}}`— the id, name and email of **AdminUser**. ⚠️ **Two documented traps:** (a) the query param `accountId` **is not** a tenant filter — it maps to `where.resourceId`+`resource:"Account"` (`:165-168`), so passing it **shrinks** the result; (b) for the same reason, putting the principal's `accountId` in that slot is a **NO-FIX** that ships an almost empty feed with the leak intact. **DoD:** it is **rebuilt** as `GET /account/activity` scoped by `where.accountId`, gated by the `audit:read` permission — ⚠️ **that gate is NOT buildable today**: the customer-side RBAC permissions are loaded everywhere and **enforced nowhere** (`ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` PARITY-D11, the 5th most exploitable item of the ledger; `FEATURE_TRACE_MATRIX.md`, row `RBAC / roles and permissions`, itself marks RBAC ✅ while its row `Per-project members with granular roles` admits "Gap: enforcement + write path + per-project role are missing"), so N-AUTH-2 is a 🔗 **dep of the `requireCustomerPermission` middleware** or its DoD falls —, with hydrated customer actors and admin/system actions as a non-identifying label, without raw URLs; it includes the **write path** (`AuditableService.logUserAction` does not write `accountId` — `UserActionOptions:93-101` has no such field — so the ADMIN/SYSTEM actor rows stay NULL and a read-only fix would give a correct and empty feed); and it **corrects `docs/security/MULTI_TENANT_GUARDS.md` §`AuditLog` design note, point 2**, which today documents the defect as a feature (it lists this service among "5 admin readers" when its only route is a customer one) — leaving that paragraph standing re-breaks it at the next reading of the canon. **It is recorded that the rebuilt endpoint has NO frontend consumer** (`rg` over both portals = 0) → it enters §5.3.b as wiring work.
- [ ] **N-AUTH-3** `[M]` **API+PKG** — The **mechanism**: that deriving authority from the request body be impossible, not a convention. `verified`. Without it the class grows again: the two CRITICALs above are instances, and there are 8 more defects of the same family (twin change). Design chosen by a panel of judges (`docs/features/principal-derived-authority/proposal.md §3`): a **total** scope registry per model (→ N-SEC-3.b) + a **brand** that only the authentication layer can mint, with the seam at `enterTenantContext(AccountScope)` + a per-route authority declaration that **fails at boot** if it is missing. **DoD:** the violation is caught at compilation, at boot or in CI —never in review—; the mechanism declares **what it cannot catch** and who covers that; fitness **#30** (actor identity from the request, hard-zero, baseline 6) + **#31b** (client-provided tenant scope, ratchet, baseline 12) mirrored in `fitness.yml`; and **repair fitness #23** (**= `FITNESS-23-EVADED`, §2H — when it closes here, it leaves that list**), which **never fired**: the documented pipeline returns 0 while raw-SQL sites exist (16 outside the exception list on 2026-08-16; 17 of 28 on 2026-10-28). **Exact cause, needed so as not to reproduce the bug when "repairing" it (re-measured 2026-10-28):** the regex is `\.\$(queryRaw|…)\(`, and in ERE `\$` is a literal dollar, so it matches only an untyped parenthesized call. Of the 28 call sites in its scope (tests and comments excluded), the 4 it sees all sit in excepted files, which is why it returns 0; it cannot see the 7 tagged templates (`$queryRaw` followed by a template literal, SMELL-111) nor the 17 calls with a type argument (`$queryRaw<T>`), parenthesized or tagged. A repair that only adds the tagged form still misses the typed calls. The same string is in `.github/workflows/fitness.yml:570`. In addition, one of the 17 (`PrismaSemanticRetrievalAdapter.ts:42,63`, raw pgvector, typed) is **not** in the exceptions list of `CLAUDE.md`, so the assessment's "no leak today" has to be **re-checked, not inherited**. Third instance of the class that N-CI-1 names for #2/#3/#4 — and the complete family has six: SMELL-74, SMELL-75, SMELL-79, SMELL-80. When the repair of #23 re-measures the tagged-template population, it also rewrites the dated blockquote of 2026-07-27 in `docs/security/MULTI_TENANT_GUARDS.md` §Worker tenant scoping, which still counts "Seven live statements in `apps/api/src`" although the `set_config` of `PrismaUnitOfWork` now lives in `packages/adapters/db-prisma/src/unitofwork/PrismaUnitOfWork.ts:106`; the rewrite keeps the dated form with the new count (backlog SMELL-130).
- [ ] **N-SEC-7** `[M]` **[SECURITY, CRITICAL]** **API+WRK** — **Extract (or redo) the orphaned security cluster of `workstream/cluster-b-mfa`.** `verified` 2026-08-16, re-measured 2026-10-28. The 07-19..21 extraction campaign declared that only "§2F write-path v1 (DO-NOT-SHIP) + A2 audit-actor read-path" remained of that branch ([`docs/reports/merge-campaign-2026-07-21.md:78,113`](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/6cabc10074ef44345fc420f871eb94343c281abd/docs/reports/merge-campaign-2026-07-21.md#L78), the version that was measured; the report was deleted from the repository on 2026-10-17 and git history keeps it); **53 commits remained**, none of them an ancestor of `main`. The commit-by-commit audit of 2026-08-16 ([the disposition report at its last version](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/b87b76ac80fbb971ac4c5733e94327d79c02d972/docs/reports/N_SEC_7_CLUSTER_B_MFA_DISPOSITION.md#L211), its 53-row table) classified 25 landed, 4 superseded, 11 live and unextracted and 13 documentation-only. As measured on 2026-10-28, of the 11: `f5470dd4` is closed, `46a6282c` is superseded, `0c15280c` and `c64a718f` are closed in part, and seven are live: `70b75124`, `a71bd837`, `bd968112`, `eb295f40`, `35d44f4f`, the `95b4ec66` residue and `6bb37e10`. Each is below with its state; every sha stays readable on `origin/workstream/cluster-b-mfa` until the closing condition holds:
  - **How a live commit is treated (the Maintainer's criterion, 2026-08-16; it governs every sub-bullet below).** The disposition report spoke of re-applying the 11 live commits and, for some, of completing what the commit left out; that is patching over a fix that was conceived wrong, against the project's standing rule that what was conceived wrong is redone, not patched. The 11 fall into two classes, and the class decides the treatment. (A) A commit that fixes a bounded defect inside a sound mechanism is applied as it is: the query-string key of `c64a718f` (a), the CodeQL fixes of `6bb37e10` and the documentation strands. (B) A commit that fixes a structural problem route by route is not re-applied: the whole surface is redone under the mechanism (N-AUTH-1, N-AUTH-3), and the commit is kept only as evidence of what is exposed, which cases it covered and which tests it wrote. The sign of (B) is a fix that covers some routes and not others: a structural authorization could not leave two out, so leaving them proves the case-by-case work N-AUTH-3 exists to remove. The witness case is `0c15280c`, which covered three of the five account routes and added `assertOwnAccount` to the customer side of account administration, a surface the Product Owner's decision of 2026-08-16 removes (account administration is Admin-only; the customer keeps only "my account": read and rename), so re-applying it would harden routes that must not exist. The same holds for its comments, notifications and post-creation parts, and for `f5470dd4`, whose class defect was a cache that ran in `onRequest`, before authentication, not a missing tenant segment in its key. Where a sub-bullet below says "by hand", it means redo that surface under the mechanism, with the commit as the inventory of what is exposed.
  - `0c15280c` **IDOR accounts/comments/notifications/post-create** (CWE-639) — accounts **closed** by N-AUTH-1 on 2026-10-18 (#657, merge `ad810deb`), redone without extracting the commit. Comments **live**: `authorId` and `editorId` come from the body (`apps/api/src/comments/commentRoutes.ts:30,36,97,183`). Notifications **live**: `recipientId` comes from the body (`apps/api/src/notifications/notificationRoutes.ts:46,218`). Both are class (B) and are redone under N-AUTH-3 (rows 6 and 9 of `docs/features/principal-derived-authority/explore.md`). Post creation in a foreign project is blocked by the composite key of `Post.project` over `[projectId, accountId]` (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma:706`; `tenant-isolation-composite-fk` slice 1, #230/#232/#233), with no test that proves it yet. The recurring part is superseded (abandon list below).
  - `f5470dd4` **`CACHE-XTENANT-HTTP`** — **closed** by DEF-104 (#634, merge `f55a21a2`, 2026-10-17; its 2xx-route test in #635, merge `01009b85`), redone under the mechanism rather than extracted: the cache read now runs as the last pre-handler of each cached route, after its authentication (`apps/api/src/middleware/autoCacheMiddleware.ts:251-287`). Do not re-apply.
  - `bd968112` **`WRK-NO-REAUTH`** — **live**: the publish path flags no channel for reauthentication (no reauthentication or authentication-failure reference in `apps/workers/src/publishHandler.ts` or `publishWorker.ts`). It is inert until the root cause of `70b75124` is fixed, and it waits for the SMELL-44 decision (§5.1): compose `UpdateChannelAuthStateUseCase` (`packages/core/channels/src/UpdateChannelAuthStateUseCase.ts`), the primitive the duplication review adjudicated canonical ([`code-duplications.md`](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/f0f38adc34958966dcdb7718f682235f6399c709/docs/reports/code-duplications.md#L12), deleted from the tree on 2026-10-17), instead of deepening the workers' recorder.
  - `acb25880` **`IDOR-POSTS`** — **superseded**: the scoping arrived through the tenant-guard rollout (`GetPostUseCase.ts:27,60`, `DeletePostUseCase.ts:22,74`), although its suite is still absent.
  - `c64a718f` **canonical limiter for the authentication endpoints** — (a) the query-string key is **closed** by #504 (merge `2e7d1619`, commit `40434165`): the key is the matched route pattern with its parameters (`apps/api/src/security/httpRateLimitPreHandler.ts:245-248`); (c) the seven inert `config.rateLimit` blocks are **closed** by #679 (merge `bdffd273`). (b) is **live**: `POST /auth/customer/register`, `/auth/customer/request-password-reset` and `/auth/customer/reset-password` match no rule of `STANDARD_ROUTE_RULES` (`httpRateLimitPreHandler.ts:89-95`) and fall to the default of 100 per minute; it goes to AUTH-RL-1 stage 1. `POST /auth/login` is capped by the AUTH preset and records attempts without ever checking them (`apps/api/src/auth/authServiceCore.ts:157,298`, which DC-2 deletes). The commit's `ADR-0019-rate-limiting-canonical-limiter.md` collides with the existing ADR-0019 (`ADR-0019-pnpm-11-migration.md`) and is not landed under that number.
  - `95b4ec66` **residue: the Admin authentication routes outside the AUTH preset** — **live**: `/admin/auth/login` keeps its own limit of 15 per minute and `/admin/auth/refresh` has no limit of its own, so it falls to the default of 100 per minute (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/adminAuthRoutes.ts:561-576`). Admin login also passes `BruteForceProtectionPort` (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/AdminAuthService.ts:66-76`), so this is a lost third layer, not the only one. It goes to AUTH-RL-1 stage 1 with (b) of `c64a718f`, and N-SEC-2's W-C2-1 does not count as closed.
  - `70b75124` + `a71bd837` **provider failure classification** — **live, the root cause**: `mapErrorToPublishError` reads only `.status` (`packages/providers/shared/src/helpers.ts:136`) while `AppError` and `ProviderError` carry `statusCode` (`packages/shared/src/errors.ts:55`, `packages/providers/shared/src/ProviderError.ts:39`), so their failures fall to `NETWORK` (`helpers.ts:149`) and AUTH never fires on the publish path; X maps any 401 or 403 message to AUTH (`packages/providers/x/src/XAdapter.ts:446,507,576,617`), and Telegram, Snapchat, Pinterest and Bluesky classify no 403. Order: `helpers.ts` first (`status ?? statusCode` and the nested `reason`), then the per-provider classifiers hunk by hunk, added to the existing suites. ⛔ Never check out a whole file from these two commits: the Facebook, TikTok, Telegram and Snapchat `apiClient.ts` predate N-SEC-1 and would drop `hashCallScope` and its cache discriminant, reverting the circuit-breaker fix, and `BlueskyClient.ts` would delete `isAcceptedImageFormat`, the magic-byte gate that the `image-size` row of SECURITY_CANON keeps as defense in depth. The provider detail of `07c1faa5` goes with it: Meta `creation_id` reuse, deterministic Snapchat names, Pinterest breaker-retry safety, a Facebook read-path 401 with no `error.code`, an X 429 with a numeric `.code` read as NETWORK, and X `publishThread` 4xx through `readHttpStatus`, without its "FIXED in #97" status claims (#97 never merged). This rescopes `prov-classifier-403-drift-03` (§5.1).
  - `35d44f4f` **customer actors on the audit read path (A2, which the 2026-07-21 campaign left pending)** — **live**: there is no `byActorType` and no `apps/api/src/audit/auditExportColumns.ts`; a CUSTOMER audit row is dropped from `topUsers` (`apps/api/src/audit/auditService.ts:287-289`, an unmatched user shown as "Unknown" at `:304`), exports the literal `undefined` in the CSV column `user.email` (`apps/api/src/audit/auditRoutes.ts:375`), and the Admin compliance view includes only `user` (`apps/api/src/admin/AnalyticsComplianceHandlers.ts:90-101`) and shows the actor as "Unknown" (`apps/admin/hooks/api/useCompliance/api.ts:107`). Cherry-pick it, then `3c43d8e8` (without it knip fails) and `84c0c7d9` (the three living specs and the archive report); three trivial conflicts were measured on 2026-08-16 (keep `normalizeAuditActorInput`, add `AUDIT_ACTOR_TYPE`, drop `deriveActorType`). The design source is `docs/features/audit-actor-polymorphism/`.
  - `eb295f40` **durable provider receipts (`WRK-DOUBLE-POST`)** — **live**, and its receipt is **delivered by N-COR-8** (the Maintainer's decision of 2026-10-28): N-COR-8's per-channel record, which carries `externalId`, is the durable provider receipt and the publish handler short-circuits on it, so no separate `PublishLog.providerPostId` is planned and the commit is not re-applied. Today no receipt exists (no `recordReceipt` and no `PublishLog.providerPostId`, `infra/prisma/schema.prisma:939-957`), so a crash between the provider's acknowledgement and the OK log publishes again on retry; the only short-circuit is a log row already OK (`apps/workers/src/publishHandler.ts:732-735`). A receipt only narrows that crash window, so the provider-native idempotency that publishing exactly once needs (the assessment's Slice 7, Bluesky included) stays owned, by N-COR-8. The assessment had narrowed this finding on a branch that never merged. The commit stays the inventory of the six pieces it touched: the column and its migration, the port method, the repository and cache write-through, the handler guard and the two cross-package tests (`apps/api/tests/publish.flow.integration.test.ts`, then named `publish.flow.test.ts`, and `packages/adapters/db-prisma/tests/subRepos.di.test.ts`).
  - `6bb37e10` **CodeQL** — **live, LOW**: `packages/adapters/storage-cloudinary/src/index.ts:153` (`js/polynomial-redos`; no manifest depends on the package) and `packages/adapters/fallback-strategies/tests/index.test.ts:38` (`js/incomplete-sanitization`, a test fake). Class (A): cherry-pick it whole; a clean three-way merge of its four files was measured on 2026-08-16.
  - `46a6282c` — **superseded** by N-TEST-1: its seven suites are `*.integration.test.ts` files that the services tier collects (fitness #30). The six documentation strands: `b97a4157` is moot (`docs/standards/` holds only `frontend-standards.md`); `84c0c7d9` rides with `35d44f4f`; `fc5a8b62` holds the acceptance criteria of `c64a718f`, `f5470dd4` and `0c15280c` in five specs, readable at that sha, for whichever change redoes those surfaces; `9479c6a4` (a) is moot and (b), a staleness banner on `FEATURE_TRACE_MATRIX.md`, belongs to §5.15; `07c1faa5` goes with `70b75124`; `6b9fa24d` is closed by the banner of `docs/security/SECURITY_TESTING_FRAMEWORK.md:261-264`.
  - **Abandon** (re-applying any of these regresses `main`): `2ce76927` (deletes the plan of its day) · `005b7252` (rolls `MfaService` back and undoes the later MFA slices and `d6429f72`) · `acb25880` (downgrades the delete gate from compile-forced to optional) · `53d371c4` (deletes the 1:1 mapping test) · `8997bcd6` (reverts six later hook commits) · `86a4ae3f` (a strict subset of `main`'s `.gitignore`) · `2b1ea5d0` (repoints at a document that is still live and different) · `3dedb7ce` (a false premise: the dependency still has a consumer) · the adapter half of `da8ef686` (`main`'s compare-and-swap is stronger than the branch's blind write) · the recurring part of `0c15280c` (`main` solved it structurally, with a wired proof suite) · the replacement text of `6b9fa24d` (it would write a new falsehood). Two items of the original list are moot, because their files left the tree on 2026-10-17: the `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_ES.md` deletion of `9479c6a4` and the `docs/api/cqrs.md` deletion of `76e92a9b`.
  - **Order**, by live exposure on `main`: the comments and notifications of `0c15280c` under N-AUTH-3; (b) of `c64a718f` with the `95b4ec66` residue in AUTH-RL-1 stage 1; `helpers.ts` of `70b75124`, then the per-provider classifiers with `a71bd837`; `bd968112` after SMELL-44; `eb295f40` through N-COR-8; `35d44f4f` → `3c43d8e8` → `84c0c7d9`; `6bb37e10`; the documentation strands. These sub-bullets are the per-sha ledger the work is tracked in: topic labels are what lost six commits the first time.
    **DoD:** the commit-by-commit audit of the 53 is done (2026-08-16, the disposition report linked above; verify any LANDED verdict with `git show <sha> | git patch-id --stable`, and test whether a path exists with `git cat-file -e`, never with `git rev-parse <ref>:<path>`, which echoes an absent path instead of failing); each live commit above lands under the criterion or is declined with a recorded reason, and so does each documentation strand; the isolation suites are recovered or rewritten; and the branch is deleted once both hold and the two false claims on `main` are corrected (N-SEC-2's W-C2-1 and the scope of `prov-classifier-403-drift-03`, both corrected on 2026-10-28). ⛔ **blocks**: accepting as good any of the 13 "fixed" verdicts of the 2026-06-29 assessment's §2.1 ([its last version](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/b87b76ac80fbb971ac4c5733e94327d79c02d972/docs/audits/FULL_REPO_ASSESSMENT_2026-06-29.md#L43)): `IDOR-POSTS`, `IDOR-ACCOUNTS`, `IDOR-ANALYTICS`, `IDOR-NOTIFICATIONS`, `IDOR-RECURRING`, `IDOR-COMMENTS`, `CACHE-XTENANT-HTTP`, `AUTH-REGISTER-PRIVESC`, `RATELIMIT-DEAD`, `WRK-NO-REAUTH`, `SSE-DOS-CAP`, `CI-GAP-BULK-SCHEDULE` and `CI-GAP-RLS`. Spot-checked on 2026-10-28: `IDOR-COMMENTS`, `IDOR-NOTIFICATIONS` and `WRK-NO-REAUTH` do not hold on `main` (sub-bullets `0c15280c` and `bd968112`).

> **⚠️ ORDERING CONSTRAINT — do not reorder without reading this.** `POST /team/invite` accepts `role:"OWNER"` with no hierarchy check (`teamRoutes.ts:21-27`; `InviteTeamMemberUseCase.ts:65-66` only validates that the role's name exists in the catalog; incidentally, `:22` also accepts `accountId` **in the body** — another instance of N-AUTH-3's class; cross-ref `SMELL-59`, which already covers that file for an unenforced `maxTeamMembers`). Today that is **capped only because the customer password flow is broken** (no acceptance route, `passwordHash:""`, and the reset returns 500 for lack of `withSystemContext`). **Repairing that 500 in isolation —an obviously correct bugfix that anyone would do first— turns the invitation into a complete OWNER takeover.** The invite's hierarchy check MUST land **before or together with** the repair of the reset (see N-SEC-6). No reviewer of the credentials PR would have the context to detect it.
>
> **And do not confuse it with its NOT-capped sibling, in the same file:** `PATCH /team/:id/role` takes `changerMemberId` **from the body** (`teamRoutes.ts:149`) → a VIEWER promotes themselves by naming a superior member. That **does not depend on the broken reset: it is exploitable today** (`ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` PARITY-C11, the 2nd most exploitable of the ledger). It goes in N-AUTH-3's twin change, not here — but its priority is **higher** than the invite's.

## §2 — PHASE 1 · PRODUCT — Necessary (deals are lost without them)

> From the feature roadmap. It starts after Leveling. Rule §8.5: do not jump to Phase 3 with Phase 1 open. Each task carries its 2026 canon.
>
> **🚦 PRODUCT-PASS GATE (new, 2026-08-16) — it precedes every task of this phase.** Leveling guarantees that the runtime is not broken; it does **not** guarantee that what was built is wired nor that it does what it says. Evidence that motivated the gate: a customer **cannot change their password** (the portal calls an endpoint that never existed), `/forgot-password` **does not exist** in any portal although the login links to it, the activity feed has **no** frontend consumer in either of the two portals, and **37 of 118** audited rows are **symmetric** — neither side has it, or both are equally wrong (`docs/architecture/ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md`) — and a sample of six of them **is not in this plan either**: they are not planned-but-unbuilt features, they are features the plan does not know are missing. **DoD of the gate:** (a) decide, feature by feature, what exists in the MVP — **the inventory is [`FEATURE_TRACE_MATRIX.md`](./FEATURE_TRACE_MATRIX.md) §1-6 (the 6 levels of need, ~70 features), §7 (capabilities built outside the competitive catalog) and §8.3 (feature → apps)**, which is the only product catalog of the repo and the one that this plan already declares "catalog, KEPT" in its header. ⚠️ It is a **May 2026 trace** — the assessment calls it "pre-Phase-0 state" and it already has verified false rows (`:65` points to a route that the migration to `[locale]` moved; `:134` claims "backup-codes CAS single-use", which N-SEC-5 has just reopened) — so each row is re-verified through (b) before it counts as decided, and the result **re-baselines the matrix**. `§5.3` + `§5.3.b` + `ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` enter as **wiring cross-coverage** (what of the ✅ has no surface), **not** as the inventory; (b) verify at **runtime**, not statically, that each capability declared closed responds end to end; (c) only then open Phase 1. Without this gate, endpoints whose destiny nobody decided would keep being hardened: it already happened with the activity feed, which was going to be scoped when the real question was whether it should exist.

- [ ] **F1-CLI-4** `[M]` **CLI** — CSV upload UI + per-row report. 🔗 dep:F1-API-3. ⚠️ **BLOCKED by the conceptual error of the bulk-schedule targeting** (per-provider targeting, not per-channel — engram `bulk_schedule_targeting_gap.md` + ADR-0016). Resolve the redesign first. Note: the stack was redesigned to `/parse` + `/confirm` (the `/imports` of the old DoD is 410 Gone). **Canon (§9.3):** validate the CSV upfront (Zod per row) → `addBulk`/FlowProducer with `continueParentOnFailure`; DLQ + per-row manifest.
- [ ] **F1-API-4** `[M]` **API** — Canva Connect OAuth backend. 🔗 dep:B3 + 🔗 dep:OAUTH-REFRESH-UNWIRED (see the Note in §1.B). **Canon (§9.5):** OAuth 2.0 Auth Code + PKCE (S256), a confidential client, the exchange in the backend, tokens encrypted at rest, server-side `state`, single-use refresh with rotation.
- [ ] **F1-CLI-5** `[M]` **CLI** — Canva embed in the composer. 🔗 dep:F1-API-4.
- [ ] **F1-DEC-1** `[S]` — Mobile decision: Expo (RN) vs PWA → an ADR in `docs/technical/`. **Canon (§9.5):** Expo + React Native + Expo Router (iOS/Android/web), EAS + OTA; PWA as the MVP tier. A candidate to defer post-Phase 1. It is a decision, not code — it can go in parallel.

## §3 — PHASE 2 · PRODUCT — Nice to have (agency objections)

> 33 tasks, all pending: 20 previous ones (the header said 21; recounted on 2026-10-05), 11 added that day by the mapping of the dead code's intentions (§5.13) and 2 on 2026-10-06 by the decision of PREG-1 (PUBAPI-1 and PUBAPI-2). Several are a 🔗 dep of the closure of the multi-tenant guards (N-SEC-3) because they create tenant-scoped data. Canon per track in §9.

- **Reviews:** F2-WRK-1 `[M]` GBP/Yelp/Trustpilot adapters · F2-API-1 `[S]` model + low-star alerts (🔗F2-WRK-1) · F2-CLI-1 `[M]` inbox + reply (🔗F2-API-1). **Canon §9.2:** adapters per source, rate-limit-aware polling, idempotent upsert `(source, externalReviewId)` + content hash, event `ReviewIngested`.
- **White-label:** F2-API-2 `[M]` tenant-by-hostname + branding · F2-ADM-1 `[S]` config (🔗F2-API-2) · F2-CLI-2 `[M]` CSS-vars theming (🔗F2-API-2). **Canon §9.5:** edge middleware resolves the tenant by hostname (cached lookup), branding through runtime CSS custom properties, not per-tenant builds.
- **Recycling/evergreen** (🔗B2): F2-API-3 `[S]` recurrence model + cooldown · F2-WRK-2 `[M]` re-enqueueing on `completed` (🔗F2-API-3) · F2-CLI-3 `[S]` queues UI. **Canon §9.3:** re-enqueue on the `completed` event with a recomputed slot, not a static repeatable; a minimum-time guard.
- **Moderation:** F2-API-4 `[M]` cascade engine (rules→LLM judge) · F2-WRK-3 `[S]` apply in the inbox sync (🔗F2-API-4) · F2-CLI-4 `[S]` rules UI. **Canon §9.2:** fast deterministic rules → an LLM policy-as-prompt only on escalation; rules as data.
- **Collision:** F2-API-5 `[M]` short-TTL lease + OCC on send · F2-CLI-5 `[S]` WebSocket presence (🔗F2-API-5). **Canon §9.2:** a short-TTL distributed lease + presence + an optimistic concurrency check on send.
- **Complete-the-partials:** F2-API-6 `[S]` competitor benchmarking · F2-API-7 `[S]` public link-in-bio · F2-API-8 `[M]` AI carousels (🔗F0-API-1) · F2-API-9 `[M]` stateless MCP server (🔗B3) · F2-API-10 `[S]` Looker connector (🔗B5) · F2-API-11 `[S]` custom report builder (🔗B5). **Canon §9.4/§9.5** per item.
- **Expanded organic analytics** (added 2026-10-05, intention of C8): F2-API-12 `[M]` **API+PORTS** a typed contract of `fetchAnalytics` in `ProviderAdapter` and an expanded metrics model — reach, real impressions, saves, link clicks, watch time, video retention and completion, follower growth, and stories and reels metrics —, each registered in the `MetricRegistry` with a single definition (🔗 dep:DEF-32) · F2-WRK-4 `[M]` **WRK+PROVIDERS** expanded ingestion by network family, split into slices (Meta → TikTok → YouTube → the rest), with the API version and the window stored in each row (🔗 dep:F2-API-12) · F2-API-13 `[S]` **API** audience demographics (age, gender, country, city, active hours and days) and hashtag tracking as time series (🔗 dep:F2-API-12) · F2-CLI-6 `[S]` **CLI** demographics, hashtag and video views in analytics (🔗 dep:F2-API-13). **Canon §9.4:** ELT into the star schema (`fact_post_metrics` at daily grain per tenant and conformed dimensions, `FEATURE_TRACE_MATRIX.md`, row `Cross-platform analytics modeling`); hashtag→ID cached and quota budgeted, demographics from the audience insights (`:365`); the names of the metrics and the version of each API are verified in the provider's doc when implementing. **DoD:** per network, a new metric travels from the API to a report through the registry; a contract test of the adapter; no unmodeled metric shows up as zero. It is reimplemented against the current API; the March 2026 implementation (`facebook/src/analytics/insights*.ts`, `tiktok/src/contentAnalyticsClient.ts`) never ran and leaves with card C8 (§5.13).
- **Alerts and attention metrics** (added 2026-10-05, intention of C8): F2-API-14 `[S]` **API+WRK** performance and sentiment alerts — spike, drop, a post going viral, a rise in negative sentiment — evaluated over the rollups, with thresholds configurable as data and delivered by the notification policy (🔗 dep:F2-API-12, N-COR-11) · F2-API-15 `[S]` **API** inbox attention metrics — time to first response, response rate, resolved conversations, SLA compliance and moderation actions —, registered in the `MetricRegistry` and available in the reports (🔗 dep:F2-API-11). **Canon:** the alert is an event derived from the rollup, never a synchronous poll in the request; crisis mode is still a manual action. **DoD:** a synthetic drop of the rollup triggers a single alert per window; the attention metrics come from the inbox data, not from fixed values; deterministic tests. It is reimplemented against the current API; the March 2026 implementation (`facebook/src/analytics/insights.ts`, `facebook/src/features/community.ts`) never ran and leaves with card C8 (§5.13).
- **Inbox: direct messages and saved replies** (added 2026-10-05, intention of C8): F2-WRK-5 `[M]` **WRK+PORTS+PROVIDERS** direct messages in the inbox — webhook-first ingestion with a re-read of the state before processing and a reply through a new optional method of the port —, starting with Messenger and Instagram Direct (🔗 dep:WEBHOOK-INGEST) · F2-API-16 `[S]` **API** saved replies with variables, per workspace · F2-CLI-7 `[S]` **CLI** saved-reply picker in the inbox (🔗 dep:F2-API-16). **Canon §9.2:** webhooks are notifications, the message is persisted by its ID before the ack, and Meta's 24 h response window is respected on send (`FEATURE_TRACE_MATRIX.md`, row `Unified inbox (sync)`); the domain already has `DIRECT_MESSAGE` (`schema.prisma:2136`) and the Instagram processor already interprets messages (`instagramWebhookProcessor.ts:102-105`). **DoD:** a DM enters the inbox only once and is answered from the UI; a reply outside the window is rejected with its reason; contract tests per network. It covers the catalog row "Saved replies / templates" (`COMPETITIVE_ANALYSIS.md:276`), which does not yet appear in the trace matrix. It is reimplemented against the current API; the March 2026 implementation (`facebook/src/features/community.ts`) never ran and leaves with card C8 (§5.13).
- **Hashtag manager** (added 2026-10-05, intention of C8): F2-API-17 `[S]` **API** groups of saved hashtags per workspace and suggestions by topic and by performance (🔗 dep:F2-API-13) · F2-CLI-8 `[S]` **CLI** hashtag picker in the composer (🔗 dep:F2-API-17). **Canon §9.1/§9.4:** suggestions in the same structured call as the caption (`FEATURE_TRACE_MATRIX.md`, row `Hashtag/caption + image-to-caption`) and ranking with rollup data (row `Hashtag tracking + demographics`); it does not depend on TikTok's Research API, which the client itself described as being for academic research (verify in the provider's doc). **DoD:** the user saves a group and inserts it in the composer; each suggestion cites its source; a component test and a contract test. It covers "Hashtag manager/generator" (`COMPETITIVE_ANALYSIS.md:243`; matrix 📐 `FEATURE_TRACE_MATRIX.md`, row `Hashtag manager/generator`). It is reimplemented against the current API; the March 2026 implementation (`tiktok/src/hashtagManager.ts`, `hashtagDiscovery.ts`, `hashtagAnalytics.ts`) never ran and leaves with card C8 (§5.13).
- **Moderation — amendment to F2-API-4 / F2-WRK-3** (2026-10-05, intention of C8): the action is executed in the source network. F2-WRK-3 applies the engine's decision — hide, delete, approve, block the author or report spam — through a new optional method of the port (`moderateComment?`), after verifying that the channel's token has moderation permission on the page or the account. **DoD of F2-WRK-3 (it replaces the previous one):** a comment that triggers a rule stays hidden in the network and the action appears in the audit with the rule that decided it; a contract test per network. It is the catalog's definition ("Auto-hide/tag/reply according to rules", `COMPETITIVE_ANALYSIS.md:281`). It is reimplemented against the current API; the March 2026 implementation (`facebook/src/features/community.ts`) never ran and leaves with card C8 (§5.13).
- **Recycling — amendment to F2-API-3** (2026-10-05, intention of C9): an evergreen queue can be fed from a template with variables; triggers by keyword, event or performance are resolved in listening, RSS (F3-WRK-3) and repurpose, which already exist, without an automation engine of templates of its own. The March 2026 automations UI (`apps/client/components/content/templates/Automation*.tsx`) never had a backend and leaves with card C9 (§5.13).
- **Public API** (added 2026-10-06, decision of PREG-1, §5.13): PUBAPI-1 `[M]` **API+PKG+CLI** — scoped API keys for the customer's own scripts, on the live pattern of `IntegrationApiKey` (`zap_`/`mak_`, `apps/api/src/auth/integrationAuthMiddleware.ts`): an owner or an administrator of the account creates them, they are shown only once and stored as a hash according to ADR-0034 (verifiers); read and write scopes per resource family that a middleware applies on each request, an optional restriction per workspace, an expiry chosen at creation, a rotation that keeps the previous key valid for 7 days at most, immediate revocation, a last-used date, the per-key quota of stage 2 of AUTH-RL-1 and an audit row per lifecycle event (🔗 dep:AUTH-RL-1) · PUBAPI-2 `[M]` **API** — an OAuth 2.1 authorization server for third-party applications (authorization code with PKCE S256, single-use refresh, RFC 9728 metadata), which is scheduled when there is a third-party consumer or a public MCP directory that needs delegated access (🔗 dep:PUBAPI-1; related: F2-API-9, which can start with a PUBAPI-1 key). **Canon:** two levels, like Buffer: the owner's keys for their scripts and OAuth for third-party applications; no leader rotates a customer's keys by calendar, and Stripe keeps a rotated key for up to 7 days; OWASP API2:2023 admits keys to authenticate API clients, not users; the canon of the public API and of MCP is still OAuth 2.1 (`FEATURE_TRACE_MATRIX.md`, row `Public API + MCP`). **DoD of PUBAPI-1:** a read key gets a 403 on a write; a rotated key works within its overlap and stops working when it expires; a revoked key fails on the next request; no key stays in clear in the database or in the logs; integration tests per rule. **DoD of PUBAPI-2:** a third-party application obtains a token with the code flow and PKCE, a refresh used twice is rejected, and the server publishes its RFC 9728 metadata. The customer's `ApiKey` portion is not revived: DC-13 deletes it (§5.13).

## §4 — PHASE 3 · PRODUCT — Differentiation (after closing Phase 1)

> 22 tasks: 15 previous ones, F3-API-9 in DEFER included (the header said 14; recounted on 2026-10-05), 5 added that day by the mapping of the dead code's intentions (§5.13) and 2 on 2026-10-06 by the decision of PREG-9 (F3-API-13 and F3-API-14). §8.5: do not start with Phase 1 open.

F3-API-1 `[M]` multi-tone triage + self-correction (🔗F0-API-2) · F3-WRK-1 `[M]` real AI text-to-video (async+webhook) + F3-API-2/F3-CLI-1 · F3-API-3 `[M]` content discovery + F3-WRK-2/F3-CLI-2 · F3-WRK-3 `[M]` RSS auto-posting (🔗B2) + F3-API-4/F3-CLI-3 · F3-API-5 `[S]` image-to-caption (🔗F0-API-1) · F3-API-6 `[S]` AI alt-text (🔗F0-API-1) · F3-API-7 `[M]` paid ads analytics (note P-GATE-2: F3-API-8 audience-targeting captures the boost but NOT the dependency on Meta business-verification) · F3-API-8 `[S]` audience targeting · **F3-API-9** `[S]` employee advocacy — **DEFER, only if the ICP includes enterprise** (matrix ⛔ + §8.4 #16; rescued P-GATE-2).

**Added on 2026-10-05 (intention of C8):** F3-API-10 `[M]` **API+WRK+PROVIDERS** stories as a schedulable sequence — an ordered set of slides (image or video, duration, text and the interactive elements each API admits) scheduled and published by the existing engine (saga + worker), with a status per slide and expiry at 24 h; Instagram and Facebook first (🔗 dep:DEF-31) · F3-CLI-4 `[M]` **CLI** story editor: timeline, order, 9:16 preview and scheduling (🔗 dep:F3-API-10) · F3-WRK-4 `[M]` **WRK+INFRA** media adaptation per network — transcode, compress, crop to the aspect ratio and split a long video into story segments — in an asynchronous job that writes keys to the configured storage; an ADR decides where ffmpeg runs (today no image installs it) and revisits the image cap of ADR-0029 (`:136`) (🔗 dep:STOR-8, STOR-9) · F3-API-11 `[S]` **API+WRK** automatic video subtitles per language, as a file where the network accepts it and burned in where it does not (🔗 dep:F3-WRK-4) · F3-API-12 `[S]` **API+CLI** video covers: a chosen frame or an uploaded image, per network (YouTube, Reels, TikTok), with automatic frame suggestion (🔗 dep:F3-WRK-4). **Canon §9.3/§9.6:** a delayed job with a deterministic `jobId` and an idempotent handler that stores the network's ID (`FEATURE_TRACE_MATRIX.md`, row `Reliable scheduled publishing`); processing as an async job → storage → short signed URL (`:397`); transcription behind the AI gateway with a tested fallback (`:313`); which stickers, links and covers each API accepts is verified in the provider's doc. They close FN-022 (§5.3) and the `videoProcessor`×3 part of F18-F21 (§5.7). It is reimplemented against the current API; the March 2026 implementation (`apps/client/components/instagram/**`, `InstagramStoryProject`/`InstagramStory`, `apps/api/src/video/**`, `VideoProcessingJob`/`VideoSegment`, `facebook/src/media/videoProcessor*.ts`, `tiktok/src/videoProcessor*.ts`) never ran —the save, schedule and publish buttons of the story editor show "coming soon" (`instagram/stories/page.tsx:22-37`)— and leaves with card C8 (§5.13).

**DoD amendments (2026-10-05, intention of C8):** F3-API-7 — the connectors include TikTok (integrated, audience and creative reports; it requires an approved advertiser account) in addition to Meta and Google · F3-API-8 — estimate reach and audience before promoting, and promote a TikTok post already published; the concrete API is verified in the provider's doc · F3-WRK-2 — the sources include each network's native trends (trending hashtags, sounds and videos) where an API for commercial use exists; TikTok's Research API is for research · F1-CLI-5 (§2) — the video designs exported from Canva (Reels, stories, TikTok) enter the composer: creative editing (text, transitions, music, watermark, video templates) is delegated to Canva and not built in-house. The March 2026 implementation of these capabilities (`facebook/src/analytics/marketing*.ts`, `tiktok/src/marketingApiClient.ts`, `researchApiClient.ts`, the video processors) never ran and leaves with card C8 (§5.13).

**Added on 2026-10-06 (decision of PREG-9, low priority; intention of C11):** F3-API-13 `[S]` **API+CLI** — recommend which networks suit a piece of content: a score per network by format, length, media and the workspace's historical performance on that network, explained in the composer; it recommends, and it neither chooses nor publishes for the user (🔗 dep:F2-API-12) · F3-API-14 `[S]` **API+PROVIDERS** — YouTube playlist optimization: scoring and reordering of a playlist by performance and detection of content gaps, on the live CRUD of `packages/providers/youtube/src/playlistManager.ts` (🔗 dep:F2-WRK-4). **DoD:** each recommendation cites the data it relies on; a contract test of the YouTube adapter for reading and reordering a playlist. The March 2026 implementation (`apps/api/src/providers/providerCapabilityManager.ts`, whose header promises to find the best networks for a piece of content, and `packages/providers/youtube/src/playlistAnalyticsHelpers.ts:14-112`) never ran and leaves with card C11 (§5.13).

**Canon §9.1/§9.4:** video with native audio + an async job (NOT Sora 2, API end Sep-2026); triage in 1 call → a tone-tagged array + self-correction; RSS with Job Scheduler + conditional GET (ETag) + dedupe by GUID; ads into the same star schema as organic + persist the attribution window.

**Never (unless the ICP pivots):** AI voiceover · meme generator · influencer marketing · blog→video · e-commerce product→post · **full ad management** (a separate product category, explicit out-of-scope per feature-decisions D11 — rescued P-GATE-2; it includes campaign optimization recommendations and the creation of branded hashtag challenges) · **shop and catalog on networks** (catalogs, products, variants, collections, sections and product tagging in Facebook/Instagram Shops posts; it extends e-commerce product→post — added 2026-10-05) · **Facebook events** (create, edit, cancel, invite and manage attendance — added 2026-10-06, PREG-11: in Graph API v26.0, consulted that day, creating, updating or deleting an event responds "You can't perform this operation on this endpoint", reading a page's events is reserved to the Facebook Marketing Partners and no permission manages page events. Revisit if Meta reopens the management of page events to third-party applications, with a documented create operation or permission) · **an in-application emergency mode of the limiter** (global hardening under attack — added 2026-10-06, PREG-8: the response to an attack goes at the edge, with its runbook, and inside the application in the per-route switch and the shadow launch of AUTH-RL-1, §5.1. Revisit if the production edge has no attack mode or if there are overload incidents that the edge does not see). Confirmed bloat or outside the scope of the provider's API — no tasks. The March 2026 implementation of _full ad management_, of the shop and of Facebook events (`facebook/src/features/shop*.ts`, `facebook/src/features/event*.ts`, the recommendations of `facebook/src/analytics/marketing*.ts`, `tiktok/src/hashtagManager.ts`) never ran and leaves with card C8 (§5.13), and that of the limiter (`monitoring/rateLimitingDashboard.ts`, which reported an emergency mode that never existed) leaves with card C4.
