## §5 — CROSS-CUTTING / CONTINUOUS (debt, forgotten-features, hygiene)

> Attended to in parallel/background. Each item closes when the workstream of its area touches it (Bucket B of the inventory) or in a dedicated sweep (Bucket C). The IDs `SMELL-*`/`FN-*` come from the detected-smells backlog, which folded into this plan on 2026-10-29: each resolves in the row that carries it or in the §7 closure ledger, and a `§N.N` in a §5.2 row is the id that item had in the normalization roadmap, which folded into §5.2 on 2026-10-28.

### 5.1 Architecture / quality (inventory §3)

- **TARGET-ARCH — target design + compliance (P1)** — design in [`docs/architecture/TARGET_ARCHITECTURE_CANON_ES.md`](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/0086df48af32db3980680158e8b76ada41358dbf/docs/architecture/TARGET_ARCHITECTURE_CANON_ES.md) (it was deleted from the repository on 2026-10-17; git history keeps it and the link leads to its last version): an application core consumable by ALL the deployables, apps = deployment containers, boundaries **enforced** (not by convention), one composition root per executable. **Compliance TODAY = partial:** the core is still trapped in `apps/api` (→ `ARCH-WORKERS-PRISMA` below) and the boundary guard was partial (`SMELL-40`: the depcruise rule did not cover `@packages/api-common`). **FUTURE compliance:** complete the dependency-cruiser gates so that every violation of the core's boundary fails CI, and promote the core to `packages/@core` so that `apps/workers`/future ones consume it. **DoD:** (a) audit the current state vs the target map; (b) depcruise rules that enforce the core's boundaries in CI for every deployable; (c) the core promotion epic closed. It is the reference design of `ARCH-WORKERS-PRISMA` + `SMELL-45`.
- **ARCH-WORKERS-PRISMA** (SMELL-26/39, P1) — workers on direct Prisma, without use-cases/UoW/outbox. 🔗 blocks: Phase 2 recycling/moderation workers. Target design: `TARGET-ARCH` above.
- **ARCH-ROUTES-PRISMA** (WF2 `routes-prisma-03`, P1) — route handlers with a direct PrismaClient.
- **EVENT-DUAL-SYSTEM** (SMELL-10, FN-002, P1) — outbox `EventDispatcher` vs a parallel `EventService`/Redis; the integration-events pipeline is dead in prod. Classify canon-vs-drift.
- **PROVIDER-CAPS-DRIFT** (SMELL-23/FN-042, P1) — `ProviderCapabilities` in 4 places + `ProviderId` `"twitter"` vs `"x"`. Unify.
- **WRK-MENTION-XTENANT** (assessment, HIGH — **it was not in this plan**, added 2026-08-16) — `MentionRepository.ts:62-67` deduplicates by `(provider, externalId)` **without `accountId`**, backed by a DB-level `@@unique([provider, externalId])` (`schema.prisma:2171`): the **second** tenant's mention collides with P2002 and is swallowed as an idempotent success → one tenant can poison/hide another's mention corpus. Verified live 2026-08-16. A candidate for N.A if it is confirmed exploitable without prior coordination.
- **tenant-health-idor-01** (assessment, MEDIUM) — `GET /health/tenant/:id/project/:id` **without authentication**, binding tenant context from a URL segment. It is purely the N.E axis; the plan only grazed it inside N-SEC-3. **The Product Owner's decision 2026-08-16:** it is deleted **only if** an equivalent per-account project health capability **already exists**; if it does not exist, it is **rebuilt properly** (authenticated, scope derived from the principal, an admin counterpart for parity) because knowing the health of the projects per account is part of multi-tenancy. Its tenant seam has run on the guarded client since `433b92b6` (`apps/api/src/index.ts:331-334`), and no two-tenant test proves its scoping: the proof the seam was promised when the swap landed was never written, so a rebuilt route carries it.
- **prov-x-media-auth-context-02** (`confirmed-adversarial`, HIGH) — the bearer-only `XApiClient` ignores OAuth1.0a → every post with media fails at upload. Align `REQUIRED_FIELDS`.
- **prov-classifier-403-drift-03** (`verified` 2026-10-28, HIGH since the shared root cause joined it; backlog SMELL-133) — rescoped on 2026-10-28 from "two providers left" to five providers and the shared root cause: `mapErrorToPublishError` reads only `.status` (`packages/providers/shared/src/helpers.ts:136`) while `AppError` carries `statusCode` (`packages/shared/src/errors.ts:55`), so an `AppError`-shaped failure falls to `NETWORK` (`helpers.ts:149`) and AUTH never fires on the publish path; X maps any 401 or 403 message to AUTH (`packages/providers/x/src/XAdapter.ts:446,507,576,617`), so a duplicate-content 403 reads as an authentication failure; Telegram, Snapchat, Pinterest and Bluesky classify no 403. The 403 fix landed for none of the five. The work is the `70b75124` + `a71bd837` sub-bullet of N-SEC-7, with a shared helper. **DoD:** `helpers.ts` reads `status ?? statusCode` and the nested `reason`; each of the five providers classifies a 403 that is not an authentication failure away from AUTH, with a test in its existing suite.
- **core-refresh-no-rotation-02** (`confirmed-adversarial`, HIGH, backlog SMELL-113) — customer token refresh without invalidation of the consumed one nor reuse detection; a leaked token survives logout 7d. Single-use blacklist + revoke-family.
- **TRIAGE-INJECTION** `[S]` **PKG(core)** (assessment, MEDIUM, `verified` 2026-10-28) — the inbox triage prompt embeds the inbound message body as it arrives, with no length bound, no delimiter escaping and no PII redaction (`packages/core/inbox/src/TriageInboxMessageUseCase.ts:132-143`, the body at `:138`); the guardrail registry (content policy, then PII redaction) runs only over the suggested replies (`:161-180`), so an oversized or instruction-bearing message spends tokens, steers the classification and reaches the operator's reply suggestions. **DoD:** the body is bounded and delimited before it enters the prompt and PII is redacted on the input as on the output, with a test that sends an oversized body and one that carries instructions.
- **ACCOUNTLIFECYCLE-AGG-BYPASS** `[M]` **API+PKG(core)** (assessment, MEDIUM, `verified` 2026-10-28) — the lifecycle of Admin operator accounts changes state by patching fields through `AdminUserRepositoryPort` (`apps/api/src/admin/accountLifecycleService.ts:248`; suspend at `:313`, reactivate at `:382`), with no aggregate method and no domain event, only audit log lines, against the aggregates rule of ARCHITECTURE_CANON (state changes only through methods). **DoD:** an aggregate owns suspend and reactivate with their invariants and events, the service calls it inside the Unit of Work, and a test proves that each change writes its event.
- **REALTIME-FULLSCAN** `[S]` **API** (assessment, LOW, `verified` 2026-10-28) — every 30 seconds (`apps/api/src/analytics/realtimeAnalytics.ts:141`) the realtime analytics loop reads every stored analytics row of each watched post, with its post and channel (`:150-156`; this caller passes `getByPostIds` no `take`, `apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaAnalyticsReadRepository.ts:45-70`), and keeps the latest per post and provider in memory, so its cost grows with each post's history, bounded only by the watched posts and the SSE cap. **DoD:** a query that returns only the latest row per post and provider, with a test that pins how many rows it reads.
- **client-perf-provider-blank-01** `[S]` **CLI** (assessment, HIGH, `verified` 2026-10-28) — `ProjectProvider` derives its blocking `isLoading` from `isFetching` (`apps/client/providers/ProjectProvider.tsx:204-209`) and renders the loader instead of its children while it is true (`:232`); the dashboard layout mounts it with no initial values (`apps/client/app/[locale]/dashboard/layout.tsx:281`), so any background refetch of the customer or projects query, such as a refocus after their 5-minute stale time (`:128`, `:148`), unmounts the dashboard and loses its filters, selection, scroll position and open dialogs. **DoD:** only the first load blocks, a background refetch keeps the children mounted, and a component test refetches and finds the same tree.
- **admin-analytics-fabricated-01** + **admin-compliance-green-01** `[S]` **ADM** (assessment, MEDIUM, `verified` 2026-10-28) — Admin shows figures that no source produces: `systemUptime` is the constant 100 and `securityScore` is the engagement rate (`apps/admin/hooks/api/useAnalytics.ts:124,129`, shown at `apps/admin/app/[locale]/(dashboard)/analytics/page.tsx:190-193,234-248`), and the "GDPR Compliance" card is hardcoded to `compliant` with a score of 100 (`apps/admin/hooks/api/useCompliance/api.ts:91-96`). **DoD:** each figure comes from its source or the card says that none exists, no figure is relabelled from another, and a test per card proves that no constant is shown as a measurement. 🔗 OBS-1 and OBS-2 for uptime.
- **admin-csrf-01** `[S]` **[SECURITY]** **ADM** (assessment, MEDIUM, `verified` 2026-10-28) — the Admin proxy copies the value of the httpOnly `admin-csrf` cookie into the `X-CSRF-Token` header of every request it forwards (`apps/admin/app/api/backend/[...path]/route.ts:78-83`), so the backend's comparison of that header with the session's token (`apps/api/src/security/csrfMiddleware.ts:42-61`) passes for any request that carries the session cookies and proves nothing the session cookie does not; `SameSite=Lax` (`apps/admin/lib/auth/sessionCookie.ts:44`) is the only CSRF defense left. **DoD:** the proxy no longer fills the token (the browser sends it, or the proxy checks `Origin` against an allowlist), with a test that a cross-origin request without it is refused.
- **AUTH-RL-1** `[M]` **API+ADM** — **Rate limits: the change `auth-rate-limit-integrity` enters the plan and the per-principal quota gets an owner.** `verified` 2026-10-05. The change exists only as a folder (then `openspec/changes/auth-rate-limit-integrity/`, now `docs/features/auth-rate-limit-integrity/`, without `tasks.md`) and no row of this plan names it; its C0 still requires `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOP_COUNT`, which ADR-0021 removed. Its design declares the per-principal quota "intention, with no owning change" (`design.md:368-402`), and the canon asks to prefer the authenticated principal over the IP (`SECURITY_CANON.md:96`). The live limiter groups by IP and the resource's route (`httpRateLimitPreHandler.ts:244-249`) and emits no metrics; the `api_rate_limit_*` counters (`apiMetrics.ts:271-311`) have no producer and label by `client_ip`. **DoD:** stage 1 — the design refreshed against ADR-0021 and executed (policy rows; its atomic C9, the 7 `config.rateLimit` blocks, `@fastify/rate-limit` and its test, already left with #679, merge `bdffd273`, 2026-10-22), the AUTH preset on the three uncapped customer credential routes (`c64a718f` (b)) and on `/admin/auth/{login,refresh}` (the `95b4ec66` residue, N-SEC-2's W-C2-1), both from N-SEC-7, together with the consolidation of `ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` §5 item 9 (a single source of rules); stage 2 — a second charge per account or user inside `requireClientAuth`/`requireAdminAuth`, with figures per role or plan that the Product Owner decides; each limiter decision as a metric with bounded labels (rule and result, never the IP) and a block-ratio alert with a runbook. It picks up the intention of `roleBasedRateLimit` (quotas per role, it closes L-26) and of `monitoring/rateLimitingDashboard.ts` (telemetry and abuse per tenant), 2026-03 code that never ran and leaves with cards C2 and C4 (§5.13). Order signed by the Product Owner on 2026-08-16: after `principal-derived-authority`. **Decision of PREG-8 (2026-10-06):** there is no in-application emergency mode of the limiter, which goes to the "Never" line (§4); the response to an attack goes at the edge, and `docs/runbooks/` gains the "edge under attack" runbook (the CDN's attack mode, the WAF's rate rules) when the deployment target exists, which is written then and not before. The row gains two points, and DEF-70 adds a third:
  - **Per-route switch:** turn off a non-critical surface or a limiter rule without deploying, with an audit of who and when; each group of routes carries a criticality label, so that load shedding can be added later (Stripe, "Scaling your API with rate limiters"; Google SRE, "Handling Overload").
  - **Shadow launch:** every new limiter rule first runs measuring what it would block, without blocking, and starts blocking when its metric justifies it.
  - **Input from DEF-70 (2026-10-09):** stage 2 picks up the intention of eleven deleted rules that never matched a route: publishing (`POST /sagas/post-publishing/start`, which also creates drafts), search (`GET /posts` with `searchText`, which shares a route with the listing), the analytics dashboard (`GET /analytics/dashboard`, polled every 30 s, and the SSE `GET /analytics/stream`, which asks for a cap of simultaneous connections per principal and not a per-minute bucket), the webhook history (`GET /webhooks/dashboard/events`), the Admin metrics (`GET /admin/dashboard/stats`, `GET /admin/analytics/metrics`) and the Admin audit (`GET /admin/audit/logs`, `GET /admin/compliance/audit-logs`, polled every 30 s); AI goes separately, to AI-QUOTA-1. Stage 1 replaces `contains`, which the table declares and never evaluates, with policy rows by pattern and method: today the row `/analytics/project/` with `contains: "/full"` also limits `GET /analytics/project/:projectId` to 5 per minute, and the row `/admin/accounts/` with `contains: "/usage"` leaves another 14 Admin routes at 10 per minute.
  - **Input from C-P1b (2026-10-16):** the customer's password-reset email has no per-address cap. `POST /auth/customer/request-password-reset` (`apps/api/src/auth/customerAuthRoutes.ts:666-678`) declares no limit of its own (its inert `config.rateLimit` block left with #679), the table `STANDARD_ROUTE_RULES` names only the customer's login and refresh (`httpRateLimitPreHandler.ts:93-94`) and the case falls into the default cap of 100 per minute per IP and route (`httpRateLimitPreHandler.ts:34`, `apps/api/src/index.ts:507`); `RequestPasswordResetUseCase` sends the email without consulting any per-address limit (`packages/core/customer-auth/src/RequestPasswordResetUseCase.ts:126-136`). Any unauthenticated caller can then have it sent to someone else's address, up to the route's per-IP cap, which is the same exposure the deleted-account notice had. C-P1b bounded that notice with `ThrottledDeletedAccountNoticeMailer` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/adapters/`): one notice per address every 24 hours, through `CachePort`, with a SHA-256 key. Stage 1 of AUTH-RL-1 gives the reset email the same per-address cap. The Admin portal has the same gap with a stricter and live per-IP cap: `POST /admin/auth/password/reset` uses `rateLimit(3, 300000)` (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/adminAuthRoutes.ts:582`), with the key `admin:<ip>:<route>` (`adminAuthMiddleware.ts:228`), and `resetPasswordRequest` sends the email on every call for an active administrator (`adminAuthRoutes.ts:236-275`) with no per-address limit; the per-address cap covers both routes.
- **AI-QUOTA-1** `[M]` **API+PKG(core)+ADM** — **AI quota per account and spending limit at the providers** (added on 2026-10-09 by the closure of DEF-70). `verified` 2026-10-09. The AI routes have no limit of their own: the four rules that the HTTP limiter dedicated to them matched no route (DEF-70) and another six LLM routes never had a rule, so they run with STANDARD, per IP. The only outbound brake is a token bucket per provider and not per account (`apps/api/src/ai/orchestrator.ts:319` calls `tryConsume(providerName)`, with capacity `AI_PROVIDER_REQUESTS_PER_MIN`, 60 per minute, in `setupServices.ts:273-280`), so one tenant can exhaust it for everyone. Usage per account is recorded (`PrismaAiTokenUsageRepository.ts`, a monthly sum by `accountId`), and `SettingsService.ts:238-239` reads it together with the pool's `monthlyTokenBudget` budget; ADR-0030 sets 10,000 tokens per paid channel per month, 20,000 in the trial and 2,000 in the free plan. It remains to verify whether any AI path rejects the request when the account exhausts its allocation. The canon treats AI consumption unmeasured per tenant as a present exposure, not a future one: OWASP LLM10:2025 asks for "rate limiting and user quotas", OWASP API4:2023 asks for "spending limits for all service providers/API integrations" or, if they do not exist, billing alerts, and Anthropic and OpenAI limit by workspace or project. **DoD:** an AI request is rejected per account when its monthly ADR-0030 allocation is exhausted, with a test; the outbound brake divides the capacity by account, so that one tenant does not exhaust the others'; each configured provider has a spending limit or, if it does not offer one, a billing alert, documented in `docs/deployment/`. The per-minute rate of the AI routes per principal is set by stage 2 of AUTH-RL-1.
  - **Launch blocker** (the Product Owner, 2026-10-10): it closes within its turn, before the first real tenant uses the platform's AI.
  - **The Product Owner's model (2026-10-09):** each plan brings a monthly AI allocation (ADR-0030: per paid channel, BILL-21) and prepaid packs can be bought (BILL-31); a counter per account, with the consumption attributed to each workspace (the agency's client) and to each member, so that the owner sees who spends the most; the owner assigns quotas per workspace or per team, taken from the account's pool; the platform pays its providers by usage and reconciles that invoice with each tenant's consumption; if the account exhausts its allocation and has no pack, the whole plan continues except content creation with the platform's AI, and whatever runs with the tenant's own key (BYOK) continues as normal and is recorded without deducting.
  - **Market (2026-10-09):** among the social media tools there is "unlimited" AI with the plan (Buffer, Sendible, SocialBee), credits per plan (Later 5/50/100 per month with packs of 100; Ocoya; Vista Social, which cut theirs in 2026; Zoho Social, without rollover; Metricool, per brand; Sprout Social, per seat) and BYOK (Publer; Zoho Social as the way out when the credits run out; Sprinklr). None lets an agency's administrator assign quotas per client or per team: that pattern lives in AI and development platforms (Microsoft Copilot Studio divides a pool per environment; GitHub Copilot sets budgets per organization, cost center and user, and does not count BYOK usage; Anthropic limits per workspace and "Organization-wide limits always apply, even if workspace limits add up to more"; OpenAI limits per project). It is a differentiator for agencies.
  - **Measured state (2026-10-09):** `AiTokenUsage` (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma:3606-3617`) stores estimated tokens (characters / 4 or a fixed 1000, `apps/api/src/ai/orchestrator.ts:384,572`), because the adapters discard the provider's `usage`, and it does not record model, input and output, cost, workspace, member or function. Only what goes through `AiRequestService.executeRequest` is recorded: structured generation, embeddings, images, background jobs and every call without `accountId` use the platform's orchestrator without a record (`orchestrator.ts:171`, `apps/api/src/ai/aiService.ts:156-238,427-435`). There are three budgets that do not match: the one that is applied, providers × `accountCount` × 10,000 (`packages/core/ai/src/AiRequestService.ts:151-178`, failing open, with no reservation and no 429); the one the screen shows, a global `monthlyTokenBudget` of the platform (`packages/core/settings/src/SettingsService.ts:238-239`); and ADR-0030's, per paid channel. BYOK is stored per account (`AccountCredential`, group `AI_BYOK`) and recorded with `isByok`; in practice only the OpenAI key is used (`AiRequestService.ts:65-74`, `orchestrator.ts:191-208`). There is no "team" entity: the workspace is `Project` and the member has a role for the whole account and `ProjectMember.permissions` per workspace. There are no packs, no one-time purchases and no credit ledger.
  - **Open decisions for the Product Owner and the Maintainer when the turn comes**, with the recommendation in parentheses: (1) the unit: flat tokens, like ADR-0030, or a cost-weighted credit (a weighted credit, like GitHub and Metronome, because a token of an expensive model does not cost what a cheap one does; it amends ADR-0030 with a new ADR); (2) the semantics of the sub-quotas: a ceiling, like Anthropic, or a reservation, like Copilot Studio (a ceiling: the account always rules and never blocks unused balance); (3) the unit of allocation: workspace and member, which exist, or a new "team" entity (workspace and member first); (4) expiry and order of consumption (the plan's allocation expires at the end of the period without rollover and is consumed first; the packs afterwards, the one that expires sooner first, like Orb and Metronome); (5) on exhaustion: a hard cut of the platform's AI or overage billing (a hard cut with packs, the Product Owner's model; alerts at 75, 90 and 100 %); (6) the reconciliation: record per call the provider, the model, the input, output and cache tokens, the current price, the cost, the account, the workspace, the member and the function, and compare it daily with OpenAI's costs API and Anthropic's Usage & Cost Admin API, with provider projects or workspaces per environment and not per tenant (Anthropic admits 100 workspaces per organization); (7) the real-time control: a credit ledger of our own, append-only, that reserves before the call and settles with the real usage (Stripe's credits apply at invoicing and cannot cut usage at the moment).
  - **Input from SMELL-16 — trend radar web search** (backlog SMELL-16, MEDIUM, `verified` 2026-10-29) — every day, `DispatchDetectTrendsUseCase` enqueues one detection job per account with active channels (`packages/core/trends/src/DispatchDetectTrendsUseCase.ts:3-4`, `:66`). The composition root always puts `PerplexityTrendingAdapter` in the source list (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/setupTrendUseCases.ts:64-65`); it filters only by the caller's `sources` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PerplexityTrendingAdapter.ts:24`) and calls `generateStructured` with the account id (`:44-48`). So every account spends the platform's web-search tokens whatever its plan, through structured generation, a path that, as this row records, writes no usage. **The Product Owner's decision:** whether web-search trends count against each plan's AI allocation or are sold as an add-on. **DoD:** the detection job checks the account's entitlement before it calls the web-search source; a test shows an account without the entitlement gets the other two sources only.
  - **Expanded DoD:** the provider's real tokens with their cost; every platform AI path recorded and attributed; `accountId` always from the authenticated principal; a single budget, ADR-0030's or its replacement; BYOK exempt and visible separately as "not billed", with every provider the UI offers; exhaustion tests (the platform's AI is cut, the rest of the plan and BYOK continue); a daily reconciliation with a deviation alert. 🔗 dep: BILL-21 (the pool per channel), BILL-26 (the free plan's cap), N-SEC-4 (the AI cache without `accountId` records `tokensUsed=0` on hits), CRED-8b (a single source of the pool's keys), DEF-73 and DEF-74. The per-minute rate per principal of the AI routes is set by stage 2 of AUTH-RL-1; BYOK as a credential is already decided in §5.14 (D4).
- **SEC-KEK-1** `[S]` **API+DOCS** — **Key separation by purpose for the channels' OAuth tokens: decide it or declare it.** `pending-review`. `OAUTH_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (`config/env.ts:114`) was required at startup for "ProviderConnection token columns" (`secretCatalog.ts:76-79`), a model that does not exist; the OAuth tokens live in `Channel.credentials` under `PLATFORM_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, together with the rest of Class A (`secretCatalog.ts:71-74`), with AAD per channel and rotation by versions (`env.ts:113-129`). The repository already separates keys when the threat model changes (`env.ts:144-147`). The 2026-03 variable is deleted with card C3 without ever having had a reader. **DoD:** an ADR decides whether the tenants' OAuth tokens carry a KEK of their own, with its re-encryption path, or whether a single KEK covers Class A; `docs/security/SECRETS.md` aligned. 🔗 dep: DEF-29 (finding F1: AAD of `Channel.credentials` between API, seed and workers), closed first. **Related (2026-10-06):** ADR-0034 sets the key custody of the five encrypted families —a single key-encryption provider with one data key per record (decisions 4 and 5)— and retires `OAUTH_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (decision 13); this row is adjudicated against it.
- **SEC-SSRF-1** `[M]` **[SECURITY]** **API+CLI** — **SSRF control on the HTTP egress toward URLs that come from the tenant.** `pending-review`. There is no network guard for those egresses (`rg 'isPrivateIp|ssrf|isLoopback'` = 0); the live control is the media host list `makeMediaUrlSchema(ALLOWED_MEDIA_HOSTS)` (`security/inputValidation.ts:174,287`), and the portal's image optimizer accepts any https host (`apps/client/next.config.mjs:30-33`, `hostname: "**"`). The URL validation "with domain restrictions" of `validation/secureSchemas.ts:113-136` (2026-03, never connected; an `includes("localhost")`) is deleted with card C3. **DoD:** an inventory of the egress points toward tenant URLs, including the Next optimizer; an outbound client that resolves DNS and rejects loopback, private ranges, link-local and cloud metadata, also after redirects; negative tests per point.
- **AUTH-AUDIT-1** `[M]` **PKG(core)+API** — **Audit trail of the customer's authentication flows** (`ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` E1, §5 item 6). `verified` 2026-10-05. On the customer side only failures are recorded (`LOGIN_FAILURE` from `RedisBruteForceAdapter.ts:214-216`); the use cases of `packages/core/customer-auth` do not write `AuditLog` for a successful login, refresh, logout, registration or reset (`rg -i audit` only finds a comment). The forensic table `AdminLoginAttempt` (2026-03, no writes) is deleted with card C7; the admin side's trail lives in `AuditLog`. **DoD:** the use cases emit rows through an injected port, with a CUSTOMER actor, IP and agent; an integration test per flow. 🔗 related: N-AUTH-2 (the reader).
- **AUTH-OBS-1** `[M]` **API+INFRA** — **Observability and security alerts of authentication** (`ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` E9-E11, §5 item 15). `verified` 2026-10-05. Today there are two security alerts (`prometheus/alerts/api.yml:35-61`: MFA backup-code reuse and Admin refresh replay, over `api_security_threats_total`); there are no metrics of login attempts, MFA verifications, revocations or refresh failures, nor alerts of a brute-force spike, a burst of MFA failures or a mass revocation. **DoD:** `login_attempts_total{side,result}`, `mfa_verifications_total`, `session_revocations_total` and `token_refresh_failures_total` with bounded labels; alerts of a brute-force spike, a burst of MFA failures, a mass revocation and a mass modification or deletion by the same actor, each one with a runbook, the compromised-account one included. It picks up the intention of `security/auditLogger.ts` (threshold alerts over the audit trail) and of `BusinessKPITracker.trackUserEngagement` (active sessions), 2026-03 code that never ran and leaves with cards C4 and C6 (§5.13).
- **AUTH-RISK-1** `[M]` **API+PKG(core)+CLI+ADM** — **Risk-based login with the category's practice, without a scoring engine** (added 2026-10-06, decision of PREG-2). `pending-review`. Today there are counters per identifier and per IP with backoff, lockout, a CAPTCHA signal after 3 failures and `LOGIN_FAILURE` rows (`RedisBruteForceAdapter.ts:79-90,214-216`, `LoginCustomerUseCase.ts:55-57`), with no device memory and no sign-in notice. **DoD:** in the customer and Admin logins, a signed known-device cookie (a security cookie, exempt from consent according to WP29, Opinion 04/2012, §3.3); on an unknown device, an email verification before issuing the session when the user has no MFA, or the MFA request when they have it; an email on each new sign-in (device, approximate location, time) with a "it wasn't me" path, and on the addition or removal of an authenticator and on recovery (NIST SP 800-63B-4 §4.6); the account owner can require MFA of all the members, with a grace period; audit rows for everything. No risk engine is built (threat score, scored geolocation, device fingerprint); revisit if an enterprise customer requires it. The current counters, lockout, CAPTCHA and audit remain as the base layer. It picks up the intention of `deviceId` and `location` of `AdminLoginAttempt` (2026-03, no writes), which leaves with card C7 (§5.13). 🔗 dep: the customer's self-service MFA (§5.3.b), the decision on the customer's session model (question 2 of `ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` §7), AUTH-AUDIT-1, AUTH-OBS-1.
- **SUPPORT-ACCESS-1** `[M]` **API+ADM+PKG(core)** — **Support access to a tenant's data only with a time-bound grant** (added 2026-10-06, decisions of PREG-3 and PREG-10). `pending-review`. Today an operator reads across tenants through `withSystemContext` without leaving a trace at run time (question 6 of `ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` §7). **DoD:** an operator reaches the data of ONE tenant only with an active grant: a written justification, the ticket reference, a maximum duration in hours and step-up MFA on activating it; each read is audited with the grant's id, and the grant expires on its own; `withSystemContext` remains the code path and the grant becomes its precondition in support reads. It is the staff's only access to the customers' content: there is no cross-tenant content library (PREG-10). It answers question 6 of `ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` §7. 🔗 dep: N-SEC-5.b.
- **BREAKGLASS-1** `[S]` **ADM+INFRA+DOCS** — **Admin emergency accounts, sealed and watched** (added 2026-10-06, decision of PREG-3). `pending-review`. **DoD:** one or two emergency administration accounts outside SSO, with sealed credentials; each use alerts the owner and is audited; a runbook to use them and to review them after each use, with a test that they work at least every 90 days, as Microsoft recommends for its emergency access accounts. 🔗 dep: AUTH-OBS-1 (the alert).
- **ADM-JIT-1** `[M]` **API+ADM** — **Time-bound elevation of an administrator above their role** (added 2026-10-06, decision of PREG-3; a row recorded for later). `pending-review`. Roles stay for routine work; this row is scheduled when the number of operators grows. **DoD:** eligible versus active assignments, activation with a justification, optional approval, MFA on activation, a maximum duration, a notice on activation, periodic review and audit (Microsoft Entra PIM, Google Privileged Access Manager; NIST SP 800-53 AC-2(2) and AC-6), on the grant machinery of SUPPORT-ACCESS-1. It picks up the expiry intention of `AdminUserPermission` (2026-03, unused), which leaves with card C7 without reusing anything, and gap D14 of `ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md`. 🔗 dep: SUPPORT-ACCESS-1.

**Folded from the detected-smells backlog on 2026-10-29** (ALR-U6-9, §5.17, decision B): each row below carries its backlog id and was re-read in the code that day; the backlog's closed and not-a-defect ids are in the §7 closure ledger.

- **TEST-TYPECHECK-PERIMETER** `[L]` **CI+TEST** (backlog SMELL-68, HIGH, `verified` 2026-10-29) — outside `apps/api`, whose tests a ratchet compiles (`apps/api/scripts/typecheck-tests-ratchet.mjs`), and `packages/adapters/db-prisma` (`include: ["src", "tests"]`), no compiler opens test files. 69 packages run `tsc --noEmit` over a `tsconfig.json` that includes `src` only (for example `packages/core/domain/tsconfig.json:6`) and hold 153 files under `tests/`. `apps/admin/tsconfig.json:30` and `apps/client/tsconfig.json:43` exclude their tests (110 `.ts` and `.tsx` files). Four packages (`crm-hubspot`, `crm-salesforce`, `storage-cloudinary`, `providers/_template`) have no `typecheck` script. Nine packages run `tsc --noEmit` without a `tsconfig.json` of their own, and each task reports green: eight (`packages/adapters/{cache-redis,dead-letter-queue,external-apis,fallback-strategies,queue-bullmq,storage-s3}`, `packages/monitoring/{circuit-breaker,health-checks}`) resolve the root solution config (`tsconfig.json:5`, `"files": []`, confirmed with `tsc --showConfig`) and compile no file, and `packages/core/threading` resolves `packages/core/tsconfig.json`, which compiles the two files of `packages/core/src` and none of its own. **DoD:** every workspace that has tests has a typecheck program that opens them: hard-zero where they compile clean, otherwise a ratchet shaped like the `apps/api` one. The nine packages get a `tsconfig.json` that opens their `src` and `tests`. A type error planted in one test of each group turns `turbo run typecheck` red. 🔗 related: DEF-128 (the portals' tests).
- **RAG-LIVE-UNVALIDATED** `[S]` **CI+TEST** (backlog SMELL-21, LOW, `verified` 2026-10-29) — the grounded path of localized generation has never run against a real provider: an embedding on upsert, the pgvector cosine retrieval, and a 200 with populated `usedTerms` and `usedRules`. Sequenced after DEF-81, which quarantines the same file.

- **TENANT-PARENT-FK** `[M]` **INFRA** (backlog SMELL-69, MEDIUM, `verified` 2026-10-29) — nothing in the database ties a denormalized `accountId` to its parent project's: `Channel` references `Project` by `projectId` alone (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma:862`) although `Project` already carries `@@unique([id, accountId])` (`:689`), and only the `Post` trio has composite foreign keys (`:706`, `:790`, `:812`). The other enrolled `projectId`-only models (ExternalNotificationConfig, ScheduledReport, Campaign, RecurringPost, TrackedLink, GeneratedImage, ProjectMember, Channel) rely on the create paths and the backfill assertion; a divergent `Channel` row makes the worker's scoped lookup match nothing while the job reports success. Slice 2 of N-SEC-3 triages the keyless models and does not cover these. Sixteen more models carry `accountId` (`WebhookEvent`'s is optional, and fitness #39 counts it) and reference `Project` by `projectId` alone, for example `Mention`, `MediaAsset` and `Template` (24 in all, measured 2026-10-29). **DoD:** a divergence audit query run first, then composite foreign keys `(projectId, accountId) → Project(id, accountId)` for the eight models (NOT VALID, then VALIDATE, as the Post trio did), the same audit and key for the other sixteen or a recorded reason per model, and an integration test that the engine refuses an inconsistent insert.
- **SAGA-ACCOUNT-LEG1** `[S]` **INFRA+API(saga)** (backlog SMELL-70, MEDIUM, `verified` 2026-10-29) — `SagaInstance.accountId` is still `String?` with no `Account` relation (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma:2113`), kept nullable for the backfill's NULL-sentinel rows; such a row matches no tenant GUC, and nothing stops a future writer from leaving the column empty. N-COR-7 closed the user-id defect, and `saga-engine-terminal-hygiene` keeps this flip out of scope (`docs/features/saga-engine-terminal-hygiene/proposal.md:31`). The Maintainer decides the sentinel rows' disposition: keep them behind a partial constraint, or archive them. **DoD:** that decision recorded, then one migration that applies it, adds the `Account` relation and makes the column non-null for live rows, with an integration test that a saga insert without an account is refused.
- **FK-CASCADE-INDEX** `[M]` **INFRA** (backlog SMELL-89, MEDIUM, `verified` 2026-10-29) — a foreign key whose referencing column leads no index makes every cascade delete scan the child table once per destroyed parent, and single-column indexes narrowed to `WHERE "deletedAt" IS NULL` cannot serve a cascade. The backlog row counted 31 such edges after a migration `20260903040000_hard_delete_cascade_fk_indexes` that indexed 10; that migration is in no tree on `main` (the deletion PRs added `infra/prisma/migrations/20260830220417_*` to `20260901120100_*`, and none indexes these edges), so the real count is unmeasured and may be the original 41. **DoD:** that row's catalog query (at the §7 closure ledger's permalink), run against a migrated database, records the list; each edge is indexed or kept on a measured cascade timing, and account-erasure time at 10 000 posts is recorded before and after.
- **BASE-CLIENT-WRITE** `[L]` **API+PKG(adapters)+CI** (backlog SMELL-90, HIGH, `verified` 2026-10-29) — a repository that writes on its injected base client instead of `PrismaUnitOfWork.getTransactionClient()` leaves its caller's transaction: the write survives the caller's rollback, and under the non-bypassing role it fails as `42501`. `rg -oP "this\.prisma\.[a-zA-Z]+\.(create|createMany|update|updateMany|upsert|delete|deleteMany)\b" apps/api/src -g '*.ts' --stats` reports 245 sites in 94 files on 2026-10-29 (257 in 97 when filed); 14 files of `apps/api/src` resolve the transactional client (17 with `packages/adapters`), and no gate reads the class (fitness #40 covers `.$transaction(` only). **DoD:** a fitness check or lint rule that fails on a base-client write in a class that can run inside a unit of work, with its red path shown, then the sites swept until the measurement falls to an allowlisted remainder, each kept site with its reason.

### 5.2 Normalization roadmap (inventory §3A, canon)

**Origin.** On 2026-10-28 the normalization roadmap, `docs/architecture/NORMALIZATION_ROADMAP.md`, folded into this section and left the tree (the Product Owner's decision; [its last version](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/3758be3ee3d4274bcc01582b437080732f50d11d/docs/architecture/NORMALIZATION_ROADMAP.md)). It tracked how the product is built (foundations, developer experience, security, resilience and operations) beside the product phases, which track what is built. Four of its follow-ups belong to N-TEST-1 (§1): the coverage ratchet §2.2.b, the provider MSW handlers §3.2.b and the chaos scenarios §4.1.b and §4.1.c. Every other open item or follow-up is a row below and keeps its roadmap id, so a citation such as `§4.2.c` resolves here. Its closed items, its deferral review (§0.2), its tracking workflow (replaced by §0.1 of this plan), its priority queue and its changelog are history and stay at that link. The tripwire ramp-up of §1.6 (its phase 3) has no DoD of its own: it is the standing procedure of `CLAUDE.md` §Mandatory Pre-Action Triggers, "How to extend tripwires". §5.1.e, deleting the then-empty `@core/application` package, no longer applies: the package holds `UseCase.ts`, `hardDeletePolicy.ts` and `retryOnWriteConflict.ts`, and `ARCHITECTURE_CANON.md` §How to extend (item 4) names `@core/application/UseCase` as vocabulary a port may import.

**What the architecture costs** (roadmap §0.1, still true). Hexagonal ports and adapters, DDD, CQRS, the Unit of Work and sagas spread a trivial feature over at least five files in four directories (entity, port, use case, adapter and routes) where a modular Fastify layout keeps one folder, so navigation and onboarding are slower; a trivial endpoint needs at least four new files, its DI wiring and a Unit of Work when it mutates; most TypeScript developers know modular Fastify, the Next App Router and BullMQ, and few know DDD, CQRS or sagas; and the ports, the fitness functions, the composition root and the saga engine protect the design while making a radical pivot expensive. What pays that cost back is the work this section and N-TEST-1 carry: documented decisions (the ADRs), developer experience, tests, the tenant guards and chaos testing.

> **ℹ️ CARD [IMAGE]** — the four container images went from red since 2026-07-31 to verified, with six defects fixed, five of them invisible without running the image; landed with #304 (merge `9e5175e4`) on 2026-09-25, and re-pinned in `96df910` after CVE-2026-84782 (`docs/deployment/base-images.md`). The Maintainer's trigger decision of 2026-10-05 (option A: `production-ci.yml` on pull requests against `main`, `omni-post-cc`, `feature/**` and `workstream/**`, delivered by #419, merge `7d73b00d`) lives in [base-images.md](../deployment/base-images.md#when-ci-runs-the-images), with its cost, its reason and the options it rejected. Still named and open: 133M of build tooling in the production closure (`docs/deployment/image-build-model.md`) and three gaps of the import assertion in the build stage (a non-resolver error passes as success, there is no `timeout`, and the portal smoke assumes a single `EXPOSE`). The full card lives at [this file at `c8c62cbf`](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/c8c62cbf06c1a38031a0c5a22b7339f120f50497/docs/product/MASTER_PLAN_ES.md).

- [ ] **IMAGE-1** `[S]` **CI** (roadmap §6.1, P1) — **The images' last two gates: `Container Security` as a required check, and a retried or mirrored Docker Hub pull.** `verified` 2026-10-28. The image hardening landed (the [IMAGE] card above), and the job `Container Security` runs on every pull request against `main` (`.github/workflows/production-ci.yml:7-10`, `:234-249`), but it is not a required check: `.github/rulesets/main.json:20-39` lists the required contexts, and none of its contexts is there. The four images pull their base from Docker Hub (`node:26.12.1-bookworm-slim`, the `FROM` of each `apps/*/Dockerfile`) with no retry and no mirror, so a Docker Hub outage or rate limit fails the job. **DoD:** the pull is retried or reads a mirror, with its red path shown; the `Container Security` contexts become required checks, and `.github/rulesets/main.json` mirrors them in the same pull request.
- [ ] **K8S-1** `[L]` **INFRA+CI** (roadmap §6.2, P2) — **Kubernetes deployment readiness.** `verified` 2026-10-28. The tree holds no orchestration configuration: no Kubernetes manifest, Helm chart or Kustomize overlay. The expensive half is already aligned: separate liveness and readiness probes (`/health/live` and `/health/ready`, `apps/api/src/health/healthRoutes.ts`), a graceful SIGTERM drain (`apps/api/src/index.ts`, `apps/workers/src/lib/gracefulShutdown.ts`), migrations run by their own script and never at boot, cross-pod cache state behind `CachePort` (fitness #14), structured logs on stdout, a Zod-typed configuration from the environment, the API and the workers as separate processes, and distroless non-root images published to `ghcr.io`. What is missing, one sub-item each when the row opens: (1) the manifests: Deployment, Service, Ingress, HPA, PDB, ConfigMap and Secret; (2) `resources.requests` and `limits` per container, without which neither the HPA nor sane scheduling works; (3) secret delivery (Kubernetes Secrets, or external or sealed secrets) instead of `.env`; (4) the migration as a Job or init container run before the rollout; (5) PostgreSQL and Redis as operators or managed services, since `docker-compose.yml` is for development; (6) a preStop hook and a readiness flip before draining, for zero-downtime rollouts; (7) GitOps delivery (Helm or Kustomize with Argo CD or Flux) and a `ServiceMonitor` instead of a static scrape. It also decides per-PR preview environments and their cleanup: the job `cleanup-preview-environments`, which pointed at an EKS cluster that never existed, left `cleanup.yml` with #658 (merge `b053ba58`, commit `7de9722e`). 🔗 dep: IMAGE-1. **DoD:** versioned manifests or charts with requests and limits, an HPA, secret delivery, the migration job, the stateful-dependency strategy and a deploy pipeline.
- [ ] **API-TYPES-1** `[M]` **API** (roadmap §3.1.b, P1) — **Route schemas complete enough that the generated API types carry value.** `verified` 2026-10-28. The generator and its CI drift gate landed (`scripts/generate-api-types.ts`, `openapi-ts.config.ts`, [`openapi-migration.md`](../architecture/openapi-migration.md)), but an operation is typed only when its route declares a response schema: 494 of the 497 operations in `packages/shared/src/api-generated/types.gen.ts` type every response as `unknown`, and only the three health routes are typed. It blocks the typed portals (API-TYPES-2). **DoD:** every route declares Zod schemas for its params, query, body and response, so each operation is typed in `types.gen.ts`, migrated area by area with the drift gate green.
- [ ] **API-TYPES-2** `[S]` **ADM+CLI** (roadmap §3.1.c) — **The portals consume the generated types.** `verified` 2026-10-28. No file of `apps/admin` or `apps/client` imports `packages/shared/src/api-generated/` (its only importer outside the package is a test, `apps/api/tests/unit/security/httpRateLimitRuleCoverage.test.ts`), so a renamed response field breaks a portal at run time instead of at typecheck. It is the third clause of the roadmap item's original DoD. 🔗 dep: API-TYPES-1 (at least three endpoints with typed responses). **DoD:** typed clients in both portals consume at least three endpoints through the generated types.
- [ ] **PROV-SANDBOX-1** `[M]` **PKG+CI** (roadmap §3.2.c and §3.2.d, P1) — **Real provider sandboxes, run nightly, with an alert.** `verified` 2026-10-28. The provider tests run against mocks and recorded handlers, so a breaking change upstream is found in production; N-TEST-1 declares the real sandbox contracts a gap it does not cover (`docs/development/TESTING_REFOUNDATION.md` §Declared gaps, "Real provider sandbox contracts (11 providers)"). No sandbox test exists: the one example, the template's `packages/providers/_template/tests/integration/sandbox.template.test.ts`, was quarantined (no runner collected it) and left with WU-2.6a on 2026-10-29 (`PR d26a`); no provider has a `sandbox.test.ts`, and no workflow runs one. The runbook for a failing contract exists (`docs/runbooks/provider-contract-failure.md`). Stripe and Paddle are outside this row: §5.9 runs them in their sandboxes (BILL-6, BILL-24). **DoD:** a sandbox app per provider, provisioned by the Maintainer outside the repository, with its credentials in GitHub Actions secrets; real assertions in each provider's `sandbox.test.ts` (§3.2.c); a nightly workflow that runs them and alerts on a failure (§3.2.d).
- [ ] **GDPR-1** `[M]` **API+WRK+PKG(core)** (roadmap §4.3.b, P2) — **The data subject access export is generated, not typed in.** `verified` 2026-10-28. A request's `exportUrl` is whatever the operator sends (`apps/api/src/compliance/complianceRoutes.ts:153`, `complianceSchemas.ts:76`); nothing serializes the requester's data. **DoD:** a background job serializes every tenant-scoped table for the request's account, uploads the dump to object storage and sets `exportUrl` with a 7-day expiry, with the end-to-end test that the roadmap's phase A1 left out because the export did not exist.
- [ ] **GDPR-2** `[M]` **API+INFRA** (roadmap §4.3.c, P2) — **Event-store retention, and a record of what an account deletion emptied.** `verified` 2026-10-28. The outbox half is closed in code: `OutboxCleaner` deletes, every hour, each published outbox event created more than 7 days earlier (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/outbox/OutboxCleaner.ts:47-56`, started at `apps/api/src/index.ts:901`). `StoredEvent` has no retention: nothing outside the tests deletes from it. An account's hard delete leaves a `DeletionRecord` tombstone since #209 (merge `2efe20fd`), which today carries the deletion's reason, lawful basis and author, but the rows beneath the account still go through the database's foreign-key cascade, with no record of which tables it emptied. **DoD:** a retention window for `StoredEvent` (the roadmap proposed 14 to 30 days after processing) enforced by a scheduled task; an account deletion cascade that records the tables it emptied; `docs/compliance/RETENTION_CALENDAR.md` updated.
- [ ] **GDPR-3** `[M]` **API+PKG(core)** (roadmap §4.3.d, P2) — **Personal data masked in audit details, and breach reporting to a regulator.** `verified` 2026-10-28. `AuditLog.details` (the roadmap called it `metadata`) stores what its writers pass, e-mail addresses included (`apps/api/src/admin/AccountSessionService.ts:92-93`, `apps/api/src/admin/accountLifecycleService.ts:123-125`), with no redaction; the only sanitizer is in `apps/api/src/security/auditLogger.ts`, dead code that leaves with card C4 (§5.13), whose audit-retention intention ADR-0041 records here. `DataBreachReport.regulatoryReportedAt` (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma:3378`) is set by no route and no use case. **DoD:** a redaction pass on the audit write path, with a test per masked field; a per-jurisdiction submission path that sets `regulatoryReportedAt`. It also owns the audit-log retention ceiling: a tenant may shorten `auditLogRetentionDays` to 30 days, and only the request schema's maximum of 3650 bounds it (`apps/api/src/compliance/complianceSchemas.ts:17`); no ceiling tied to the regulation exists (`docs/compliance/RETENTION_CALENDAR.md` §Jurisdictional matrix).
- [ ] **QUEUE-SPLIT-1** `[L]` **PKG** (roadmap §5.2, P2, DEFERRED) — **Split `@adapters/queue-bullmq` into job types, a client and a worker base.** `verified` 2026-10-28. The package mixes the three responsibilities (`packages/adapters/queue-bullmq/src`, seven files, 867 lines), which a common monorepo layout keeps in three packages. Deferred while its trigger has not fired: there is one worker process (`apps/workers/src/bootstrap.ts`), and three packages would triple the boilerplate with nothing gained. **Trigger:** a second worker process. **DoD when it runs:** three packages, `apps/api` consuming the client, `apps/workers` the worker base, and the job types a leaf dependency of both.
- [ ] **DELETION-RESIDUE-1** `[S]` **INFRA+ADM** (roadmap §7.1, P2) — **What `project-deletion-integrity` adjudicated out of its scope.** `verified` 2026-10-28, except (2). (1) Foreign-key edges without a covering index: owned by SMELL-89, now FK-CASCADE-INDEX (§5.1), which counted 31 left after its slice (a count FK-CASCADE-INDEX re-measures: the migration it rested on never reached `main`), with the query that re-derives the list (the change's own count was 24, of which it closed 2). (2) The Admin retention dropdown offers 1 to 7 only, a product decision rather than a defect; `pending-review`: no such dropdown was found in `apps/admin` or `apps/client` on 2026-10-28, and the only bound of 1 to 7 is `DELETION_RECORD_RETENTION_YEARS`, an environment variable (`apps/api/src/config/env.ts:369`). (3) Email squatting that blocked a restore is **closed**: the customer registration refuses an address held by a deleted account, answering as it does for any address in use and e-mailing the owner (`packages/core/customer-auth/src/RegisterCustomerUseCase.ts:5-7`, `:307-310`; #629, merge `a26b8cc8`). (4) `Account_slug_key` is still a total unique index (`infra/prisma/migrations/00000000000000_baseline/migration.sql:2182`), not partial on `deletedAt IS NULL` as `Account_email_key` and `Project_accountId_name_key` are since migration `20260901120000_deletion_record_retention_and_partial_uniques` (`migration.sql:95-102`), so a soft-deleted account keeps its slug reserved. **DoD:** (2) decided by the Product Owner and recorded here; (4) a partial unique index, with the restore path answering a slug collision as it answers a name collision; (1) closes with SMELL-89, table by table.

**§4.2.b — alerts and dashboards over fed sources** (added 2026-10-05; the Maintainer's decision on card C6, amended by findings F5 and F6 of the §5.13 mapping): §4.2.b is built on series that some source feeds —ApiMetrics once the metrics plugin runs for every route (DC-1), the workers' metrics and those of the live breaker of `external-apis`— and never on instruments without samples. Today three of the four provisioned rules and the SLO availability query read series that nobody exports (DEF-34), and the business dashboards read recorders with no producer (DEF-35); the rows below make the live series real.

- [ ] **OBS-1** `[S]` **API+INFRA** — **Saturation of the API process and dependency health as fed series, with alerts.** `verified` 2026-10-05. The API does not register the process's default metrics (only the workers do: `apps/workers/src/publishWorker.ts:149`) and its gauges of memory, active connections, health and queue depth have no producer (`apiMetrics.ts:338-356`, setters without callers); the dashboards of `grafana/dashboards/system-resources.json` and the "Availability" alert that §4.2.b plans depend on them. **DoD:** the process's default metrics in the API; the health of the registered checkers as a series; heap/RSS, event-loop lag and availability alerts (with the real job label, see DEF-34), each one with a runbook. It picks up the intention of `monitoring/performanceMonitor.ts` (an alert for memory > 85 % and aggregated health; it ran without a reader) and of `health/healthMetrics.ts` (never registered), both from 2026-03, which leave with cards C1 and C6 (§5.13).
- [ ] **OBS-2** `[M]` **API+ADM** — **An "application behavior" view in Admin.** `pending-review`. The Maintainer's decision of 2026-10-05 (card C1): Admin reads Prometheus through the API, with PromQL over its HTTP API, and Prometheus is never exposed to the tenants. Today the operator only has Grafana (`grafana/dashboards/*.json`). **DoD:** permissioned Admin routes that return latency, errors and traffic per route, saturation, active alerts and limiter decisions (AUTH-RL-1); a page in Admin; no response mixes tenants' data. It picks up `PerformanceMonitor.getDashboardData` (it ran without a reader) and the `/admin/rate-limiting/*` routes of `rateLimitingDashboard.ts:123-215` (never registered), 2026-03 code that leaves with cards C1 and C4 (§5.13). 🔗 dep: DEF-34, DEF-35.
- [ ] **OBS-3** `[M]` **API+WRK** — **Technical observability per tenant.** `pending-review`. No HTTP series and no span carries the account (`rg 'setAttribute\("(tenant|account)'` = 0 in API and workers); only `tenantHealth.ts` emits gauges with `tenant_id` (`packages/monitoring/health-checks/src/tenantHealth.ts:22-40`). **DoD:** an `account_id` label only on chosen series of bounded cardinality; an `account.id` attribute on the API and workers spans; a per-account query in OBS-2. It complements `tenant-health-idor-01` (§5.1) and STOR-13. It picks up the intention of `PerformanceMonitor` according to the Product Owner (behavior per customer) and of `correlationTracking.ts` (`tenantId` in the spans), 2026-03 code that leaves with cards C1 and C4 (§5.13).
- [ ] **OBS-4** `[S]` **INFRA** — **Alerts and dashboards over the providers' live series.** `verified` 2026-10-05. The breaker of `packages/adapters/external-apis/src/circuitBreaker.ts:244-341` exports requests, failures, timeouts, state changes (`from_state`/`to_state`) and duration, and the workers measure the duration per provider (`apps/workers/src/metrics/workerMetrics.ts:203`); no rule and no dashboard queries them. **DoD:** alerts of an open breaker, error rate and latency per provider, and consumption of the provider's quota where its API reports it; a dashboard in Grafana; a runbook. The breaker's state is an alert signal, not a readiness one. It picks up the intention of `@monitoring/circuit-breaker` (four rules: open, error rate, slowness, recovered) and of `CircuitBreakerHealthChecker`, 2026-03 code that never received a single sample and leaves with card C6 (§5.13).
- [ ] **OBS-5** `[S]` **INFRA** (roadmap §4.2.b, P1) — **The six alerts the SLOs name, each with a runbook.** `verified` 2026-10-28. Phase A1 of the roadmap's §4.2 delivered four alerts with their runbooks and `docs/observability/SLO.md`; the rule files now hold sixteen alerts (`prometheus/alerts/api.yml`, `outbox.yml`, `saga.yml`), and none of them is one of the six the SLOs leave as TBD: availability, saga completion rate, saga average duration, outbox publish latency, post-creation p99 and publish success rate. Each is built over a fed series, by the rule above, so each waits on the source of its metric. 🔗 dep: OBS-1 (availability), DEF-34, DEF-35. **DoD:** the six rules, each over a series a source feeds and each with its runbook under `docs/runbooks/`, and the TBD cells of `SLO.md` filled.

Every rule is evaluated and none is delivered: the `alerting:` block of `prometheus/prometheus.yml:14-18` is commented out, and `docs/deployment/DEPLOYMENT_CHECKLIST.md` lists the routing among its open gaps; the nightly chaos run opens a GitHub issue on a failure (`.github/workflows/chaos.yml`), and nothing pages anyone. It is design work, not a one-line wire: (1) an Alertmanager for development against a managed one in production; (2) the Slack, e-mail or PagerDuty webhook secrets; (3) a Sentry API token and its alert rules. It picks up the channels that `performanceMonitor.ts` and `rateLimitingDashboard.ts` only simulated, and the Sentry alert chain the roadmap planned for the chaos and provider-sandbox runs (§4.1, §3.2.d). **DoD:** Alertmanager wired with routing to Slack, e-mail or PagerDuty, and Sentry alert rules created through its API.

Decide before writing it (cross-ref N-DOC-2). (b) An **audit pass** over the existing statements: each normative sentence of the 4 canon children + the JSDoc of `packages/ports/**` **+ `FEATURE_TRACE_MATRIX.md` and `MULTI_TENANT_GUARDS.md`** (two of the seven false statements live there and would stay outside a scope limited to canon+ports) either names its test/fitness, or is rewritten as an intention. **DoD:** ADR accepted; the 6 false statements already identified corrected (§0.2 4.c lists them); a fitness or script that detects normative statements without declared backing, with a **measured baseline** (if a clean hard-zero is not possible, the approximation and its false-positive profile are documented, instead of a rule that nobody can enforce).
- **GUARD-NESTED-INCLUDES** `[M]` **INFRA/P0-security** (**= `SMELL-59` structural-hardening candidate, 2026-07-15 — promoted from a diff-gate suggestion to a change of its own**) — **The Prisma guard does not cover nested relation reads.** `tenantGuardCheck` receives **one** `model` and mutates only `args.where` (`infra/prisma/src/extensions/tenantGuard.ts:186-219`); Prisma's extensions intercept **top-level** operations, so an `include`/`select` travels uninspected **even if that model is enrolled** — `project` is, and it leaked anyway through the `include` of `GET /accounts` (N-AUTH-1, which deleted that route on 2026-10-18; the doctrine gap remains). **`MULTI_TENANT_GUARDS.md` never mentions nested `include`s and `tenantGuard.test.ts` does not have a single case.** It is a **doctrine** gap, not a route's: it affects every `include` of an enrolled model in the repo. A high-priority change of its own (the Product Owner's decision): a sweep of every `include`/`select`, a test case in `tenantGuard.test.ts`, and the correction of the canon. Its natural input is N-SEC-3.b's total registry (which tenant predicate applies to each target relation). 🔗 dep: N-SEC-3.b.

### 5.3 Forgotten-features — backend ready, UI/wire absent (inventory §4)

They map to product — cheap value: **WEBHOOK-INGEST** (SMELL-38, a complete HMAC pipeline unwired, P1) · **FN-024** scheduled-reports cron (FN-021: its queue constant, `REPORT_GENERATION`, has no producer or consumer and leaves with DEF-172) · **SMELL-3** repurpose approve/reject · **SMELL-13/14** inbox priority/direction · **SMELL-30** admin SSE proxy buffering · **SMELL-45/46** threading/saga UI · **SMELL-9/27/41/51** (classify/wire) · **FN-022/023/025** video/predictive/AI-quality (NEEDS_PRODUCT_OWNER; FN-022 is answered when the Product Owner and the Maintainer approve card C8 of §5.13: its intention —splitting a long video into story segments— already lives in F3-WRK-4 and F3-API-10). **admin-logs-gitignored-01** (`confirmed-adversarial`, HIGH): the `/logs` page is covered by `.gitignore:51` → 404 on a clean build; anchor the rule + `git add -f`.

Clusters: analytics-client (4: `/analytics/posts/:id`, `/analytics/channels/:id` nonexistent + `/analytics/posts/best-times` and `/analytics/content/media-performance` with a **prefix mismatch** — the backend exposes them WITHOUT `/analytics`); posts-client media/thread (2); scheduling rules PATCH/toggle (2); referral page (the use cases exist, the route does not; BILL-30 absorbs it, §5.9); template item-level `/templates/:id`; admin mfa `regenerate-backup-codes`, rbac per-permission DELETE, and **the whole CRUD of the admin's webhook-subscriptions** (POST/PUT/DELETE to phantom routes). **DoD:** for each one, either create the missing endpoint or fix the FE call (the prefix mismatches are a trivial fix); a test that exercises the contract.
- **CONTENT-VERSION-UI** `[S]` **CLI** (added 2026-10-05, intention of C9) — the version history of a post in the portal: timeline, comparison, detail and restoration over the live backend (`GET/POST /content/versions/:postId`, `POST /content/versions/:postId/restore/:versionId` and `/content/versions/compare`, `apps/api/src/content/contentRoutes.ts:179-228`; `ContentVersionManager` and `VersionController` registered through DI). `FEATURE_TRACE_MATRIX.md`, row `Content version control`, presents it as a differentiator and it had no task. **DoD:** the user sees and restores a version from the post's detail; a component test and a contract test. The March 2026 UI (`packages/ui/src/components/business/ContentVersioning.tsx` and 8 more files, with no importers and no tests) leaves with card C9 (§5.13); it is rebuilt in `apps/client` against the current contract.

- [ ] **PUB-QUEUE-1** `[M]` **API+CLI** **P1** (added 2026-10-17 from the client portal backlog, [`docs/development/CLIENT_BACKLOG.md`](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/f0f38adc34958966dcdb7718f682235f6399c709/docs/development/CLIENT_BACKLOG.md), which was deleted from the repository that day; git history keeps it) — `verified` 2026-10-17: the customer has no view of their publishing work in progress. The portal's old `/dashboard/queue` page called `/admin/queue/*`, which requires `requireAdminAuth` and `SYSTEM_MONITOR` (`apps/api/src/admin/queueRoutes.ts:268-308`), and it was retired; today the portal only filters posts by status (`apps/client/app/[locale]/dashboard/posts/components/PostsFilters.tsx`), without the per-channel status or the reason of a failure, and the API has no route of the customer's publishing jobs. Until this lands, the portal's sidebar still links `/dashboard/queue` (`apps/client/app/[locale]/dashboard/layout.tsx:91`), which answers 404 since the page left (assessment `client-nav-dead-routes-01`). **DoD:** a customer route, scoped to their account and filterable by project, lists their pending, in-progress, failed and completed publications per channel, with the scheduled date, a preview and the readable reason of a failure, read from N-COR-8's per-channel record and never from BullMQ; a portal page groups them by status and retries a failed channel with N-COR-8's retry; an integration test of the route (a customer does not see another account's jobs) and a component test. The queues' raw statistics, job deletion and other accounts' jobs remain Admin's. 🔗 dep:N-COR-8.
- **SETTINGS-HUB** `[S]` **CLI** (added 2026-10-05, intention of C12) — the user menu links `/dashboard/settings` (`apps/client/app/[locale]/dashboard/layout.tsx:170`), which has no `page.tsx`; of the 11 settings sections, 6 have no link anywhere (`crm`, `integrations`, `referral`, `sso`, `team`, `usage`; `ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` F20). **DoD:** an index page that links the current sections; the menu link stops giving 404.
- **ADM-NOTIF-1** `[M]` **API+ADM** (added 2026-10-06, decision of PREG-10) — **Admin notification center.** Today Admin only has toasts (`apps/admin/lib/stores/notificationStore.ts` re-exports the shared notification store, which holds the bell's list, unread count and SSE state, `packages/shared/src/stores/notificationStore.ts:1-5`; only its own test imports it (`apps/admin/lib/stores/__tests__/notificationStore.test.ts:8`), and it leaves with card C12) and every notifications route requires `requireClientAuth` (`apps/api/src/notifications/notificationRoutes.ts:407-477`); `ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` F7 counts it as the largest customer→admin gap. **DoD:** the Admin twin of the notifications module (SSE, bell, read and attended state, preferences per operator), fed by operator events —backlogged queues, publishing failures above a threshold, authentication failures with the providers, billing and gateway events, the tenants' health— from the live series and the outbox, never from the instruments without data that card C6 deletes; it closes question 4 of `ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` §7. It does not include a cross-tenant content library: staff see a tenant's content only with the grant of SUPPORT-ACCESS-1 (§5.1). 🔗 related: OBS-4, AUTH-OBS-1.
- **NAME-DIGEST-LOOKUP** `[M]` **API+ADM** (backlog SMELL-107, LOW, `verified` 2026-10-29) — `verifyNameDigest` (`apps/api/src/security/nameDigest/nameDigest.ts:152`) answers "was this person deleted?" over degraded tombstones and has no caller outside tests. The Product Owner decides whether a DSAR or dispute surface needs it. **DoD:** the decision recorded; if built, an Admin-only route with its own authorization, a rate limit and an audit row per lookup, tested with the composed app; if not, the verifier enters the dead-code ledger.
- **ORPHAN-ISSUED-TOKENS** `[M]` **API+CLI** (backlog SMELL-112, HIGH, `verified` 2026-10-29) — two tokens are issued and never claimed. The team invitation stores `inviteToken` and mails `/accept-invitation?token=…` (`packages/core/team/src/InviteTeamMemberUseCase.ts:79-95`, `:182`), but no route uses `findByInviteToken` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaCustomerUserRepository.ts:242`) or `CustomerUser.acceptInvitation` (`packages/core/domain/src/entities/CustomerUser.ts:459`), and no portal has that page, so an invited member cannot join. The public `POST /compliance/dsar` (`apps/api/src/compliance/complianceRoutes.ts:228`) stores a `verificationToken` (`packages/core/compliance/src/ComplianceService.ts:498`) that is never sent or checked, so a request is filed for any email without proof of identity. The Product Owner decides, per token, whether its flow is built or its issuance removed. **DoD:** the decision recorded per token; an invitation acceptance built only after the invite hierarchy check of the ordering constraint in §1.N.E and the hashed tokens of CRED-15c, tested end to end; a DSAR verification step (mailed token, claimed once) or the column removed.
- **CRM-ADAPTERS-WIRE** `[S]` **API+PKG** (backlog FN-047, MEDIUM, `verified` 2026-10-29) — the two finished CRM adapters (`packages/adapters/crm-hubspot/src/HubSpotAdapter.ts:18`, `packages/adapters/crm-salesforce/src/SalesforceAdapter.ts:18`) are in no app's manifest and nothing constructs them, although `SyncCrmContactsUseCase` already takes a `CrmAdapter` (`packages/core/crm/src/SyncCrmContactsUseCase.ts:22`). `triggerSync` (`apps/api/src/crm/crmRoutes.ts:112`), behind `POST /crm/:platform/sync` (`:214`), always answers 501 "CRM adapter not configured" (`:128`). The portal's sync button calls that route (`apps/client/components/settings/crm/CrmConnectionCard.tsx:41,62`; `apps/client/hooks/api/useCrm.ts:69-74`). The Product Owner decides whether CRM sync ships. How the CRM connection is authorized stays an open point of ADR-0034. **DoD:** both adapters are registered in the composition root under a platform-keyed token; the route resolves the platform's adapter and runs the sync, and the 501 is gone; one integration test per platform runs against a stubbed CRM API. If the Product Owner retires CRM sync instead, the two packages, the route and the portal button leave together. 🔗 dep: CRED-15b (the tokens are encrypted before any sync reads them).

### 5.3.b Security self-service surface (customer) — backend live, UI absent

> **New subsection (2026-08-16).** §5.3 has the right shape ("backend ready, UI/wire absent") and **none** of these in its inventory. Source: `docs/architecture/ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md`. It scales as **FE-only** work — but **after** N-SEC-6 and N-AUTH-2 repair the backends underneath, not before.

- **MFA self-service (customer)** — 5 live endpoints, **zero** UI (`rg 'auth/mfa' apps/client` = 0 hits). Admin does have its own.
- **Forgot / reset password** — the page **does not exist in any portal**; `apps/client/.../login/page.tsx:118` links `/forgot-password` and that route dir is not there. In admin it is worse than recorded: the **confirm** page exists but **not the request one**, and `POST /admin/auth/password/reset` has **zero** consumers → an admin cannot trigger the email whose token their own page consumes. The client's methods also call the wrong paths: `requestPasswordReset` posts `{ email }` to `/auth/customer/reset-password`, the confirm endpoint (`apps/client/lib/auth/authApi.ts:282`, `apps/api/src/auth/customerAuthRoutes.ts:682`), and `resetPassword` posts to `/auth/customer/reset-password/confirm`, which does not exist (`authApi.ts:292`); neither method has a caller outside tests (backlog SMELL-96). 🔗 dep: N-SEC-6.
- **Change password while authenticated (customer)** — **the endpoint does not exist**; the portal calls `POST /auth/customer/change-password` and receives 404. Admin has the complete stack. 🔗 dep: N-SEC-6.
- **Activity / audit viewer** — a backend on both sides, a consumer on neither; the admin hooks (`useAuditLogs`/`useAuditStats`) are imported only by each other and their docblock points to a `/logs` page that is not in the tree, while `SidebarNav.tsx:88` publishes the link. 🔗 dep: N-AUTH-2.
- **API-key CRUD · analytics export · session management · profile editing** — the same pattern, a total or partial backend with no surface.
- **Restore a deleted account before login (customer)** — **does not exist** (neither backend nor UI). `POST /accounts/:accountId/restore` (`apps/api/src/accounts/accountRoutes.ts:781`) accepts the owner only with a client token that has `account:delete`, and `LoginCustomerUseCase` does not let the user of a deleted account in (`packages/core/customer-auth/src/LoginCustomerUseCase.ts:186-208`): the owner cannot get that token, so today only the admin with `account:manage` restores. A pre-login path is missing, for example a restoration link verified by e-mail. **The Product Owner's decision (2026-10-16, unit C-P1b):** a registration with the e-mail of a deleted account responds with the same `409 EMAIL_EXISTS` as a live account and sends an e-mail notice to the owner of the address, which today refers them to support; when this flow exists, the notice adds its link.
- **Definitive deletion by the customer themselves (customer)** — **does not exist** (neither backend nor UI). `DELETE /accounts/:accountId/hard` (`apps/api/src/accounts/accountRoutes.ts:791`) is admin-only: `requireAdminAuth`, `account:manage` and a written reason. What is missing is for the owner to complete the deletion of their deleted account, with the warning that it deletes all the account's data and cannot be undone. **The Product Owner's decision (2026-10-16, unit C-P1b):** the same e-mail notice as the previous item; when this flow exists, the notice adds its link.

> **`admin-logs-gitignored-01` (§5.3) — closed:** `.gitignore:56` anchors the rule as `/logs/` and `apps/admin/app/[locale]/(dashboard)/logs/page.tsx` is tracked since `bd7be8fe` (#197, merge `fdfc375e`, 2026-08-25). ⚠️ **A tool gotcha kept from it:** `fd -H` shows hidden files but **still respects `.gitignore`** — to see an ignored file you need `fd -H -I`. A "does not exist" derived from `fd -H` over an ignored path is a false negative.

- **CQRSIntegration dead-route** `[S]` (P-VERIFY-2 bonus) — **RESOLVED:** `apps/api/src/cqrs/CQRSIntegration.ts` was confirmed DEAD_CODE (never wired in any composition root) and was removed from the repo (branch `workstream/cqrs-integration-purge`; merged to `main` via **PR #128**, 2026-07-19, +30/−2358); the pending classification is closed. In the same PR the `statements` coverage floor was re-baselined 55→54 (authorized). FN-009 (optimizedPostsRoutes) is still open: it is part of card C11 of §5.13, which waits for the approval of the Product Owner and the Maintainer. FN-010/027 (rateLimitingDashboard) **RESOLVED 2026-10-05 by card C4** (the Maintainer's decision): the file leaves with unit DC-4 of §5.13; the limiter's telemetry and alerts live in AUTH-RL-1 (§5.1), its Admin screen in OBS-2 and the routing of notifications in OBS-6 (§5.2, roadmap §4.2.c). ⚠️ **Naming note:** the campaign's fact sheet labeled #128 as "N-SEC-3", but in this plan and in the repo's commits `N-SEC-3` is the guard for `projectId`-only models (§1.A, **IN PROGRESS**), NOT the CQRSIntegration purge. They are distinct items; do not confuse them.

FN-033, FN-034 and FN-035 are closed (§7 ledger). · FN-043 ROI calculation: `apps/api/src/infrastructure/adapters/ROICalculatorAdapter.ts:82-96` calls `ROICalculator.calculateROI` and then recomputes the investment total and the ROI ratio itself from the result's cost breakdown, beside the pipeline `apps/api/src/analytics/roiCalculator.ts` delegates to (`roi/ROIMetrics.ts` and its siblings); the inventory counted three calculation sites in July 2026 and ordered the legacy ones deleted, and the finding never entered the backlog (measured 2026-10-29). One computation, in the pipeline, with the adapter mapping only.

"Admin123!"` (a weak fallback, CWE-798); its fix plan died with `REMEDIATION_ROADMAP.md`. **DoD:** fail-fast without a fallback (the env.ts Zod pattern); + fix the dead ref of `T0A_SECRETS_ROTATION_RUNBOOK.md`. ### 5.8 Deferred product capabilities (rescued P-GATE-2, 2026-06-29)

> DEFER decisions of `feature-decisions.md` (March) without an heir in the living spine. Detail: the branch's P-GATE-2 results (a work artifact, not kept; base evidence: [the 2026-06-29 assessment](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/b87b76ac80fbb971ac4c5733e94327d79c02d972/docs/audits/FULL_REPO_ASSESSMENT_2026-06-29.md)).

- **INDUSTRY-BENCHMARKS** (D7) — DEFER: benchmarking against the industry average; it requires a base of >1000 accounts OR a data license. An activation trigger, not an immediate task. (Distinct from F2-API-6 competitor-benchmarking, which is per-tenant.)
- **INTEGRATION-MARKETPLACE** (D13) — DEFER: an integrations marketplace; premature until 10+ integrations (today ~6). Trigger "10+ integrations".
- **DROPBOX-IMPORT** (D9, minor) — DEFER: today only Google Drive (matrix Level 4 🟡). Low effort when it is prioritized.
- **TEMPLATE-COLLAB** (added 2026-10-05, intention of C9) — DEFER: collaborators per template with a role, permissions and invitation (multi-user editing with conflict resolution, `docs/features/templates.md:47`). Activation trigger: per-workspace roles wired (`FEATURE_TRACE_MATRIX.md`, row `Per-project members with granular roles`) and a customer who asks for it. The March 2026 `TemplateCollaboration` table never had reads or writes and leaves with card C9 (§5.13).

### 5.9 Payments and media storage — decisions of the Product Owner and the Maintainer (2026-10-04)

> **ℹ️ CARD [PAYMENTS-STORAGE] — decisions on payment gateways and media storage, written down (2026-10-04).**
> **Status: decided and documented; nothing implemented. Queued: it does not start until N-TEST-1 (Testing Refoundation) and the legal register (§5.10), which goes first, have closed; its exact place is fixed at that moment (the Product Owner, 2026-10-05: "we already have a marked route, let's not deviate").** On 2026-10-04 the Product Owner and the Maintainer closed, after five research reports, the decisions on the payment gateways (Stripe and Paddle) and on media storage. They are recorded in six ADRs and two specifications so that they do not have to be asked again. That same night, after a code map and a sixth report, the Product Owner also closed the pricing model and the domain vocabulary (ADR-0030 and ADR-0031), which are integrated into the payments specification. The slices below are the pending work; each one is a PR of about 400 lines as a planning heuristic, with its tests and its documentation, and its DoD is that of its row in the specification plus the §0.2 bar.
>
> **Decisions:** [ADR-0024](../technical/ADR-0024-billing-currency-and-price-catalog.md) to [ADR-0031](../technical/ADR-0031-domain-vocabulary.md) hold each decision, the points resolved on 2026-10-04 and 2026-10-05, and the initial values, with their research sources; the open points are the "Open points" section of [billing-gateways.md](../features/billing-gateways.md) and, for the unit of the AI pool, decision (1) of AI-QUOTA-1 (§5.1); the specifications are [billing-gateways.md](../features/billing-gateways.md) and [media-storage.md](../features/media-storage.md). The findings F13 = SMELL-184 (the measured mechanism of N-COR-4, §1, N.B), F14 = SMELL-185, F15 = SMELL-186, F16 = SMELL-187 and SMELL-188 to SMELL-191 are carried by the rows below; SMELL-192 and SMELL-193 by the slices of [billing-gateways.md](../features/billing-gateways.md) alone.

**Payment slices** (detail and dependencies in [billing-gateways.md](../features/billing-gateways.md) §10):

- [ ] **BILL-1** **PKG** — `PaymentEvent` in the `PaymentAdapter` port + a Stripe translator tested with a real `2026-03-25.dahlia` payload. ADR-0026.
- [ ] **BILL-2** **PKG** — a Paddle translator with a real payload. 🔗 dep:BILL-1.
- [ ] **BILL-3** **API+PKG** — the webhook route passes the provider and the service receives the typed event; only native event ids. It closes F13/SMELL-184, N-COR-4 and L-6. 🔗 dep:BILL-2.
- [ ] **BILL-4** **INFRA+API** — `Invoice` to integer minor units (schema and data migration). ADR-0024. 🔗 dep:BILL-3.
- [ ] **BILL-5** **API+ADM** — billing reads the configuration saved in Admin; the gateways' environment variables, the undeclared `STRIPE_PRICE_*` and `PADDLE_PRICE_*` variables and the two copies of `buildPriceMap` leave. It closes F14/SMELL-185. ADR-0025.
- [ ] **BILL-6** **PKG+ADM** — synchronization of the computed amounts with Stripe and Paddle: each price cohort in the organization's currency, with the term as the interval, through synchronized or inline prices as decided after the sandbox; Admin's price ids become read-only. It changed on 2026-10-04: the catalog and its screens moved to BILL-18 and BILL-19. ADR-0024/0030. 🔗 dep:BILL-4, BILL-5, BILL-18.
- [ ] **BILL-7** **ADM+PKG** — country→currency and country→gateway maps, country of establishment and the European notice. ADR-0024/0025/0027. 🔗 dep:BILL-6, BILL-19.
- [ ] **BILL-8** **CLI+API** — a billing profile in the checkout and routing by country; the client's gateway selector is retired; the checkout sends the quote's lines (one per cohort, with quantity and term) instead of only `{ gatewayProvider }`. It closes F16/SMELL-187 together with BILL-6 and BILL-22. ADR-0025/0030. 🔗 dep:BILL-7, BILL-28.
- [ ] **BILL-9** **API+CLI** — the access state machine, a single guard with its error code, banners and a pay-only screen. ADR-0026. 🔗 dep:BILL-3.
