A synchronous verdict model (ClamAV) and an asynchronous one (cloud scanners that tag the object after the upload): the media carries `scan: pending | clean | infected | error`, publishing waits for `clean` and an infected object is deleted or quarantined with an audit row and a message for the user; the switch `MALWARE_SCANNER` is validated at startup (`clamav` by default; `none` is rejected outside tests). Never VirusTotal's public API: its terms forbid commercial use. The unit reviews whether ADR-0029, which covers type, size, format and duration and names no antimalware control, needs an amendment; if a cloud scanner is activated, LEGAL-1 records it as a processor. 🔗 dep:STOR-9.
- [ ] **STOR-15** **PKG+CI** — cloud scanning adapters (added on 2026-10-06, PREG-7), written now from the official documentation and marked **UNCERTIFIED against the live service** in their header and in this row until their first live run in green: AWS GuardDuty Malware Protection for S3 (asynchronous: object tags and EventBridge toward the verdict job) and Microsoft Defender for Storage (asynchronous: blob tags and Event Grid); Google Cloud has no native object scanner, so its path is the ClamAV adapter, as in Google's reference architecture. A single contract suite: real against ClamAV, with the SDK mocked for the cloud adapters and a live batch under its own tier, which fails without credentials and is declared in `docs/development/TESTING_REFOUNDATION.md` (the pattern of CRED-9a to CRED-10, §5.14). 🔗 dep:STOR-14.

### 5.10 Legal facts register — immediately after N-TEST-1 (2026-10-05)

> **ℹ️ CARD [LEGAL-REGISTER] — first in the queue: it starts immediately after N-TEST-1 closes, before §5.9 and §5.11. Under way since 2026-10-07, after the closure of phase 0 of N-TEST-1 (2026-10-06), with the method below. The mechanics slice (L-0 to L-3) has been on `main` since that same day (#459 to #473): LEGAL-2, LEGAL-3 and LEGAL-4 closed and the skeleton of LEGAL-1; next come LEGAL-1's decision sessions, the retro pass by batches and LEGAL-5.**
> **The register, its place on the route and its method** — approved on 2026-10-04, placed on 2026-10-05 exactly after the Testing Refoundation, and the method of 2026-10-07 shared with §5.15: mechanics first, the present taken by the generators, one retro pass guided by the ledger below, the qualitative columns "pending decision" until the Product Owner's sessions, and the sequence L-0, L-1, L-2, L-3, the retro pass, LEGAL-5 — are [ADR-0042](../technical/ADR-0042-legal-facts-register-method.md). §5.15 follows it on the route, sharing the form of its gate (LEGAL-3 ↔ SUP-3) and the edits of the templates (LEGAL-2 ↔ SUP-2).
>
> - [~] **LEGAL-1** — the register, anchored in the GDPR record of processing activities (art. 30): personal data, purposes, legal bases and retention; subprocessors; OAuth permissions and data brought in over the network; security measures (for the credentials, ADR-0034, with its stance on a breach); commercial rules (ADR-0024 to ADR-0030). It also inventories the purposes with their legal basis, on which the destiny of `ConsentRecord` depends (`schema.prisma:3322-3336`, today with no reads or writes, with an entry in the guard and an RLS policy), which LEGAL-5 resolves (card C7 of §5.13; PREG-6, decided on 2026-10-06). Its qualitative columns —purposes, legal bases, retention periods, frameworks beyond the GDPR and the language of the register— stay "pending decision" until the Product Owner's sessions (ADR-0042, rule 4). **Progress 2026-10-07 (#465):** the register's skeleton, `docs/legal/REGISTER.md`, with the 13 sections of GDPR art. 30, each one `generated`, `linked` or `pending decision (Product Owner)`, and `docs/legal/README.md`. Waiting for the decision sessions: the pending sections, the frameworks beyond the GDPR, retention, the language of the register and 17 `pending` rows of the inventories (16 schema fields and one subprocessors dependency), which fitness #45 holds exact.
> - [x] **LEGAL-2** — a `## Legal impact` section in the ADR and specification templates; closed 2026-10-07 by #473 (merge `2fd9d33d`).
> - [x] **LEGAL-3** — the CI gate on legally relevant surfaces, `pnpm check:legal` as fitness #45; closed 2026-10-07 by #472 (merge `7b49a3b0`).
> - [x] **LEGAL-4** — the four inventories generated from the code, and their initial load; closed 2026-10-07 by #461 to #469 (merges `bb439662` to `f317a6e4`) and #471 (`24e56a53`); findings DEF-52 to DEF-62 (§5.11), registered by #460.
> - [ ] **LEGAL-5** — the acceptance evidence and the destiny of `ConsentRecord` (added on 2026-10-06, decision of PREG-6): (a) a versioned record of the acceptance of the terms, of the privacy notice and of the DPA —who, which version, when and from where— as contractual evidence, separate from any consent; (b) once LEGAL-1 has inventoried the purposes, `ConsentRecord` stays only for the purposes whose legal basis is consent (candidate: marketing email to individual creators), with the evidence the EDPB asks for (the text and version shown, the moment, the mechanism and the session; guidelines 05/2020, ¶108) and a withdrawal as easy as the opt-in; if there is none, it is deleted with its entry in the guard and its policy, and its row of card C7 (§5.13) is updated; (c) the sign-up never asks for a consent in the same click as the acceptance of the terms (¶81). 🔗 dep:LEGAL-1.
>

>
> The ledger grows by state, not by position in the phase: an item enters when it reaches `[x]` and its change touches data or credentials, whatever section it comes from (Leveling, product base, Pre-phase verdicts, defects or §5.x items); items of pure process or documentation (templates, gates, plan edits) do not enter (ADR-0042 decision 3, 2026-10-07), and the rule also applies to the rows already listed. An open item whose already merged slices touch credentials, personal data, OAuth, consent, retention or a third party also enters, as a "(partial)" row, so that what was published does not stay unread; its row is completed with the item's closing PR. An item that closes with the LEGAL-3 gate already on `main` enters with its two columns marked by its own closing PR, which the gate requires to document it (ADR-0042, rule 3).
>
> A row is marked in "Legal" only when its facts are in `docs/legal/`, and in "Support" only when its page is in `docs/support/`; an item without legal facts or without a support surface is marked with a declared not-applicable, with its reason, in the batch's PR. That declaration belongs to the ledger and is read by whoever reviews the batch, not by a gate: LEGAL-3's not-applicable lives in the inventories' classification files. The "Batch" column carries the number of the PR of the retro batch that marks the row, or that of the closing PR for an item closed with the gate already on `main`.
>
> From the Pre-phase, the verdicts whose text names personal data, credentials, OAuth, consent, retention or a third-party service enter: 8 verdicts in 4 rows (P-GATE-1, P-GATE-2 and six P.B documents grouped in two rows). Left out are 128 of the 136 verdicts that their blocks add up to (P.A 14, P.B 36, P.C 1, P.D 72, P.E 9 and P.F 4): the 14 of P.A, 30 of the 36 of P.B, the one of P.C, the 72 of P.D, the 9 of P.E, P-VERIFY-1 and P-VERIFY-2 (14 + 30 + 1 + 72 + 9 + 2 = 128) decide the destiny of a document or of an engram memory, or measure cycles and endpoints, without naming any of those surfaces; P.A-12 names authentication only as a false claim of the document that was deleted.

### 5.11 Defects found on 2026-10-04 — queued after N-TEST-1

> **ℹ️ CARD [DEFECTS-2026-10-04] — decided (item 3 of 2026-10-04); they do not start until N-TEST-1 closes.**
> The ones the payments and storage decisions absorb (F2, F3, F11) live in §5.9. These are fixed as decided:
>
> - [ ] **DEF-1** — F10: the Paddle webhook route wraps everything in a single `try` and answers 400 "Invalid signature" to any error; an invalid signature (400) is separated from a processing error (500, so the gateway retries), each with its own log entry.
> - [ ] **DEF-2** — an unconfigured gateway or OAuth provider answers 500 (`getAdapter` outside the `try`); it must answer 404/503 "not configured".
> - [ ] **DEF-3** — F8: Facebook's `client_secret` travels in the query of a URL (`providerOAuthConfigs.ts:295`, `facebook/src/apiClient.ts:237,252`); it moves to the body if Meta accepts it and, in any case, that URL never reaches a log.
> - [ ] **DEF-4** — Paddle's SDK compares the signature with `===`: OmniPost verifies the signature with `crypto.timingSafeEqual` and the SDK only parses the event; the issue for PaddleHQ is drafted and shown to the Maintainer before it is published.
> - [ ] **DEF-5** — fitness #16 does not see `process.env[...]` reads with brackets (12 price IDs): it is widened with its red path proven (SMELL-190).
> - [x] **DEF-6** — `any` on 3 lines of `quality/scripts/bundle-analyzer.ts`; closed 2026-10-11, when the file was deleted with DEF-84, by #553 (merge `47230884`).
>
> **Added on 2026-10-05** — found while sizing the §5.12 route migration (ADR-0033) and while verifying its findings, read statically, not run. They stay queued as findings: their fix is decided when they are taken, and those that live in §5.12 code name the area that moves it.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-7** — two customer routes read a principal their guard never sets: `apps/api/src/auth/apiKeyRoutes.ts:86` and `apps/api/src/custom-reports/customReportRoutes.ts:333` take the `accountId` from `request.user`, but their preHandler `requireClientAuth` only sets `request.customerUser` (`auth/customerAuthMiddleware.ts:59`; the only one that writes `request.user` is `auth/integrationAuthMiddleware.ts:175`). The `accountId` is `undefined` for every customer and every call answers 401. Neither the portals nor the tests use those routes, which is why nothing failed. Verified statically, not run. Same code as ARCH-17 and ARCH-18. SMELL-194. The `apiKeyRoutes.ts` half closes by deletion with DC-13 (§5.13, decision D4 of 2026-10-06); the `customReportRoutes.ts` half remains.
> - [ ] **DEF-8** — the account update from Admin stores the email unnormalized: `apps/api/src/admin/AnalyticsAccountHandlers.ts:64-84` checks uniqueness and writes the value as it arrives, and its schema (`admin/analyticsSchemas.ts:48`) does not normalize it, while account creation does (`accounts/accountRoutes.ts:154`). An email that differs only in case passes the route's uniqueness check and is stored that way. Verified statically, not run. Same code as ARCH-5 (AA-5). SMELL-195.
> - [ ] **DEF-9** — the OIDC PKCE state lives in process memory: `apps/api/src/auth/oidcRoutes.ts:63` keeps it in a module `Map`, and its own comment (`:59-60`) says that in production it must go in Redis or in encrypted cookies. With more than one pod, a login that starts on one and comes back to the callback through another does not find the state and fails. Fitness #14 does not see it: it only looks for class fields `private *Cache = new Map()`. Verified statically, not run. Same code as ARCH-17. SMELL-196. Fix decided on 2026-10-06 (ADR-0034, decision 7): the state moves to Redis with the `OAuthFlowStore` pattern and an atomic consume, CRED-3 (§5.14); the credentials inventory flagged it again and it has no row of its own.
> - [ ] **DEF-10** — updating notification preferences is not atomic: `apps/api/src/notifications/notificationRoutes.ts:291-293` runs one `upsert` per preference outside any Unit of Work, and a failure midway leaves the first ones applied and not the following ones. Verified statically, not run. Same code as ARCH-14. SMELL-197.
> - [ ] **DEF-11** — the onboarding GET writes: `apps/api/src/onboarding/onboardingRoutes.ts:37-44` creates the record if it does not exist, and completing a step (`:103-120`) runs two statements without a transaction. Verified statically, not run. Same code as ARCH-10. SMELL-198.
> - [ ] **DEF-12** — `TrendAnalysisService` receives `PrismaClient` and never uses it (`apps/api/src/trends/trendAnalysisService.ts:55-60`, no `this.prisma`), and it is the only reason `trendRoutes.ts:348` resolves `PrismaClient` from the container. In addition, `getTrendingContent` and `generateTrendPredictions` return `[]` behind a module circuit breaker (`:82`, `:153`), and the five endpoints of `trendRoutes.ts` have no references in the portals. Whether the service and those endpoints stay is the Product Owner's decision, under the §0.3 taxonomy. Verified statically, not run. Same code as ARCH-19. SMELL-199 and SMELL-18. In addition, `GET /trends/viral` (`apps/api/src/trends/trendRoutes.ts:366-374`) returns the same invented analysis for any `contentId`: `analyzeViralContent` builds fixed `viralMetrics`, `viralDNA`, `replicationBlueprint` and `competitorResponse` values (`apps/api/src/trends/trendAnalysisService.ts:186-274`, e.g. `viralCoefficient: 2.8` at `:201`). A tenant who calls it receives invented figures presented as a measurement. Backlog FN-020.
> - [ ] **DEF-13** — the Admin password-reset link falls back to `http://localhost:3100` when its base URL is missing: `apps/api/src/admin/adminUserRoutes.ts:398` and `apps/api/src/admin/auth/adminAuthRoutes.ts:263` evaluate `(adminUrlResult.ok && adminUrlResult.value) || "http://localhost:3100"` over `PlatformCredentialService.getCredential("PLATFORM", "adminUrl")`, a platform credential configured in Admin (`packages/core/settings/src/credentialKeys.ts:54`), not an environment variable. The link goes out the same way if the credential is not loaded, if it is empty or if its read or its decryption fails, so a store error stays hidden, and the email reaches the administrator with a link to `localhost` instead of the application. It is the class the environment canon forbids for `env` (SECURITY_CANON §Secrets and Environment: a typed value that fails fast, never `X || "fallback"`), here over a stored configuration that reaches users; fitness #15 only looks at `env` fallback values with a secret's name and does not see it. Verified statically, not run. Same code as ARCH-6. SMELL-200.
> - [ ] **DEF-14** — the CORS origin list the API uses is fixed to `localhost`: `apps/api/src/index.ts:391-400` passes `SecurityManager` six origins (`http` and `https` on ports 3000, 3100 and 3200) with `allowCredentials: true` (`:403`), and `SecurityManager.register` hands them as they are to `@fastify/cors` (`apps/api/src/security/securityHeaders.ts:149-155`). No environment value reaches that list and no branch depends on the environment, so every deployment, production included, admits credentialed calls from a browser only for those six `localhost` origins, and any other origin receives the response without `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`. The portals do not notice because they call the API from their servers (in Admin, a Server Action and a server layout; in both, the proxy route `app/api/backend/[...path]/route.ts`). It is the same class as DEF-13: a URL configuration fixed in code, with no typed environment value. Outside the §5.12 areas. Verified statically, not run. SMELL-201.
> - [ ] **DEF-15** — dead security configuration that reads as live: `apps/api/src/security/securityHeaders.ts:400` exports `SecurityConfigs`, whose `production` preset allows credentialed CORS (`:438`) to `env.CLIENT_URL || "https://app.yourapp.com"` and `env.ADMIN_URL || "https://admin.yourapp.com"` (`:435-436`); both variables are optional (`apps/api/src/config/env.ts:162-163`), so without them the preset would open credentialed CORS to the `yourapp.com` hosts. Nothing in `src` uses it (the API builds its own configuration in `index.ts:374`, DEF-14); only two unit suites that pin its values import it (`apps/api/tests/unit/securityHeaders.test.ts:137`, `apps/api/tests/unit/securityHeaders.mutations.test.ts:505`), and that is why it looks like tested configuration. In the same file, `handleCorsOrigin` (`:168`) has no callers: `register` hands the array to `@fastify/cors` (`:149-155`), so its matching of `*` and `*.domain` never runs. Whether the preset and that method stay is the Maintainer's decision, under the §0.3 taxonomy. Outside the §5.12 areas. Verified statically, not run. SMELL-202.

> - [ ] **DEF-16** — the API scheduler loses the context of its errors: `apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/setupServices.ts:163` passes it `createLogger("scheduler")`, a pino logger, as `SchedulerLogger`, whose `error(message, data)` puts the message first (`packages/observability/background-scheduler/src/port.ts:17-21`). Pino reads `(message, object)` as a message plus a format argument and drops the object when the message has no specifier, so the error of a scheduled task is logged without `taskId` or `err` (`packages/observability/background-scheduler/src/default-scheduler.ts:139-142`, `:168-171` and `:175-178`; also the `info` at `:144`). Measured with the repository's pino (10.3.1): that call writes only the message. The workers wrap theirs in pino's order (`apps/workers/src/publishWorker.ts:134-140`, `apps/workers/src/bootstrap.ts:74-80`). It is the risk class that decision D-R10 of ADR-0033 closed for the core; the rest, verified statically.
> - [ ] **DEF-17** — the data-retention sweep records as successful a sweep that failed: `packages/core/compliance/src/DataRetentionService.ts:52-53` turns each port's failure into `0` (`deletedAuditResult.ok ? deletedAuditResult.value : 0`, and the same for the expired DSAR requests), and the `DATA_RETENTION_CLEANUP` audit entry that closes the sweep is emitted with `success: true` (`:56-62`). A sweep that could not delete reads as a clean one with zero rows, and no WARN or ERROR records it: the service logs no failure and the daily task discards its result (`apps/api/src/index.ts:974-981`). In addition, a failure to read the GDPR settings is taken as automatic deletion turned off and returns zeros without auditing anything (`:37-41`). Item (v) only removed the closing `info` line. Verified statically, not run.

The daily task discards both results (`apps/api/src/index.ts:963-964`), so no failure reaches a log. Verified statically, not run.
> - [ ] **DEF-19** — eight Redis connections of the composition root discard their errors without logging them, with an empty `redis.on("error", () => {})`: `apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/setupServices.ts:604` (the real-time webhook broadcaster), `:706`, `:725` and `:743` (the three content sync services), `:806` (the notification broadcaster) and `:822` (the analytics broadcaster); `apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/setupBillingUseCases.ts:57` (the gateway-switch job service), and `apps/api/src/index.ts:922` (the connection of the gateway-switch processor). While one of those connections fails, nothing logs it; CODING_STANDARDS §Error Handling admits no empty `catch`, and an empty error handler is its equivalent. The two at `setupServices.ts:849` and `:870` stay out of the count: their comment says where each failure goes (the fail-open path of the brute-force adapter and the `err("STORE_ERROR")` of the MFA challenge store). No production code resolves the token today, but whoever resolves it will receive `ok` with zero affected subscriptions while neither the price nor the history is saved. Same code as BILL-19 (§5.9), which decides whether to resolve it or delete it; SMELL-191 (4) records that nobody resolves it, without naming the empty adapters. Verified statically, not run.
> - [ ] **DEF-21** — the SSE stream of the Admin webhook dashboard opens CORS to any origin and subscribes with a key the events do not carry: `apps/api/src/webhooks/webhookDashboardRoutes.ts:205-211` opens the response with `reply.raw.writeHead` and `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` (`:209`), unlike the origin list the API hands to `@fastify/cors` (DEF-14); since the route requires `requireAdminAuth` and `WEBHOOK_MANAGE` (`:387-392`), a foreign origin gets no events without the credential. In addition, it subscribes to the broadcaster with the administrator's id as `accountId` (`:199`, the `sub` of their token according to `apps/api/src/admin/auth/adminAuthMiddleware.ts:127-129`), and the broadcaster delivers to the subscriptions whose key is the event's `accountId` (`apps/api/src/webhooks/realtimeWebhookBroadcaster.ts:393-398`), so the stream would only receive the events of an account whose id matched the administrator's. Verified statically, not run. Same code as ARCH-20.
> - [ ] **DEF-22** — the database health probe reads every account: `packages/monitoring/health-checks/src/checkers/database.ts:18` calls `listAccounts()`, which in `packages/adapters/db-prisma/src/AccountRepository.ts:197-201` is a `findMany` with no `where`, no `take` and no `deletedAt` filter, and maps every row. The probe runs every 30 s from the health manager (`apps/api/src/health/healthRoutes.ts:47`, `:59-62`) and on every call to `/health/detailed` and to `/health/ready` (`:138`, `:232`), so its cost grows with the number of accounts, and its latency can cross the thresholds of `database.ts:33-39`, which mark the database as degraded or down because of volume, not connection. The read without `deletedAt` is one of the eleven that the ratchet of fitness #38 holds over `db-prisma` (SMELL-87); what belongs to this finding is the probe. Verified statically, not run.

Whoever reads the dashboard takes a response time for a TTL. Verified statically, not run.

The operation reports as warmed precisely the keys that are still missing. Verified statically, not run.

It is also a second BullMQ `Queue` for the same queue, with its own connection, beside the adapter the root already obtains from `queueRegistry.forQueue(QUEUE_NAMES.PUBLISH)` (`index.ts:351`); the two are closed in different places, Admin's in an `onClose` (`:368-375`) and the registry's at shutdown (`:1353-1354`). > **Added on 2026-10-05 from CI** — DEF-27, observed in a run of `Integration Tests`, and DEF-28, found while reading the code it pointed at; they are registered in this queue and wait their turn, like the previous ones.

> - [x] **DEF-27** — the click-count integration test was intermittent; closed 2026-10-14 by #574 (merge `b5773184`).
> - [ ] **DEF-28** — recording a click and its GA4 event can fail without anything logging it: `RedirectAndTrackClickUseCase` fires `recordClick` with `void` (`packages/core/links/src/RedirectAndTrackClickUseCase.ts:65`; its comment at `:64` justifies it by performance) and the event with `void this.ga4?.trackEvent(...)` (`:68`; the one at `:67`, because a failure must not hold up the redirect), and observes neither result. The port declares `recordClick` as `Promise<Result<void, Error>>` (`packages/core/domain/src/repositories/TrackedLinkRepository.ts:67`, not in `packages/ports`), and the Prisma adapter does the write —the `linkClick.create` and the increment of `trackedLink.clicks`, in a transaction (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaTrackedLinkRepository.ts:215-237`)— inside a `try` that turns any failure into `err(...)` (`:211`, `:240-242`): what the `void` discards is an error `Result` already resolved, not a rejected promise, and a click that was not saved is lost without a log line. In GA4 the loss is also in the wiring: its adapter always returns `ok` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/adapters/GA4TrackingAdapter.ts:99-100`), as its port asks (`packages/core/domain/src/repositories/GA4TrackingPort.ts:26-28`, `:35`), and logs its failures (`:89-97`) with a logger the root does not pass it (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/setupServices.ts:894`), so it uses the empty default (`GA4TrackingAdapter.ts:35-39`). It is the class of DEF-17 and DEF-18: a port failure turned into silence. Proposed fix: the redirect still does not wait, but both promises are observed; the use case receives a `LoggerPort` (`packages/ports/src/LoggerPort.ts`) that the composition root injects, as in the five services of ADR-0033, and logs a `warn` with `err` and `trackedLinkId` when `recordClick` returns an error or rejects, and the same for the GA4 event, which only stops being lost if the root also passes a logger to its adapter, because the adapter always returns `ok`; no `void` over an unobserved `Result`. The deterministic seam of DEF-27 and the observation point of DEF-28 are in the same place (`:65`), so a single change can close both. Verified statically, not run. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

> **Added on 2026-10-05 from the verification of F1** — DEF-29, confirmed at run time; unlike the previous ones, the Product Owner decided to fix it now as a unit of its own, outside the chain of slice 0.22, because it breaks publishing.
>
> - [~] **DEF-29** — the workers cannot decrypt the channel credentials the API writes: the API encrypts `Channel.credentials` with AES-256-GCM and binds `Channel.credentials\x1f<channelId>` as AAD (`apps/api/src/security/EncryptionService.ts:99-101` and `:152`; `apps/api/src/security/ChannelCredentialsCrypto.ts:40` and `:65-69`, called from `PrismaChannelRepository.save` `:348-351`), and the seed does the same (`infra/prisma/seed.ts:48-72`); the workers decrypt with `packages/shared/src/channelCredentialsCrypto.ts:79-102`, which never calls `setAAD` nor reads `credentialsKeyVersion`, through the callback of `packages/adapters/db-prisma/src/ChannelRepository.ts:83-90`, which receives the four envelope columns and not the channel id (`apps/workers/src/publishWorker.ts:127-132`, `apps/workers/src/mentionIngestWorker.ts:71-79`). Measured on 2026-10-05 with the real modules and a throwaway key: the envelope written by the API throws `Decryption failed: data may be tampered or key version is wrong` in the worker, and with the AAD it decrypts; the full path returns `err(DATABASE_ERROR)` in `getChannelsByIds` and `err(AUTH)` in `CredentialResolver.resolve`. In production every publish fails with `AUTH` (`apps/workers/src/publishHandler.ts:282-305`) and mention ingestion marks `needsReauth` on healthy channels (`apps/workers/src/lib/handleProviderAuthError.ts:28-37`); reconnecting encrypts with AAD again, so the failure repeats. The divergence starts in `1a48bbf3` (2026-05-03), whose JSDoc claims that the shared helper mirrors `EncryptionService` and that the API, the workers and the seed use it; neither was true from that commit on. The only test of the worker path with real encryption, `apps/api/tests/integration/publishWorkerTenantIsolation.integration.test.ts:122` and `:143-145`, encrypts and decrypts with the same helper without AAD, which is why it stayed green. Second latent defect: the helper ignores `credentialsKeyVersion`, so after a rotation the workers would have no previous key to look up. **PR 1 merged, PR 2 pending** (2026-10-06): the DEF-29 unit fixes it in two PRs. PR 1 —one canonical cipher with AAD and a key ring per version in the Node module of the shared core, the channel id in the adapter contract, the workers with `PLATFORM_ENCRYPTION_KEY_VERSION` and `_V<N>`— was merged into `main` on 2026-10-05 (#429, `8710327d`); PR 2 —`EncryptionService` and the seed delegate to it, a cross-process test, the `CREDENTIALS_UNREADABLE` code end to end and an opaque key ring— is pending, goes after unit B (DEF-37, merged on 2026-10-06 as #434 and #435) and its number is assigned when it is published. ADR-0034, which that PR was going to write in a narrow form ("one cipher for channel credentials"), was written on 2026-10-06 as the full credentials model (§5.14, #433): PR 2 cites it instead of writing it. The rows already written are not re-encrypted: they carry the AAD and the active version, and the fixed worker reads them. The fix only unblocks Bluesky: measured with the cipher of PR 1, the eight OAuth providers still do not publish because of DEF-36. The box is checked when PR 2 is on `main`.
>
> **Added on 2026-10-05 by the dead-code intention mapping (§5.13)** — DEF-30 to DEF-35, found while crossing cards C1 to C10 with the code that runs; they are not dead code. DEF-32 was confirmed at run time; the others were read statically. They wait their turn in this queue, like the previous ones.

> - [ ] **DEF-30** — the audit and metrics middlewares never run: `apps/api/src/index.ts:455-465` adds their hooks inside `typedApp.register(async function auditPlugin/metricsPlugin…)` without `fastify-plugin`, and Fastify applies those hooks only to the routes registered in that child, which has none; a burst of credential guessing leaves no audit row, although `audit/auditMiddleware.ts:63-90` would record `statusCode` and `success`. Only `apps/api/tests/unit/auditMiddleware.test.ts` calls the audit function, and it calls it directly, so no test noticed. It was noted on 2026-08-16 (`docs/features/auth-rate-limit-integrity/proposal.md:98`) and never entered this plan. The metrics half is repaired inside card C1 (the Maintainer's decision of 2026-10-05, unit DC-1 of §5.13); the audit half changes behavior (rows per request) and goes here. Proposed fix: the audit hook runs for every authenticated route, with a test over the composed app that proves it; the 401s and 403s of `requirePermission` are recorded (it picks up the intention of `auditPermissionAccess`, which leaves with card C2); volume and retention reviewed. Verified statically, not run. Same code as ARCH-21. **Finding while measuring DEF-70 (2026-10-09):** once it runs, its classification does not work either. `getActionFromRequest` (`audit/auditMiddleware.ts:112-171`) decides the action with prefixes over the raw `request.url`, the class of DEF-68, and not over the route pattern: the `/publish/` branch (`:161`) matches no registered route, and `POST_CREATED` (`:156`) requires that a path starting with `/posts` contain no `/`, so it is never chosen. `apps/api/tests/unit/auditMiddleware.test.ts:248-258` calls the function with `/publish/123` and only checks that a record exists. The fix of DEF-30 includes classifying by `request.routeOptions.url` and method, with a test that requires every branch to match a registered route, like the one of DEF-70. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-31** — Instagram does not publish stories or reels: `detectContentType` (`packages/providers/instagram/src/contentHelpers.ts:23-33`) only returns `FEED` or `CAROUSEL` since the first commit (`5603d6be`), so `publishStory` and `publishReel` (`InstagramAdapter.ts:342`, `:373`) are never reached from `publish()` (`:284-299`). The suite `InstagramAdapter.stories-reels.test.ts:77-110` asserts on its own fixtures, not on the adapter, and `docs/features/provider-capabilities.md:22-23`, `FEATURE_TRACE_MATRIX.md`, Level 6 row `Native publishing per network`, and `INVESTOR_EN.md:63` declare Instagram stories and reels. On the Facebook side, the adapter decides story or reel by metadata (`FacebookAdapter.ts:182-215`) that no producer in `apps/` or `packages/core` writes, so its end-to-end reach is not verified. Proposed fix: the routing of `publish()` tells story and reel apart, and the test exercises the real adapter instead of its fixtures; the producer of that metadata arrives with F3-API-10, which depends on this defect. Verified statically, not run. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-32** — analytics ingestion persists nothing for any of the 11 networks and marks healthy channels for reconnection. Confirmed at run time on 2026-10-05 on `main` `20c4e9ea`, with a read-only harness (the real use case and adapters, mocked API clients and repositories, no database): `IngestChannelAnalyticsUseCase` reads `metrics` as an array of daily rows, but Facebook, Instagram, LinkedIn, Pinterest, Snapchat, TikTok, X and YouTube return an object (`FacebookAdapter.ts:472`, `InstagramAdapter.ts:550`, `LinkedInAdapter.ts:342`, `PinterestAdapter.ts:330`, `SnapchatAdapter.ts:318`, `TikTokAdapter.ts:387`, `XAdapter.ts:424`, `YouTubeAdapter.ts:281`), so `metrics.map` (`packages/core/analytics/src/IngestChannelAnalyticsUseCase.ts:144`) throws inside the transaction, before `upsertDailySummaries`, and the use case answers `INTERNAL_ERROR` (`:181-188`) without writing a row; Telegram returns no `metrics` and ends in `ok` with zero rows, without a warning; Threads returns an array, but its `postId` is the Threads media id while `AnalyticsDailySummary.postId` is a foreign key to `Post.id` (migration `20260502010535`), so it would fail with P2003 (read in the code); Bluesky does not implement `fetchAnalytics` (`ANALYTICS_NOT_SUPPORTED`). In production that step is not even reached: `PrismaChannelRepository.toDomain` passes the credentials through `parseCredentials` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaChannelRepository.ts:53-61`), which keeps only `accessToken`, `refreshToken`, `expiresAt`, `tokenType` and `scope`; the `REQUIRED_FIELDS` of each adapter requires a field of its own network (`pageId`, `userId`, `personUrn`, `boardId`, `clientId`, `botToken`, `clientKey`, `apiKey`…), `validateCredentialStructure` fails with `err("AUTH")`, the use case answers `AUTH_ERROR`, `apps/api/src/analytics/analyticsIngestConsumer.ts:97` marks `needsReauth` ("Provider rejected credentials during analytics ingestion") and throws (`:107`), and BullMQ retries three times: every channel of the 10 networks with `fetchAnalytics` is marked for reconnection every 6 h and nothing is persisted; on reconnecting, `PrismaChannelRepository.save` (`:333-347`) re-encrypts only the five generic fields. That chain was read in the code for ingestion; the same projection was confirmed at run time in DEF-37 and DEF-38. The readers that see nothing: custom reports, repurpose detection, the top-performers context and the trend score; `AnalyticsMonthlySummary` is read and never written. No test joins the real output of an adapter or the credentials the repository loads with the use case: the unit test feeds a hand-made array with a mocked adapter and channel (`apps/api/tests/unit/application/ingestChannelAnalytics.test.ts:44-49`). The shapes never matched (the use case was born in `8e5f8f5a`, on 2026-03-30, expecting an array; Facebook returns an object since `5603d6be`); the projection is from `5603d6be`, the dispatcher from `7679830d` (2026-05-11) and the in-process consumer from `ff675659` (2026-05-24). Root of the contract: the port returns `unknown` (`packages/ports/src/ProviderAdapter.ts:162-165`), and F2-API-12 types it. Its two leads were confirmed at run time the same day: the comment use cases receive the same projected credentials (DEF-38), and the OAuth connection flow saves only generic fields, so it does not publish either (DEF-36). Proposed fix: the projection keeps each network's own fields, the `fetchAnalytics` contract is typed (F2-API-12) and an integration test joins the real adapter, the credentials the repository loads and the use case; a projection defect never marks `needsReauth`. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-33** — disconnecting a channel does not revoke the token at the network: `DELETE /channels/:channelId` only sets `deletedAt` (`apps/api/src/channels/channelRoutes.ts:410-430`) and no path calls the provider's revocation; the only revocation written, TikTok's (`packages/providers/tiktok/src/authService.ts:371`), is unreachable and leaves with card C8 (§5.13). The connection canon asks for "scoped/revocable" tokens (`FEATURE_TRACE_MATRIX.md`, row `Connect without passwords`); it is a fact for LEGAL-1 (OAuth permissions and the data fetched per network). Proposed fix: disconnecting calls the revocation of each network whose API offers it, records the result and declares the networks that do not offer it. Verified statically, not run. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-34** — three of the four alerts that phase A1 of the normalization roadmap's §4.2 delivered ([its last version](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/3758be3ee3d4274bcc01582b437080732f50d11d/docs/architecture/NORMALIZATION_ROADMAP.md)) cannot fire: `ApiLatencyP99High` (`prometheus/alerts/api.yml:8`) queries `http_duration_bucket{job="api"}` and `ApiErrorRateHigh` (`:20`) `http_requests_total{status=…}`, while the API exports `api_http_request_duration_seconds` and `api_http_requests_total` with the label `status_code` (`apps/api/src/metrics/apiMetrics.ts:108-118`) and the scrape job is named `api-server` (`prometheus/prometheus.yml:37`); `OutboxLagHigh` (`prometheus/alerts/outbox.yml:10`) queries `outbox_pending_events`, which no registry exports. `docs/observability/SLO.md:25-27,70` (`up{job="api"}` included) and the runbooks `alert-api-latency.md:42`, `alert-api-error-rate.md:44` and `alert-outbox-lag.md:8,61` repeat the names, and there is no rules test (`rg promtool .github scripts` = 0). Proposed fix: rules, SLO and runbooks over series that exist; the outbox relay publishes its backlog; a rules test (`promtool test rules`) in CI with its red path proven. Verified statically, not run: it can be confirmed with a `count(http_duration_bucket)` query against the live Prometheus. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-35** — the provisioned dashboards read series nobody feeds: in `apps/api/src/metrics/apiMetrics.ts`, `recordBusinessMetric` (`middleware/metricsMiddleware.ts:140`: posts, media, threads, previews), the recorders of provider calls, errors and health, of database operations and connections, of memory, active connections, queue health and depth, of cache, storage and queues, of the limiter and of validation errors have no caller in production; every panel of `grafana/dashboards/business-metrics.json` and part of `system-resources.json` (`process_cpu_*`: the API never calls `collectDefaultMetrics`, only `apps/workers/src/publishWorker.ts:149` does) and of `api-performance.json` stay empty, while the live series of the breaker (`packages/adapters/external-apis/src/circuitBreaker.ts:244-341`) and of the workers (`apps/workers/src/metrics/workerMetrics.ts:195,203`) have no panel or rule. The OpenTelemetry Prometheus exporter listens on `:9464` (`packages/observability/opentelemetry/src/index.ts:108-110`) and `prometheus/prometheus.yml` does not scrape it, so the OTel-only instruments (for example `content_published_total`) do not arrive. Proposed fix: every panel and every rule names a fed series (it gets fed or it is deleted), with a check that fails if a panel or a rule names a series no registry declares, with its red path proven. Verified statically, not run. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

> **Added on 2026-10-05 while verifying the leads of DEF-32 at run time** — DEF-36 to DEF-38 share one cause: the channel credentials model. Application keys are asked for per channel instead of coming from the configuration, the per-channel ids (page, board, organization) are not discovered on connecting and a repository trims the credentials to five generic fields. Writer and reader diverge since `1a48bbf3` (2026-05-03), the same commit as DEF-29, and the OAuth flow saves three generic fields since `f9d64a40` (2026-05-07); no test joins a row written by the OAuth flow with a real adapter.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-36** — channels connected by OAuth cannot publish: the OAuth callback of the 8 OAuth providers stores in `Channel.credentials` only `{accessToken, refreshToken?, expiresAt}` (`apps/api/src/auth/providerOAuthFlow.ts:193-197`); the provider's id goes only to the `providerAccountId` column (`:202`), there is no page, board or organization selection, and reconnecting (`:216-225`) writes the same three fields; `POST /channels` and `PUT /channels/:channelId` keep only the five generic fields with `mapCredentials` (`apps/api/src/channels/channelRoutes.ts:73-81`). Each adapter requires in `REQUIRED_FIELDS` application keys or per-channel ids that nobody stores (X: `apiKey`, `apiSecret` and `bearerToken`, `XAdapter.ts:43`; Facebook: `pageId`, `appId` and `appSecret`, `:45`; Instagram: `userId`, `:52`; YouTube: `clientId`, `clientSecret` and `channelId`, `:45`; TikTok: `clientKey`, `clientSecret` and `openId`, `:46`; LinkedIn: `personUrn`, `:44`; Pinterest: `boardId`, `:46`; Snapchat: `clientId`, `clientSecret` and `organizationId`, `:40`), and the workers' `CredentialResolver` (`apps/workers/src/services/CredentialResolver.ts:58`) hands over the decrypted JSON without merging `providerAccountId` or the Admin `SOCIAL_*` keys (`packages/core/settings/src/credentialKeys.ts:60-70`, which nobody reads). Confirmed at run time on 2026-10-05 with the real publish handler and the real adapters over mocked HTTP: with the DEF-29 encryption applied, the 8 OAuth providers end in `PublishLog` ERR `AUTH` with zero calls to the provider; only Bluesky publishes, and the seed channel `dev-x` fails with `AUTH`; Telegram and Threads have no connection writer (enum of `providerOAuthFlow.ts:32`, configurations of `providerOAuthConfigs.ts:644-646`). Mention ingestion marks `needsReauth` on the Facebook, Instagram and X channels. Proposed fix: the connection flows store the per-channel fields each adapter requires, discovered on connecting; the application keys come from the configuration when credentials are resolved; the repository stops trimming; a credentials contract typed per provider replaces the `REQUIRED_FIELDS` lists; contract tests join a real row of the OAuth flow with the real adapter. It is the first slice of the §2F workstream (write path per provider, [`docs/archive/PENDING_WORK_INVENTORY.md` §2F](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/cb4921bda93a39e300f1bd5203985edde8055258/docs/archive/PENDING_WORK_INVENTORY.md#L198), which was deleted from the repository on 2026-10-17 and git history keeps), not a one-night unit. **Decided on 2026-10-05 (option B):** the surgical part (DEF-37) goes now as a unit of its own, the credentials model went into ADR-0034 (2026-10-06) and this redesign is slice 1 of §2F, CRED-14 (§5.14), which waits for DEF-37 and CRED-8a; before building, the Maintainer asked for the inventory of every credential in the environment and the database, so that the unit would not stay isolated from them.
> - [x] **DEF-37** — saving a Bluesky channel destroyed its credentials; closed 2026-10-06 by #434 (merge `e0b2cee0`) and #435 (merge `5a65dba0`).
> - [ ] **DEF-38** — comment sync and reply do not work: with the same credential projection, `SyncProviderCommentsUseCase` returns `FORBIDDEN` for LinkedIn and the consumer marks `needsReauth` in every 30-minute cycle, on every channel not deleted (`processInboxSyncJob` → `UpdateChannelAuthState`); for Facebook, Instagram, X and YouTube the sync does nothing and reports success, because the use case never passes `postExternalId` and the adapters return `[]` before calling; `SendReplyUseCase` fails with `AUTH` on the five providers that support replies (Facebook, Instagram, LinkedIn and YouTube before calling; X after an unauthenticated call), and Pinterest, TikTok and Snapchat do not support replies. Confirmed at run time with the real use cases (`packages/core/inbox/src/SyncProviderCommentsUseCase.ts`, `SendReplyUseCase.ts`) and mocked repositories. Proposed fix: it depends on DEF-36 (complete credentials) and adds the `postExternalId` in the sync, with per-provider tests over the real use case. It waits its turn with slice 1 of §2F, CRED-14 (§5.14).

> **Added on 2026-10-06 by the credentials inventory (§5.14), registered in #433** — DEF-39 to DEF-45, measured on `main` `8710327d` by reading the code, not run; the Maintainer decided the fix of each one with the credentials model (ADR-0034), and it lives in a row of §5.14 or, for DEF-44, of §5.13. The in-memory OIDC PKCE state, which the inventory also flags, was already DEF-9. They wait their turn in this queue, like the previous ones.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-39** — the Admin and customer TOTP seeds are stored in plain text: `AdminUser.mfaSecret` (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma:186`) and `CustomerUser.mfaSecret` (`:357`) are written as they come out of `authenticator.generateSecret()` (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/MfaService.ts:120-126`, through `apps/api/src/infrastructure/adapters/PrismaAdminMfaUserRepository.ts:71` and `PrismaCustomerMfaUserRepository.ts:76`) and are read to verify each code (`MfaService.ts:160-162`, `:209-211`). A read of the database hands over the second factor of every administrator and every user; NIST SP 800-63B-4 §3.1.4.2 asks that those shared keys be strongly protected against disclosure, and they cannot be hashed because verification needs them. Fix decided on 2026-10-06 (ADR-0034, decision 6): encrypt them with the single encryption seam and delete the plain-text column, CRED-15a (§5.14). Verified statically, not run.
> - [ ] **DEF-40** — the HubSpot and Salesforce OAuth tokens are stored in plain text: `CrmConnection.accessToken` and `refreshToken` (`schema.prisma:2853-2854`, `@db.Text`) are sent by the client in the request body (`apps/api/src/crm/crmRoutes.ts:35-36`, `:96-97`), written unencrypted by `apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaCrmConnectionRepository.ts:60-72` and read by `packages/core/crm/src/SyncCrmContactsUseCase.ts:70`; the API read does mask them (`packages/core/crm/src/GetCrmConnectionsQuery.ts:36-40`). It is the same class of secret as `Channel.credentials`, which is encrypted with AAD and a key version. There is no server-side code exchange either: the portal sends the tokens, and which application connects the CRM stays an open point of ADR-0034. Fix decided on 2026-10-06 (ADR-0034, decisions 6 and 11): encrypt them with the single encryption seam, with a write-only API, CRED-15b (§5.14). Verified statically, not run.
> - [ ] **DEF-41** — the password-reset and invitation tokens are stored in plain text and looked up by equality: `AdminUser.passwordResetToken` (`schema.prisma:183`, written by `apps/api/src/admin/auth/PasswordService.ts:192-203`, looked up and claimed at `:251-258` and `:322-330`), `CustomerUser.resetToken` (`:354`, written from `packages/core/customer-auth/src/RequestPasswordResetUseCase.ts:108` and claimed in `apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaCustomerUserRepository.ts:222-226`) and `CustomerUser.inviteToken` (`:367`, written from `packages/core/team/src/InviteTeamMemberUseCase.ts:79`). Whoever reads the database can use any valid token: a random token stored in clear and compared by equality is a password in clear (OWASP Forgot Password; NIST SP 800-63B-4 §3.1.2.2). The `"CHANGE_REQUIRED"` sentinel that `apps/api/src/admin/AccountSessionService.ts:79` writes into the same column (SMELL-110) first moves to a flag of its own. It shares the claim sites of `PasswordService` with N-SEC-6 (§1) and the consumption markers of fitness #41, which are renamed with the column. Fix decided on 2026-10-06 (ADR-0034, decision 6): `@unique` columns with the SHA-256 hash of the token, looked up by hash through a single `tokenHash()` helper, CRED-15c (§5.14). Verified statically, not run.
> - [ ] **DEF-42** — the workers and the provider packages log without redaction, and the API's list has gaps: `@observability/logger`, the logger of `apps/workers` and of the packages that use it (23 package files build their own `pino()` instead: LOGGER-FACTORY-PKGS, §5.1), is a `pino()` with no `redact` option (`packages/observability/logger/src/index.ts:30-38`), and the workers hold the decrypted credentials in memory; the API's `REDACT_PATHS` (`apps/api/src/lib/logger.ts:14-35`) does not include `appPassword` (Bluesky), `clientSecret`, `appSecret`, `botToken`, `secretKey`, `webhookSecret`, `mfaSecret`, `backupCodes`, `codeVerifier`, `webhookUrl`, `targetUrl` or `privateKey`, and its wildcards only cover `password`, `token`, `apiKey` and `secret` at one level (not `*.accessToken`, `*.refreshToken` or `*.credentials`). A sweep found no call today that logs a credentials object, but nothing prevents it. Fix decided on 2026-10-06 (ADR-0034, decision 8): one exported list that every logger applies, CRED-2 (§5.14). Verified statically, not run.
> - [ ] **DEF-43** — the keys of the platform AI pool are read from two sources: the Admin orchestrator takes `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `PERPLEXITY_API_KEY` and `GEMINI_API_KEY` from the environment (`apps/api/src/ai/orchestrator.ts:116-157`, for the health checks and the requests without an `accountId`), and customer requests read the `AI_POOL` group edited in Admin (`packages/core/ai/src/AiRequestService.ts:109`). An operator can change a key in Admin while the orchestrator keeps the one from the environment, or the other way round. Fix decided on 2026-10-06 (ADR-0034, decision 2): Admin is the single source and the three variables leave the environment, CRED-8b (§5.14). Verified statically, not run.
> - [ ] **DEF-44** — the API does not start without a dead key: `OAUTH_ENCRYPTION_KEY` is required in the environment schema (`apps/api/src/config/env.ts:119`, `z.string().min(SECRET_MIN)`) and no code reads it (`rg -n 'OAUTH_ENCRYPTION_KEY' apps packages` only finds the schema and the catalog); its table, `ProviderConnection`, was deleted with the migration `20260507222903_consolidate_channel_drop_provider_connection`. Every deployment has to generate and safeguard a secret that protects nothing, and `docs/security/SECRETS.md` and `secretCatalog.ts:75-79` describe it as the KEK of `ProviderConnection`. Fix: DC-3 deletes it (§5.13, card C3), already decided; ADR-0034 (decision 13) counts it among the dead secrets. Verified statically, not run.
> - [ ] **DEF-45** — key rotation exists only on paper: there is no re-encryption job, and the error at `apps/api/src/security/EncryptionService.ts:241` points the operator to a "re-wrap script" that does not exist; two rotation records drive nothing —`PlatformEncryptionKey` (`schema.prisma:3572`), which the Admin rotation endpoint fills with a new version and the instruction to edit `.env` and re-encrypt (`apps/api/src/settings/settingsRoutes.ts:156-175` → `SettingsService.logEncryptionKeyRotation`) without anything reading it to choose a key, and `SecretRotationLog` (`:290`), which has no run-time writer and was seeded by the migration `20260506193101_add_secret_rotation_log`, so the status page always shows the same date—; and the dual-key window for the JWT and cookie signing keys that the runbook prescribes (`docs/security/T0A_SECRETS_ROTATION_RUNBOOK.md`, "Dual-key validity windows") is not implemented: with no `kid` and no previous key, a rotation closes every session. Fix decided on 2026-10-06 (ADR-0034, decision 9): the re-encryption job with a single record (CRED-6) and the dual-key window (CRED-11), in §5.14. Verified statically, not run. SMELL-104.

> **Added on 2026-10-06 by the credentials inventory and by the reading of unit B, registered in #433** — DEF-47, the exposure CRED-12 fixes, and DEF-48, found while writing unit B (DEF-37, #434) and outside its scope; both read in the code, not run. They wait their turn in this queue, like the previous ones.

> - [ ] **DEF-47** — the external-notifications list returns the Slack and Teams webhook URLs decrypted: `GET /external-notifications` (`apps/api/src/external-notifications/externalNotificationRoutes.ts:215-218`, with `requireClientAuth`) answers with `ListExternalNotificationsQuery`, which projects `webhookUrl` as it comes out of the repository (`packages/core/external-notifications/src/ListExternalNotificationsQuery.ts:49`), and the repository decrypts it on reading (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaExternalNotificationConfigRepository.ts:166-176`). It is a capability URL: whoever has it posts in the customer's Slack or Teams channel. At rest it is encrypted (`ExternalNotificationConfig.webhookUrl*`, `infra/prisma/schema.prisma:2515-2518`, with AAD per row), but every read of the list hands it over whole, and `REDACT_PATHS` does not cover `webhookUrl` (DEF-42). It is the only measured violation of the write-only rule of ADR-0034 (decision 10): a secret is shown once, when it is created, and masked afterwards. Proposed fix: the list returns the presence or the masked URL, never the decrypted value, with a route test; it is CRED-12 (§5.14). Verified statically, not run.
> - [ ] **DEF-48** — updating a channel overwrites its lifecycle fields: `PUT /channels/:channelId` (`apps/api/src/channels/channelRoutes.ts:663-670`) reaches `updateChannel` (`:370`), which rebuilds the channel with `Channel.reconstitute` (`:388-401`) passing only project, account, provider, name, credentials, status, error count, last error, last check and dates, without `isPrimary`, `needsReauth`, `authFailedAt`, `authFailureReason`, `accountName`, `profileImage`, `connectedAt`, `expiredAt`, `lastUsedAt` or `providerAccountId`; the update branch of the upsert of `PrismaChannelRepository.save` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaChannelRepository.ts:380-395`) then writes them as `false` or `null`. Every rename or credentials change removes the primary-channel flag, erases a pending reauthentication flag, erases the expiry trail and the display data, and nulls `providerAccountId`, with which the OAuth callback finds the channel on reconnecting (`findByProjectProviderAccount`, `apps/api/src/auth/providerOAuthFlow.ts:205`): a reconnection after a rename creates a duplicate channel. Proposed fix: the handler loads the aggregate and applies its methods (`updateHandle` and `updateCredentials`, `packages/core/domain/src/entities/Channel.ts:368`, `:382`) instead of reconstituting it from the request, as the aggregates rule of ARCHITECTURE_CANON asks (state changes only through methods), with a route test that checks `isPrimary` and `providerAccountId` after a `PUT`; a unit of its own, ordered with slice 1 of §2F (CRED-14), because it is the same route family as DEF-36 and DEF-37. Verified statically, not run (engram 1335).

> - [x] **DEF-49** — no portal scanned `packages/ui` with Tailwind; fixed by #444 (merge `06fead38`), in `main` since 2026-10-06.
> - [ ] **DEF-50** — the Admin theme does not define the colors the shared components use: its `@theme inline` block (`apps/admin/app/globals.css:20`) defines `--color-muted-foreground`, but not `secondary`, `secondary-foreground`, `muted`, `popover`, `popover-foreground`, `card` or `card-foreground`, which the client does define (`apps/client/app/globals.css:32-48`), so Tailwind never generates in Admin the utilities that name them. Outside stories and tests, `packages/ui/src` uses `bg-secondary` and its variants 18 times, `bg-muted` and `border-muted` 12, `bg-popover` 5 and `bg-card` 4, plus `text-secondary-foreground` (2), `text-popover-foreground` (5) and `text-card-foreground` (1). Admin renders `@packages/ui` components that use them: the webhooks page (`apps/admin/app/[locale]/(dashboard)/webhooks/page.tsx:11-26`) imports `Tabs`, `Select` and `Card`, and their `TabsList` (`bg-muted`, `:177`), `SelectContent` (`bg-popover`, `:116`) and `Card` (`bg-card`, `:208`) are drawn without a background. > - [ ] **DEF-51** — `storybook build` of `apps/client` prints 58 warnings, the same in 10.6.0 and in 10.6.1 (measured on 2026-10-06): 48 Vite `MODULE_LEVEL_DIRECTIVE` for the `"use client"` directive of `packages/ui` components; 8 from react-docgen-typescript, which leaves `packages/ui` components and `.storybook/preview.tsx` out of the active TypeScript project ("not included in the active TypeScript project"), so their props are not documented by types (the autodocs pages no longer exist: `@storybook/addon-docs` left on 2026-10-25 with deps-remove-2); 1 "No story files found" for the glob `../components/**/*.stories.@(ts|tsx)` of `apps/client/.storybook/main.ts`, which today finds no file because no component of `apps/client/components` has its story beside it yet (that glob is the location ADR-0035 sets, and the warning goes away with the first story of the `apps/client` slice of the backfill); and 1 chunk-size warning. The same warnings come out in the smoke test (`storybook dev --smoke-test --ci`) and in `pnpm --filter @apps/client test:stories`. No gate fails because of them, but the zero-warnings rule reaches them. Proposed fix: a small configuration unit after the closure of phase 0 that brings them to zero, each one by its cause.

> **Added on 2026-10-07 by the cookie inventory of the legal register (§5.10, unit L-1b)** — DEF-52 and DEF-53, found while classifying the lifetime of each cookie from the code; they are registered and wait their turn, with no fix in that unit.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-52** — the API's `refreshToken` cookie lives about 7000 days instead of 7: `apps/api/src/auth/authRoutes.ts:112` and `:184`, `apps/api/src/auth/samlRoutes.ts:391` and `apps/api/src/auth/oidcRoutes.ts:464` write it with `maxAge: 7 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000`, a value in milliseconds that carries the comment «7 days» in `authRoutes.ts`, while `@fastify/cookie` 11.0.2 (`apps/api/package.json:118`, the same in `pnpm-lock.yaml`) reads `maxAge` in seconds —its README says so, and the serializer `cookie@1.1.1` writes it as is as `Max-Age`—, so the browser keeps the cookie 604,800,000 seconds, about 7000 days. The server-side expiry of the refresh token still bounds the session; the defect is that the cookie's own lifetime no longer backs it and the browser carries a dead cookie for years, with the `/auth` path and the token's value. Measured on 2026-10-07 by whoever wrote L-1b, and the four sites and the version again on this branch. Proposed fix: `maxAge: 7 * 24 * 60 * 60` in the four sites and a test that asserts `Max-Age=604800` in the `Set-Cookie` header of each login flow; a unit of its own, with its tests. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-53** — Admin's backend proxy renews the `admin-session` cookie with a 1-day lifetime and skips the 15-minute helper: `apps/admin/lib/auth/sessionCookie.ts:31` sets `SESSION_MAX_AGE = 15 * 60` and declares itself the single source of that cookie, but `apps/admin/app/api/backend/[...path]/route.ts:46-52` writes it with `cookieStore.set("admin-session", …, { maxAge: 24 * 60 * 60 })` without going through `setSessionCookie`, so after a renewal through the proxy the short-lived JWT access token stays in the browser 96 times longer than the helper sets. The route `apps/admin/app/api/auth/refresh/route.ts`, by contrast, does use the helper, so two renewal paths leave the same cookie with different lifetimes. Measured on 2026-10-07 by whoever wrote L-1b. Proposed fix: the proxy calls `setSessionCookie` so that a single place owns the cookie's lifetime, with a test that fails when a write of `admin-session` does not go through the helper; a unit of its own. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
>
> **Added on 2026-10-07 by the OAuth permissions inventory of the legal register (§5.10, unit L-1c)** — DEF-54 to DEF-62, found while comparing the product's four scope declarations (connection configuration, adapters, shared metadata and CRM routes); they are registered and wait their turn, with no fix in that unit. Three scopes from a single source were reviewed and are benign: `x:offline.access`, `facebook:pages_show_list` and the read of Snapchat's `me.email` under `snapchat-marketing-api`.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-54** — Instagram: the connection flow never requests `instagram_content_publish`. `packages/providers/instagram/src/InstagramAdapter.ts:74` (`requiredScopes`) and `packages/shared/src/providers/providerConfig.ts:122` declare it, and `InstagramApiClient` publishes through `/{userId}/media` and `/{userId}/media_publish`, but `apps/api/src/auth/providerOAuthConfigs.ts:237` requests only `user_profile`, `user_media` and `instagram_basic`. Impact: a connected Instagram account stores a token without the publish permission. Proposed fix: request the scope in the connection configuration and add a test that asserts that every scope an adapter requires is requested by its connection configuration (the inventory's Mismatches section is the oracle); a unit of its own, with its tests. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-55** — Instagram: the connection flow uses the retired Basic Display API. `apps/api/src/auth/providerOAuthConfigs.ts:237` sends `user_profile` and `user_media` to `api.instagram.com/oauth/authorize` (`:238`) and the connection step reads `graph.instagram.com/me` (`:263`), while the adapter works over the Graph API at `graph.facebook.com`; Meta retired the Basic Display API (2024-12-04). Impact: the connection token does not match the API the adapter calls. Proposed fix: move the connection to the Instagram Graph API login (Facebook Login for Business) with the set `instagram_basic`, `instagram_content_publish` and `pages_show_list` the adapter needs; a unit of its own, with tests against a recorded flow. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-56** — TikTok: direct publishing cannot be authorized. `TikTokApiClient` publishes through `/post/publish/video/init/`, which TikTok conditions on the `video.publish` scope; only the shared metadata (`packages/shared/src/providers/providerConfig.ts:212`) and the package's own authentication service (`packages/providers/tiktok/src/apiClient.ts:112`) list it, while the connection configuration (`apps/api/src/auth/providerOAuthConfigs.ts:405`) requests only `user.info.basic` and `video.upload`, and `packages/providers/tiktok/src/TikTokAdapter.ts:71` does not declare it either. Impact: a connected TikTok channel cannot complete a direct publish. Proposed fix: request `video.publish` where the product publishes directly, align the adapter and test as in DEF-54; a unit of its own. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-57** — LinkedIn: publishing as an organization cannot work. `packages/providers/linkedin/src/LinkedInAdapter.ts:64` and `packages/shared/src/providers/providerConfig.ts:241` declare `w_organization_social`, and `LinkedInApiClient` acts as the organization when the credentials carry an `organizationUrn`, but the connection configuration (`apps/api/src/auth/providerOAuthConfigs.ts:467`) requests only `openid`, `profile` and `w_member_social`. Impact: the token does not authorize writing on behalf of an organization. Proposed fix: request the scope, or make publishing as an organization an explicit option with its own consent step, and test as in DEF-54; the Product Owner decides between the two. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-58** — LinkedIn: the connection step reads the email without the `email` scope. `apps/api/src/auth/providerOAuthConfigs.ts:511` reads `userInfo.email` while the requested scopes are `openid`, `profile` and `w_member_social` (`:467`). Impact: the stored username stays empty on LinkedIn channels. Proposed fix: request `email` or stop reading it, with a test over the fixture of the connection step; a unit of its own. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-59** — Pinterest: the connection step calls `/v5/user_account` without the `user_accounts:read` scope. `packages/providers/pinterest/src/PinterestAdapter.ts:67` declares the scope; the connection step (`apps/api/src/auth/providerOAuthConfigs.ts:553`) and `PinterestApiClient.getUserAccount` call `/v5/user_account`, which requires it, but the connection configuration (`:526`) requests only the four board and pin scopes. Impact: the account read can be rejected after connecting. Proposed fix: request the scope and test as in DEF-54; a unit of its own. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-60** — Threads has adapter scopes but no connection flow. `packages/providers/threads/src/ThreadsAdapter.ts:67-72` declares four scopes, and `apps/api/src/auth/providerOAuthConfigs.ts:646` registers Threads with `createUnimplementedProvider`. Impact: no Threads account can be connected, while the adapter and the product's provider list present it as available. Proposed fix: implement the Threads OAuth flow or withdraw Threads from the connectable providers until it exists; it is a product decision of the Product Owner before any code. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-61** — the OAuth scopes are declared in five places that do not match: the connection configuration, the adapters' `requiredScopes`, the shared `PROVIDER_CONFIGS` (which feed `apps/api/src/providers/providerRegistry.ts` and the client's `useProviders`), the client's `getDefaultScopes` (`apps/client/lib/utils/providerMapper.ts:112`, which holds names that are not scopes, such as `read`, `write` or `r_liteprofile`, and which are shown to the user) and TikTok's in `packages/providers/tiktok/src/apiClient.ts:112`; in addition, `HubSpotAdapter` and `SalesforceAdapter` have a `getAuthorizationUrl` of their own, a second CRM authorization URL, where HubSpot adds `crm.objects.companies.read` and the routes (`apps/api/src/crm/crmRoutes.ts:251`) never call it. Also, `packages/providers/youtube/src/YouTubeAdapter.ts:70` writes short names (`youtube.upload`, `youtube.readonly`) that are not Google scope strings, while the connection configuration uses the full URLs, and the shared metadata do not include `tiktok:user.info.basic`. Impact: each declaration can diverge without anything detecting it, and the user sees permissions the product does not request. Proposed fix: a single source per provider (the adapter's declaration, consumed by the connection configuration and by the shared metadata), the client's mapper reading it, and the inventory's Mismatches section as the regression oracle; first a design decision and then its unit. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-62** — more access is requested than is used: `pinterest:boards:write` (`createBoard` has no caller) and `hubspot:crm.objects.contacts.write` (`HubSpotAdapter` never writes a contact) are requested without use, and the inventory classifies them as `unused`. Impact: data minimization (GDPR, art. 5(1)(c)) and user trust. Proposed fix: withdraw the scopes or deliver the features that need them; the Product Owner decides scope by scope. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

> **Added on 2026-10-08 by the handlebars CVE floor (#492)** — DEF-66, found while measuring the reach of GHSA-8r5x-fm3f-whwj, whose fix in 4.7.10 warns never to compile a stored AST where users can write:
>
> - [ ] **DEF-66** — two paths pass to `Handlebars.compile` the content of a `Json` column converted with `as string`: `ServerTemplateEngine.compileComponent` with `TemplateComponent.content` and `compileWithABTest` with `Template.variants`. Today they are dormant: nothing in `apps` or in `packages` writes `TemplateComponent`, no route calls `compileTemplateWithComponents`, and the template creation and edit handlers do not write `variants`. If either one is activated, a stored object would reach `compile` as an AST. Proposed fix: validate that the content is a string before compiling (or type the column as text) and cover it with a test that rejects an object.

> **Added on 2026-10-09 by the fix of DEF-70** — DEF-71 and DEF-72, found while measuring that fix; they are registered and wait their turn, with no fix in that unit:

> - [ ] **DEF-71** — polling a saga's status requests faster than its limiter bucket refills. `GET /sagas/:sagaId` falls in the STANDARD preset (100 per minute per IP and resource: the `defaultConfig: RateLimitConfigs.STANDARD` that `apps/api/src/index.ts:518` passes to the preHandler), with one bucket per saga since DEF-68. `runSagaAndAwaitTerminal` (`apps/client/lib/api/clients/sagaClient.ts:178-199`), which the pages of the post and of its preview, `useSchedulePostViaSaga` and `lib/api/hooks.ts` use, polls every 200 ms (300 per minute) for up to 60 s: the bucket, full at the start, empties at 30 s, and from then on two of every three polls receive 429, which the loop discards with `continue` without reading `Retry-After`. `useSagaStatus` (`apps/client/lib/hooks/useSagaStatus.ts:26`, 1 s) stays within the limit. Two tests poll the route over HTTP faster than the bucket: `sagaCustomerFlow.live.test.ts:97` (200 ms) and `production.live.test.ts:97` (250 ms). The full-tier run of 2026-10-09 with the limiter on recorded 289 429 responses on `GET /sagas/:sagaId` and passed anyway, because the polls retry. Proposed fix: the polling honors `Retry-After` and does not request faster than the bucket refills (600 ms with STANDARD), with a test that pins it. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

If it is omitted, the call goes to the platform orchestrator with no budget and no record, so any authenticated user uses the platform's AI without limit (OWASP API4:2023 and API6:2023). If it belongs to another account, the tenant guard throws `TenantContextMismatchError` on the queries to `brandVoice`, `accountCredential` and `aiTokenUsage` (`infra/prisma/src/extensions/tenantGuard.ts:220,296`; the three models are in `TENANT_SCOPED_MODELS`), not yet measured at run time. Proposed fix: `ai:configure` on the BYOK routes and `ai:use` on the AI ones, with tests per role. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

It touches the legal register of §5.10. Proposed fix: a CI step that fails on a license outside a reviewed list, with the red path proven; the list itself is a decision of the Product Owner. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

The four Dockerfiles comply today (`USER nonroot`, no `:latest`, no `ADD`), so the risk is of regression, not current. Proposed fix: `trivy config` (Trivy already runs in `Container Security`) or hadolint over the four Dockerfiles, with the red path proven. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

> - [ ] **DEF-77** — the scheduled `OWASP ZAP DAST` job (`.github/workflows/security-testing.yml:168-177`) ends red without a single failing finding. The scheduled run of 2026-10-09 gave 0 FAIL, 68 PASS and 2 WARN («Non-Storable Content» [10049] ×3 and «Sec-Fetch-Dest Header is Missing» [90005] ×11), and with `fail_action: true` the action also fails on WARN. There is no rules file that triages those alerts, and the action tries to open an issue without the `issues: write` permission («Resource not accessible by integration»). `docs/reports/UNUSED_CODE_INVENTORY.md:147` describes it with `fail_action: false`, which no longer holds. A permanent red hides the day a real FAIL appears. Proposed fix: a versioned rules file that triages each alert with its reason, the permission the issue step needs or removing that step, and the red path proven with a planted FAIL; it touches a workflow, so it needs a `sensitive-edit` token. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11. **Maintainer decision (2026-10-18):** replace `zaproxy/action-baseline@v0.15.0` (`security-testing.yml:166`) with ZAP's API scan (`zaproxy/action-api-scan`), fed with the app's OpenAPI specification, the one the versioned route table projects (`apps/api/scripts/dump-openapi-spec.ts`), so that it walks the real routes; a versioned rules file that triages each alert, with its reason, after looking at it; remove the step that opens the issue, because the red job is the signal; and the red path proven with a planted FAIL. A real finding of the API scan is fixed, not suppressed. Measured: the scheduled runs of 2026-10-16, 2026-10-17 and 2026-10-18 fail with 0 FAIL and 2 WARN (10049 ×3, 90005 ×10) and with «Resource not accessible by integration» in the issue step, and the baseline scan visits only `/`, `/robots.txt` and `/sitemap.xml`, so the 68 PASS of the 2026-10-09 run measure almost nothing. In its turn, with a `sensitive-edit` token. With the document's relative server (DEF-143), an API scan that reads the document from a file needs `-O http://localhost:3000` to set the host.

> **Added on 2026-10-10 by the fix of DEF-72** — DEF-79 and DEF-80, found while measuring that fix; they are registered and wait their turn, with no fix in that unit:
>
> - [ ] **DEF-79** — cancelling or rescheduling a scheduled post neither stops nor moves its publish job. The two Admin routes, `POST /admin/posts/:id/cancel` and `POST /admin/posts/:id/reschedule` (`apps/api/src/admin/schedulingRoutes.ts:33,42`), build their handler with only the Prisma client (`:21`) and never call the queue. `SchedulingPostHandlers.ts:211-299` moves the post to `DRAFT`, clears `scheduledAt` and marks as `ERR` its queued or in-progress publish records; `:305-402` moves it to `SCHEDULED` with the new time and, with `updateChannels`, writes it into those records. The job is in BullMQ already since the saga's pivot, with `runAt` equal to the original time (`packages/shared/src/saga.ts:798-810`, `apps/api/src/saga/SagaIntegration.ts:296-303`), and the worker does not read the post's status before publishing: `handleJob` (`apps/workers/src/publishHandler.ts:721-774`) only skips a `dedupeKey` whose record is already `OK` and a deleted post, project or account chain (`SOFT_DELETED`), and `getPostById` (`packages/adapters/db-prisma/src/PostRepository.ts:25-36`) looks up by `id` without looking at the status. So a job already queued publishes at its original time after cancelling or rescheduling. A customer has neither route, and `DeletePostUseCase` (`packages/core/posts/src/DeletePostUseCase.ts:112`) does not allow deleting a scheduled post. Verified statically, not run; found while rewriting the API reference for DEF-72, which already documents this limitation. Proposed fix: cancelling removes and rescheduling re-enqueues the post's jobs through the queue port, and the worker discards a job whose post is no longer scheduled or publishing, with an integration test that cancels a scheduled post and checks that, when its time comes, there is no call to the provider. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-80** — the API reference documents routes that the generated route table (`packages/shared/src/api-generated/types.gen.ts`) does not have, outside the publishing surface DEF-72 fixed. In [`docs/api/README.md`](https://github.com/edwardricardo/omni-post/blob/fb399b4897b6e2229dac2a53ab3a488a46620d49/docs/api/README.md): `GET /health/full` (`:34`), `PUT /posts/{postId}` (`:185`), `GET /analytics` with filters (`:312`), `POST /analytics/fetch` (`:339`) and `PUT /admin/accounts/{accountId}/suspend` (`:400`). In `docs/architecture/API.md`: those five (`:49`, `:272`, `:456`, `:483` and `:592`) and also `POST /auth/rbac/roles` (`:186`), `POST /auth/rbac/assign` (`:199`), `POST /posts/{postId}/media` (`:287`), `POST /media/sign` (`:303`), `GET /audit/logs` (`:511`), `POST /billing/trial/start` (`:626`), `POST /billing/subscription/convert` (`:640`), `GET /providers/health` (`:655`), `GET /providers/x/capabilities` (`:662`) and `POST /providers/x/validate` (`:669`), plus two WebSockets with no registered route, `ws://localhost:3000/analytics/realtime` (`:497` and `:761`) and `ws://localhost:3000/admin/dashboard/live` (`:767`). Two of them have the wrong method: the table registers `PATCH /posts/{id}` and `POST /admin/accounts/{accountId}/suspend`. The two response-format sections (`README.md:11-19` and `API.md:24-32`) claim `{ ok: true, value: T }`, but the handlers over `BaseRouteHandler` answer `{ ok: true, data }` and `{ ok: false, error, details }` (`apps/api/src/lib/route-handler/BaseRouteHandler.ts:263-286`) and the saga routes `{ success: true, data }` (`apps/api/src/saga/SagaIntegration.ts:480,534`). In addition, `docs/features/auth-rate-limit-integrity/design.md:291` cites `docs/architecture/API.md:669-672` and `docs/api/README.md:383-385` for the rate-limit headers (`:502` names `API.md`), which after DEF-72 are at `API.md:739-742` and `README.md:434-436`. Measured on 2026-10-10 while closing DEF-72, against the 500 method+route pairs of the table. `docs/api/README.md` was deleted from the repository on 2026-10-17 (git history keeps it) with the rest of the hand-copied API pages, so its half of this row was closed by deletion and what stays open is `docs/architecture/API.md`. Proposed fix: rewrite each section of `API.md` against the generated table and the code of its route, or remove the one that documents something that does not exist; fix its response-format section, and make `design.md` cite the headers of `API.md` by their content and not by line number (since 2026-10-17, its citation of `docs/api/README.md` is a permalink to the version of that page it measured). It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
