> **Added on 2026-10-10** — DEF-84, found in the CI of #538; DEF-85 and DEF-86, found while measuring the return of Turbopack to `next dev`, and DEF-87 and DEF-88, found while preventing `next dev` from writing agent files, all on the branch `workstream/dev-turbopack`. They are registered and wait their turn, with no fix in this unit:
>
> - [x] **DEF-84** — a `bundleAnalyzer.test.ts` case timed out in CI; closed 2026-10-11 by deleting the analyzer, #553 (merge `47230884`); its licence check moved to DEF-90.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-85** — in a freshly created worktree, `next dev` answers 500 until the `dist` of the portal's workspace dependencies is built. The `predev` of `apps/admin/package.json:9` and of `apps/client/package.json:9` (`pnpm --filter @shared/types... build`) only builds `@shared/types` and its dependencies, and the `dev` task of `turbo.json:54` only depends on `@shared/types#build`; the packages the portal imports through their `dist`, such as `@packages/i18n` (its `exports` points to `./dist/index.js`), stay unbuilt. Measured on 2026-10-10 in Admin: `GET /en/login` answered 500 with `Module not found: Can't resolve '@packages/i18n'` from `apps/admin/i18n/request.ts:9`; the client imports the same package in `apps/client/i18n/request.ts:9`. After `pnpm exec turbo run build --filter='@apps/admin^...' --filter='@apps/client^...'` (19 tasks, 11 s), both portals answer 200. The main checkout does not show it because it keeps those `dist` from earlier builds. It was measured with Webpack; the cause, a `dist` that does not exist, does not depend on the bundler. Proposed fix: the `predev` and the `dev` task of `turbo.json` build every workspace dependency of the portal (`^build`), not only `@shared/types`. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
>
> - [x] **DEF-86** — Sentry never initialized in production, in either portal; closed 2026-10-11 by #554 (merge `00f57868`), with the CI probe of #556 (merge `a76930c3`).
>
> - [ ] **DEF-87** — `apps/admin/next-env.d.ts` and `apps/client/next-env.d.ts` are tracked although `.gitignore:99` lists the file. Next 16 always sets the development `distDir` to `.next/dev` (`next/dist/server/config.js:1248-1250`, in 16.3.8), so `next dev` rewrites the file to import `.next/dev/types`, while `next build` and `next typegen` write `.next/types`: every development run leaves a change in the tree. The guide the package itself ships asks to ignore it and take it out of Git (`next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/05-config/02-typescript.md:120`). Untracking it requires running `next typegen` before the typecheck scripts (`typecheck` in `apps/admin/package.json:16` and `apps/client/package.json:21`, and `type-check` in `apps/client/package.json:31`), which today read the tracked file through their `tsconfig.json` (`apps/admin/tsconfig.json:26`, `apps/client/tsconfig.json:35`). Measured on 2026-10-10: every `next dev` of the branch `workstream/dev-turbopack` left its portal's `next-env.d.ts` modified, and it had to be restored by hand. Proposed next step: untrack both files and run `next typegen` before the typecheck, in a unit of its own. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-88** — `apps/admin/public/README.md` is tracked against the documentation policy: all documentation lives under `docs/`, and there are no `.md` files in `apps/` or in `packages/`, except the root `CLAUDE.md` and `.claude/`. It is the only tracked `.md` under `apps/`, `packages/` and `infra/` (`git ls-files`). Its three lines explain that the directory holds the files Next serves as is from `/` and that it must exist even when empty, because `apps/admin/Dockerfile:96` copies it into the image and a missing directory breaks that stage; as it is the only file in the directory, the README itself is what keeps it in Git. Nothing detects a new misplaced `.md` under `apps/` or `packages/`: no fitness and no gate of `scripts/` covers it, and the pre-edit hooks of `.claude/hooks-py` skip `.md` files (`pre_edit_tripwire_blocker.py:262`, `pre_edit_planmode_guard.py:62`) and never see the files a tool writes, such as the `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` of `next dev`. Proposed fix: move its explanation to `docs/` and keep the directory with a file that is not `.md`, or make the `Dockerfile` not depend on it, because deleting the README outright breaks that copy; then, a gate with its red path proven. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
>
> **Added on 2026-10-11** — DEF-90, found while closing DEF-84; DEF-91 and DEF-92, found while documenting `apps/api/tests/setup.ts` on the branch `workstream/stdout-ascii-guard`, and DEF-93, found while fixing DEF-86 on the branch `workstream/fix-def-86-sentry-instrumentation`. > - [ ] **DEF-90** — the copyleft license check was the only useful value of its own in the bundle analyzer DEF-84 deleted, and it only ran by hand, with `pnpm quality:bundle`: it read `pnpm licenses list --json` and flagged every installed package whose license expression contained `GPL-2.0`, `GPL-3.0` or `AGPL-3.0` (the substring match also flagged `LGPL-2.0` and `LGPL-3.0`, but not `LGPL-2.1`), without failing anything and returning an empty list when pnpm could not list the licenses. No gate covers that check: `pnpm quality:security` lists the licenses without judging them. It needs a real gate, one that reads `pnpm licenses list --json` and fails on a copyleft expression, with its red path proven, probably beside the legal dependency inventory (`docs/legal/inventories/subprocessors.generated.md`, fitness #45) or inside `pnpm check:legal`. Where it lives and which expressions count as copyleft is a decision of its own unit. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-91** — `setupTest` of `apps/api/tests/setup.ts` logs «DB/Redis connections verified» without inspecting the `Result` of `queue.health()` or that of `listLogs({ limit: 1 })`: both are awaited with `Promise.all` and the value is discarded (`_healthResults`), so a database or Redis error returned as `err` passes the setup, and only a rejection or the time limit (`TEST_CONFIG.dbTimeout`) make it fail. In addition, when it fails, the `catch` drops both adapters (`globalRepo = null`, `globalQueue = null`) without closing them, and the next call creates others. Proposed fix: narrow both results and make the setup fail on an `err`, closing what was opened. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-92** — `makeFlowCleanup`, in the same file, discards the `Result` of each deletion (`deletePost`, `deleteProject`, `deleteAccount`) and its `catch` also ignores the rejections («Ignore cleanup errors»), and then empties the lists anyway: a failed cleanup leaves no trace and the next suite inherits the rows. `deletePost` is not a method of `RepoPort`: `packages/ports/src/RepoPort.ts` declares `deleteAccount` (`:152`) and `deleteProject` (`:180`) and no post deletion, so `apps/api/tests/setup.ts:362` throws a `TypeError` on every recorded post and the bare `catch` at `:363` absorbs it; the posts leave only through their project's `ON DELETE CASCADE` (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma:706`), and the tests typecheck ledger records one `TS2339` for `tests/setup.ts`. Proposed fix: gather the failures and report them, and call a deletion the port has or delete the post loop. Backlog SMELL-157 (and SMELL-86). It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-93** — `initBrowserSentry` (`packages/observability/sentry-next/src/client.ts`), which `apps/admin/instrumentation-client.ts` and `apps/client/instrumentation-client.ts` call, sets `replaysSessionSampleRate: 0` and `replaysOnErrorSampleRate: 1.0`, but `@sentry/nextjs` 10.48.0 does not add `replayIntegration` by default: its client's `getDefaultIntegrations` (`build/cjs/client/index.js:116-122`) adds `browserTracingIntegration` to the default integrations of `@sentry/browser` (`build/npm/cjs/dev/sdk.js:18-37`), and none of them records sessions: the two options have no effect. They came from the configuration files DEF-86 replaced, and were kept unchanged. The decision belongs to the Product Owner, because a session replay is personal data and turning it on changes the legal register: add `Sentry.replayIntegration()` with its entry in the register (the note and `dataSent` of `@sentry/nextjs` in `docs/legal/classification/subprocessors.json`), or remove the two options. It waits its turn, like the rest of §5.11.

> **Added on 2026-10-18** — DEF-105 to DEF-109, found during the fixed-wait sweep of N-CI-7 (#641 to #646) and while ordering its PR chain; measured statically on `main` at `30d9c8cb`. They are registered without a fix and wait their turn, like the rest of §5.11:
>
> - [ ] **DEF-105** — two tests of the AI orchestrator assert a behavior that does not exist. The orchestrator has no per-call time limit: `AITaskConfig.timeout` (`apps/api/src/ai/types.ts:48`) is set to 30000 (`apps/api/src/ai/orchestrator.ts:128`, `:147` and `:165`) and nothing reads it; and `getOptimalProvider` (`orchestrator.ts:191-209`) tries the providers in the fixed order of `providerStrengths`, without looking at latency. «should handle provider timeout gracefully» (`apps/api/tests/unit/aiOrchestrator.content.test.ts:226`) passes because the slow provider ends up answering at 2 s, not because another one replaces it, and «should prefer faster providers over time» (`apps/api/tests/unit/aiOrchestrator.providers.test.ts:200`) passes because `openai` goes first in that fixed order. Proposed fix: decide whether the orchestrator must have a per-call time limit and an order by latency, and implement them with their tests, or rewrite the two tests so they assert the behavior that exists; the decision is a product one. Found in U4b (#644). **Product Owner decision (2026-10-18):** rewrite the two tests so they assert the behavior that exists (the provider that fails or exceeds its time limit is skipped and the next one answers; the choice follows the fixed order of each task), delete `AITaskConfig.timeout` and its three assignments, and build no order by latency; in Phase 2 of the Testing Refoundation or in its turn.

No step of CI, of the battery or of the hooks runs actionlint. In its turn; it touches workflows, so it needs a `sensitive-edit` token.
> - [ ] **DEF-108** — the database circuit breaker of the shared adapter counts a domain error as an outage. `createDatabaseCircuitBreaker` (`packages/adapters/db-prisma/src/resilience.ts:57-68`) creates an opossum 10.0.0 breaker without `errorFilter` (the default counts every rejection) and without `volumeThreshold` (0 by default), and `createPrismaRepoAdapter` (`packages/adapters/db-prisma/src/index.ts:102-122`) opens the writes breaker at 40 % failures for 60 s and the transactions breaker at 30 % for 90 s. With `volumeThreshold` 0, a single error in a window with no other calls is enough: a `createPost` for a project that does not exist (`PostRepository.ts:146-151` throws inside the transaction) or a duplicate email (P2002) in `createAccount` (`AccountRepository.ts:44`) would open the breaker for every tenant. Latent: a static search found no production caller of the adapter's `createPost` or `createAccount`. Proposed fix: an `errorFilter` that leaves domain and constraint errors out, and a measured `volumeThreshold`; what counts as an outage is a decision of the Maintainer. Found while ordering the N-CI-7 chain. **Maintainer decision (2026-10-18), together with DEF-109:** demolish instead of fixing. Measured on `28807cef`: the three breakers protect only `createAccount`, `getAccountById` and `createPost`, none with a production caller, and `createPost` duplicates the path of `CreatePostUseCase`. The three methods are deleted from the adapter and from `RepoPort` (`packages/ports/src/RepoPort.ts:138`, `:142` and `:222`) with their tests, the three breakers with their metrics wiring, and `perf:baseline` moves to seeding through the seed client or the real use cases.
> - [ ] **DEF-109** — `createPost` of the same adapter (`packages/adapters/db-prisma/src/PostRepository.ts:142-145`) looks up the project with `tx.project.findFirst({ where: { id: input.projectId, deletedAt: null } })` in a transaction without a tenant, on purpose; if `input.projectId` arrives `undefined` at run time, Prisma takes `id: undefined` as the absence of a filter and returns any live project of any account, so the «project does not exist» guard does not fire and the creation fails later with a Prisma validation error (`Post.projectId` is required) instead of the guard's message. The type `CreatePostInput.projectId: string` (`packages/ports/src/RepoPort.ts:59`) excludes it at compile time, not at run time. Latent, like DEF-108. Proposed fix: reject an empty `projectId` before the query. Found while ordering the N-CI-7 chain. **Maintainer decision (2026-10-18):** it is resolved with the demolition of DEF-108.

> **Added on 2026-10-18** — DEF-110 to DEF-112: DEF-110 and DEF-111, found by unit K5c of N-CI-7 (#651) while making no saga run outlive its test; DEF-112, found by unit O-F of N-CI-7 (#655) while making the bulk-scheduling reconciliation suite independent of order. They are registered without a fix and wait their turn, like the rest of §5.11:
>
> - [ ] **DEF-110** — `SagaManagerLifecycle.shutdown()` (`apps/api/src/saga/SagaManagerLifecycle.ts:1179-1228`) neither waits for nor cancels the detached runs. `startSaga` dispatches its run with `executeSagaAsync` (`:894`), and `shutdown()` empties `activeInstances` and `definitions` (`:1222-1225`) without waiting for the dispatched runs. Measured: a run dispatched before `shutdown()` runs afterwards and logs at error level «Saga definition not found» (`apps/api/src/saga/SagaManagerExecution.ts:492`); the saga unit run logged 17 of those lines before #651. Read, not measured: a run that is already inside a step keeps its definition, and its later writes can overwrite the hand-over to `PENDING` that `shutdown()` does, because the compare-and-swap of `SagaManagerExecution.ts:1576-1580` rejects only the terminal states. Proposed fix: `shutdown()` waits for or cancels each run in progress before handing over its sagas; which of the two is a decision of the Maintainer. Found in K5c (#651). **Maintainer decision (2026-10-18):** wait, do not cancel: `shutdown()` stops accepting new runs, waits for the ones in progress with a cap shorter than the platform's shutdown deadline and then hands over the ones left; in addition, the persistence compare-and-swap rejects a write that advances over a row already handed over (it requires `RUNNING`), so that a run that outlives the cap does not overwrite the `PENDING`. Cancelling requires every step to honor the abort and does not undo an external call already sent: it only loses its record. In its turn, together with DEF-111.
> - [ ] **DEF-111** — `shutdown()` empties the `activeInstances` map (`SagaManagerLifecycle.ts:1222`) without going through `stopTracking` (`:1835-1841`), the only one that decrements `metrics.activeInstances` (`:1839`), so the metric keeps its last value after the shutdown. Measured: it stays at 2 after closing the manager with two parked sagas. Proposed fix: decrement each instance when emptying the map, with a test that pins the metric at zero after the shutdown. Found in K5c (#651). **Maintainer decision (2026-10-18):** it is fixed in the same unit as DEF-110.
> - [ ] **DEF-112** — the bulk-scheduling reconciliation always re-enqueues the same rows of the dead-letter queue and never reaches the others. `sweep()` (`apps/api/src/bulk-scheduling/BulkScheduleReconciliationService.ts:95-102`) reads from `OutboxDeadLetter` up to `MAX_PER_TICK = 50` (`:32`) unresolved `BulkScheduleItem` rows, with no `orderBy`, re-enqueues each one (with the deduplication key `bulk-${batchId}-${itemId}`, `:151`) and never writes `resolvedAt`: only the admin routes of `apps/api/src/outbox/outboxAdminRoutes.ts` (`:90` and `:115`) resolve rows, by hand. So the same rows, up to 50, are processed again every 60 s (`:72`) until someone resolves them, and the ones beyond those 50 are never processed again. Read, not measured in production. Proposed fix: decide when a re-enqueued row is resolved and in what order the rows are taken, with a test for more than 50 rows; the decision is a product one, the Product Owner's. Found in O-F (#655). **Product Owner decision (2026-10-18):** mark the row resolved (`resolvedAt`, reason «re-enqueued by the reconciliation») as soon as its re-enqueue succeeds, and take the rows from the oldest to the newest, with a test of more than 50 rows. Measured: the `bulk-schedule` queue configures neither `removeOnComplete` nor `removeOnFail` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/setupServices.ts:678-681`), so today a repeated re-enqueue is a no-op and does not duplicate; the defect is the starvation beyond 50 rows and the trap of turning on `removeOnComplete`. A re-enqueued job that fails again already goes to `bulk-schedule-dead-letter` (`apps/api/src/index.ts:1202-1215`).
>
> **Added on 2026-10-18** — DEF-113 to DEF-116, found by N-AUTH-1 (§1): DEF-113 while planning it, DEF-114 while fixing the ENTERPRISE quota, DEF-115 while making the assertions of its live tier exact and DEF-116 by `pnpm check:duplicates`. Read and measured on `c8c826fe`; they are registered without a fix and wait their turn, like the rest of §5.11:
>
> - [ ] **DEF-113** — the Admin usage panel never loads. `UsageMetricsPanel` (`apps/admin/components/settings/UsageMetricsPanel.tsx:62`, mounted in `apps/admin/app/[locale]/(dashboard)/subscriptions/page.tsx:381`) asks for its data with `useUsageMetrics` (`apps/admin/hooks/api/useUsageMetrics.ts:27`) to `GET /accounts/:accountId/usage` through the Admin proxy, which forwards the Admin token (`apps/admin/app/api/backend/[...path]/route.ts:76`); the route is a customer one (`requireClientAuth`, `apps/api/src/usage/usageRoutes.ts:103`) and `verifyCustomerToken` rejects every token whose `type` is not `customer` (`apps/api/src/auth/customerJwt.ts:96`), so the panel always receives 401. It was already so on `main` at `28807cef`, before N-AUTH-1, and the generated route table has no Admin route for an account's usage (`packages/shared/src/api-generated/types.gen.ts`, only `/accounts/{accountId}/usage`). Read, not measured at run time. Proposed fix: `GET /admin/accounts/:accountId/usage` with `requireAdminAuth`, its permission and `withSystemContext`, and the Admin hook pointed at it. **Product Owner decision (2026-10-18), question 4 of N-AUTH-1:** an item of its own, with no code in N-AUTH-1.
> - [ ] **DEF-114** — outside `createProject`, the `maxProjects = -1` of ENTERPRISE (unlimited: `packages/core/domain/src/entities/Account.ts:56`, and `canCreateProject` at `:354`) is used as a number. `TrialManagementService.buildTrialResponse` (`packages/core/billing/src/TrialManagementService.ts:108` and `:110`) and `dashboardService.mapAccountToSummary` (`apps/api/src/admin/dashboardService.ts:124`, `:153` and `:155`) answer, for a limit of -1, 0 projects remaining and 0 % usage instead of unlimited. `SubscriptionManagementService.validateSubscriptionLimits` (`packages/core/billing/src/SubscriptionManagementService.ts:83-84`, `:93` and `:110`), behind `POST /admin/billing/accounts/:accountId/validate-limits` (`apps/api/src/billing/subscriptionRoutes.ts:96`), would deny the creation of a project and would compute negative team and storage limits (`-1 * 5`, `-1 * 10`), but it reads `AccountSubscription.maxProjects` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaAccountSubscriptionQueryRepository.ts:142-148`), not the account's column, and the subscription use cases that were read set it to 3 or to 3 per provider (`packages/core/billing/src/CreateAccountSubscriptionUseCase.ts:117-164`, `ChangeAccountSubscriptionUseCase.ts:130-219`): latent. Read, not measured at run time. Proposed fix: a single unlimited-limit predicate in the domain, the `-1` of `Account.ts:354`, used by the three places, with a test of each for -1.
> - [ ] **DEF-115** — an existing post that the saga schedules stays in `DRAFT` and can be deleted while its publish jobs wait for their time. With `mode: "schedule"` and a `postId`, `CreatePostStep` does not touch the post (`packages/shared/src/saga.ts:601-615`, `skippedCreation: true`), the pivot enqueues one job per channel, and `WaitForPublishingCompletionStep` and `UpdatePostStatusStep` finish without doing anything in that mode (`saga.ts:860` and `:990`), although the second one's JSDoc says the post stays in `DRAFT`/`SCHEDULED` (`:976-977`). **Measured** in the live tier of N-AUTH-1 (`apps/api/tests/production.live.test.ts`, «should schedule publication» and «should delete post», 179 of 179 green): with the saga in `COMPLETED`, `DELETE /posts/:postId` answers 200, because `DeletePostUseCase` deletes an editable post (`packages/core/posts/src/DeletePostUseCase.ts:112`). The deletion is soft (`packages/adapters/db-prisma/src/post/PrismaPostRepository.ts:132-134`) and the worker discards the job of a deleted post (`apps/workers/src/publishHandler.ts:746-762`), so it does not publish; what fails is the status: the post shows as an editable draft while its jobs wait. It contradicts two records: DEF-79 says that `DeletePostUseCase` does not allow deleting a scheduled post, true only for a post in `SCHEDULED`, and WU-4.3 of the Testing Refoundation plan expects 403 for that deletion (`docs/development/TESTING_REFOUNDATION_PLAN.md:1311`). N-COR-9 already records that the saga skips the `schedule` mode; a new post created in that mode was not measured. Proposed fix: the `schedule` mode leaves the post in `SCHEDULED` through the aggregate, and the live test of the deletion asserts 403 with the domain code; which saga step writes the status is a design decision.
> - [ ] **DEF-116** — the Admin login and the customer login repeat a block: `apps/api/src/auth/authRoutes.ts:41-59` and `apps/api/src/auth/customerAuthRoutes.ts:180-200` (19 lines, 89 tokens; measured with jscpd over the two files, with the thresholds of `.jscpd.json`). It is a baseline clone: `c8c826fe` added inside its span the JSDoc of `login` that fitness #47 requires, and since jscpd's fingerprint covers the raw text, comments included, it went from `d5cf65c1e8562321` to `09c2cc687c1608ad`; the baseline swapped one fingerprint for the other without raising the count. Proposed fix: move what the two handlers share (the validation of the body and its error response) to a common function and delete the baseline entry.

Two definitions of the CSP, one of them dead and tested as if it were in force, with values that differ (the development preset turns the CSP off). Decide: `createApp()` reads the preset of its environment, or the presets leave with their tests. **Decided on 2026-10-28 (Maintainer): accepted** the recommendation — the presets and their two tests are deleted, since they pin values of dead code: the development preset sets `["*"]`, which in `@fastify/cors` 11.1.0 lets no browser origin through, and the production one falls back to `https://app.yourapp.com`. The real CORS stays as DEF-146.

> **Added on 2026-10-28** — DEF-146 to DEF-150 and a requirement, found while preparing the decisions of DEF-120 to DEF-145 and while reviewing the local observability. They are registered without a fix and wait their turn:
>
> - [ ] **DEF-146** — `createApp()` fixes six `localhost` CORS origins in every environment, production included (`apps/api/src/index.ts:417-422`). The origins have to come from typed variables per environment, with no example domain as fallback (decided with DEF-145).
> - [ ] **DEF-147** — to verify: the client gate looks only at the `customer-session` access cookie (`apps/client/proxy.ts:44`), which lasts 15 minutes (`apps/client/lib/auth/sessionCookie.ts:29`), and not at the refresh one, which lasts 7 or 30 days (`:32`, `:35`). If nothing renews it before it expires, every session idle for more than 15 minutes goes back to the login.
> - [ ] **DEF-148** — Grafana links the Prometheus exemplars to the `jaeger` datasource (`grafana/provisioning/datasources/prometheus.yml:14-16`), but no provisioning file defines it: the jump from a metric to its trace does not work.
> - [ ] **DEF-149** — Jaeger keeps the traces in memory (`jaeger/config.yaml:20`), so they are lost every time it restarts; the requirement below asks not to lose the history.
> - [ ] **DEF-150** — to measure: with `TRACING_ENABLED=true` the API starts the OpenTelemetry SDK (`apps/api/src/index.ts:15-20`) before Sentry (`:469`, `:476`) and does not pass `skipOpenTelemetrySetup`. According to Sentry 10's code, its trace provider does not stay as the global one and its API spans degrade; it is an inference from reading, not measured. The requirement below demands that the two coexist.
> - [ ] **[REQUIREMENT] OpenTelemetry in every application: one error monitor, Sentry or GlitchTip, and an OTLP backend at the same time** (Maintainer, 2026-10-28): "It has to be added to the portals; every application must use it. I don't think, in fact, both should be on at the same time; they should be mutually exclusive, but without deleting the previous history when they are switched." Today the API and the workers export traces over OTLP with `TRACING_ENABLED`; the two portals have no OpenTelemetry. Grafana 11.2.2, Prometheus v2.48.1 and Jaeger exist only in `docker-compose.yml`, and the deployment checklist names none of them. Clarified that same day: "When I referred to mutual exclusion I meant Sentry or GlitchTip, but you can send to either of these 2 and still be able to send to an otpl backend. And yes, everything goes to both sides." So: the four applications (API, workers and the two portals) are instrumented once with OpenTelemetry; each deployment chooses at most one between Sentry and GlitchTip, which speak the same protocol, and can add, at the same time and independently, an OTLP backend; errors and traces go to both destinations, and switching from Sentry to GlitchTip, or the other way round, does not delete the history each one keeps. Making them coexist requires resolving DEF-150: Sentry has to be mounted on the application's OpenTelemetry SDK instead of registering its own.
>
> **Added on 2026-10-28** — DEF-151, registered by the artifact-language workstream (§5.17, decision D6). Recorded without a fix; it waits its turn:
>
> - [ ] **DEF-151** — notifications persist pre-rendered text in one fixed language and ignore the recipient's locale. A `Notification` stores a rendered `title` and `body` (`packages/core/domain/src/entities/Notification.ts:71-79`), and the client shows them as they are (`apps/client/lib/api/clients/notificationsClient.ts:19-20`). Each producer hardcodes one language: `packages/core/notifications/src/handlers/NotificationEventHandlers.ts` writes English (`:60`, `:85`, `:112` and the rest of the file), and `packages/core/inbox/src/handlers/InboxEventHandlers.ts:46-47,76-77` writes Spanish. The recipient's locale exists (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma:28` and `:202`, default `"en"`) and nothing reads it. The fix is a message key with parameters, rendered in the recipient's locale by the portal's i18n. The application is pre-production, so no recipient receives these messages yet. Until the fix, the artifact-language gate allowlists `InboxEventHandlers.ts` with this DEF as its reason, and its strings are not translated: changing user-visible text is this defect's own decision. Backlog SMELL-138.

> **Added on 2026-10-28** — DEF-152, found while translating the webhook ingest wiring document (ALR-U6-6b) and confirmed in the code by its verifier. Recorded without a fix; it is latent until the inbound route is wired, and it waits its turn:
>
> - [ ] **DEF-152** — the inbound webhook pipeline picks a provider subscription across tenants. `getWebhookSubscription` (`apps/api/src/webhooks/webhookHandlerCore.ts:310-316`) runs `findFirst({ provider, isActive: true })` with no account filter and no order; the processor runs under `withSystemContext("system:inbound-webhook")` (`apps/api/src/webhooks/webhookJobProcessor.ts:246`), and the tenant guard passes system-context queries unchanged (`infra/prisma/src/extensions/tenantGuard.ts:20`), so with two accounts subscribed to one provider an arbitrary account's secret verifies the signature (CWE-639). Latent today: no inbound provider route exists and nothing constructs `WebhookManager`. The planned `/webhooks/:provider` route also shares its prefix with `POST /webhooks/stripe` and `/webhooks/paddle` (`apps/api/src/billing/billingWebhookRoutes.ts:188`, `:243`) and `/webhooks/dashboard/*`. Read in the code, not run. **Proposed fix:** resolve the subscription from the request's own identity, not by provider alone, and give the inbound route a prefix of its own; prove both with a red test of two subscribed accounts before the route is wired.
>
> **Added on 2026-10-28** — DEF-153 to DEF-157, found while reading the `openspec/` living specs once before their retirement (ALR-U8-3, §5.17, decision D4) and confirmed in the code. DEF-156 is closed in that same unit; DEF-153 to DEF-155 and DEF-157 are registered without a fix and wait their turn:
>
> - [ ] **DEF-153** — the Admin refresh-session family revocation has no owner. The rotation refuses a replayed Admin refresh token without revoking the rest of its session family (`apps/api/src/auth/authServiceSession.ts:184-190`), so where the loser of the race was the legitimate holder the other pair stays live until it expires, and the runbook leaves the remediation manual until that work lands (`docs/runbooks/alert-admin-refresh-token-replay.md`, §"What this alert does NOT do — deferred family revocation"). Both name the work only as going together with the customer refresh flow (SMELL-113), and the Master Plan row of that flow, `core-refresh-no-rotation-02` (§5.1), covers the customer refresh alone. Read in the code, not run. **Proposed fix:** give the Admin family revocation an owner, inside `core-refresh-no-rotation-02` or as a row of its own, and revoke the family on a detected replay, proven by a red test that replays a rotated token.
> - [ ] **DEF-154** — `docs/compliance/RETENTION_CALENDAR.md` has no row for `DeletionRecord.name`, although the calendar is the retention window per data type and the scheduled job that enforces it. The plaintext name of a hard-delete tombstone is kept until `retainUntil`, never earlier than `clientUntil` plus one year (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma:3740-3742`), and the daily `deletion-record-degrader` tick (`apps/api/src/index.ts:985`) replaces it with its HMAC digest once that date has passed (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/retention/DeletionRecordDegrader.ts:94`). **Proposed fix:** one row of the retention matrix for `DeletionRecord.name`, with its window and that job.
> - [ ] **DEF-155** — dependency-cruiser has no `adapters-no-apps` rule. `.dependency-cruiser.cjs` holds `core-no-apps` (`:158`) and `shared-no-apps` (`:251`), and no rule starts from `packages/adapters/` or `packages/providers/`, so nothing stops an adapter or a provider from importing an application. Measured on 2026-10-28: no file under either imports `apps/`; fitness #40 Part B holds how the relocated `@adapters/db-prisma` adapters derive their tenant scope, not what they import. **Proposed fix:** an `adapters-no-apps` rule, with a providers twin, its red path proven; the known-violations baseline needs no entry, because the count is 0.
> - [x] **DEF-156** — ARCHITECTURE_CANON §Event-Driven Architecture said "After dispatch, mark event `PROCESSED` atomically", while `OutboxEvent` has no status column (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma:2070`): the relay marks a delivered event by setting `publishedAt` in one `UPDATE` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/outbox/OutboxClaimService.ts:113-118`), the column it also sets on a dead-lettered event, in the transaction that writes its `OutboxDeadLetter` row (`:138-164`). A description fix, not a rule change: the canon sentence names `publishedAt` and keeps its rule, atomic marking after dispatch; done 2026-10-28 by ALR-U8-3.
> - [ ] **DEF-157** — six code comments still say the tenant guard audits or logs a `withSystemContext` reason, which it does not: the guard and `tenantGuc.ts` never read it (`infra/prisma/src/extensions/tenantGuard.ts:20-21`, `:202-204`; `infra/prisma/src/extensions/tenantGuc.ts:120`), as `docs/security/MULTI_TENANT_GUARDS.md` §How to use `withSystemContext()` says since 2026-10-28. The sites: `apps/api/src/auth/customerAuthRoutes.ts:208` ("audits the reason instead"), `apps/api/src/webhooks/webhookJobProcessor.ts:20-21` ("this audited bypass"), `apps/api/src/security/tenantContext.ts:45-47` (a free-text reason "logged on every cross-tenant query for audit purposes", with an interpolated example) and `:134` ("logged on each guarded query"), and `apps/api/src/billing/billingWebhookRoutes.ts:199` ("the tenant guard audits the reason") and `:253` ("audited bypass"). **Proposed fix:** each comment states what the reason is, a declaration the guard never reads, in the change that next touches each file, so fitness #47 holds the whole file to the JSDoc canon.

> **Added on 2026-10-29** — DEF-158 to DEF-172, folded from the detected-smells backlog (ALR-U6-9, §5.17, decision B) and re-read in the code that day (`verified` 2026-10-29); each carries its backlog id. Recorded without a fix; they wait their turn:
>
> - [ ] **DEF-158** — `matchesWhere` of the shared Prisma test double (`apps/api/tests/unit/helpers/mockPrisma.ts:72`) compares an operator object it does not recognise with the stored value by reference (`:140`, then `recordVal !== val` at `:143`). So `{ equals: x }`, the form `apps/api/src/security/auditLogger.ts:319` sends, matches no row: a zero that looks like a correctly firing count. Each operator it does recognise ends in `continue` (`:101-139`), so an object holding two, such as `{ gte, lte }`, is checked on the first alone. 37 files import the helper. Proposed fix: evaluate every key of an operator object and throw on an unrecognised one, then rerun the importing suites and fix any test that passed on the old answer. Backlog SMELL-116. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-159** — the duplicate-code gate does not read ten source files. `.jscpd.json:8` ignores `**/reports/**`, a pattern meant for generated report output, and it also matches every source directory named `reports`: `apps/api/src/reports/reportRoutes.ts`, the seven files of `packages/core/reports/src`, `apps/client/app/[locale]/reports/shared/[token]/page.tsx` and `apps/client/app/[locale]/dashboard/analytics/reports/page.tsx`. So `pnpm check:duplicates` reports nothing about a clone inside those files or between them and the rest of the tree. The one report output `.gitignore` names is `**/reports/mutation/` (`.gitignore:33`), and the architecture gate already narrowed its exclusion to that (`.dependency-cruiser.cjs:378-381`). Proposed fix: narrow the ignore to `**/reports/mutation/**` and add to `.jscpd-baseline.json` exactly the fingerprints that appear, each read before it is accepted. Backlog SMELL-203. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-160** — no gate sees a Node.js runtime advisory in the four images. The `Container Security` gate (`.github/workflows/production-ci.yml:455-460`: Trivy with `severity: "CRITICAL,HIGH"` and `exit-code: "1"`) reports the image's Debian packages and each module's `package.json`, never the `node` executable, which distroless ships with no dpkg record. Measured on 2026-10-22 with Trivy 0.76.1 against `gcr.io/distroless/nodejs26-debian13@sha256:746f728c…` (node v26.12.0, four High advisories fixed in 26.12.1): 0 HIGH or CRITICAL, exit 0. The only reader of the nodejs.org release index is a manual review recorded in `scripts/testing/maturity-watchlist.json:90-91`. Proposed fix: a step reads the `node` version each image runs (the smoke already runs `/nodejs/bin/node`, `production-ci.yml:327`) and fails when `https://nodejs.org/dist/index.json` lists a newer release on the same line with `security: true`, with its red shown on the v26.12.0 digest. Backlog SMELL-207. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-161** — a tracked link's public code can resolve to either of two links: `vanitySlug` is a plain optional column with a non-unique index (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma:897`, `:917`) while only `shortCode` is `@unique` (`:896`), and both the redirect lookup and the availability probe match `OR: [{ shortCode: code }, { vanitySlug: code }]` with `findFirst` and no order (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaTrackedLinkRepository.ts:130-133`, `:287-290`); `CreateTrackedLinkUseCase` checks availability and then inserts with no constraint behind the check (`packages/core/links/src/CreateTrackedLinkUseCase.ts:60-69`), so two concurrent creates, or a generated `shortCode` equal to another link's slug, leave one code that redirects to whichever row the planner returns. Proposed fix: one code namespace the database enforces across both columns (a unique `vanitySlug` plus a guard against a slug equal to another link's `shortCode`, or one table of codes), after a migration that finds and resolves existing collisions. Backlog SMELL-57. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-162** — the Admin password-reset request tells a known email from an unknown one by its response time: for an unknown or inactive email `PasswordService.initiatePasswordReset` returns after one lookup with the sentinel `"reset_token_placeholder"` (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/PasswordService.ts:183-188`; its `crypto.randomUUID()` equalizes nothing), while for a known one it writes the token and the route awaits the credential read, the template and the email provider before answering (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/adminAuthRoutes.ts:253-290`), and the route tells the two apart by comparing the token with that string literal (`:253`). Proposed fix: return a typed outcome instead of the sentinel and send the email after the response, so both branches answer in the same time. Backlog SMELL-115. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-163** — an Admin can change or reset their password to the one they already have: the reuse check compares the new password only with `passwordHistory` (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/PasswordService.ts:105-114` in `changePassword`, `:272-278` in the reset claim), and the current hash joins the history only in the same write (`:123`, `:289`), so the live password is never compared. Proposed fix: compare the new password with the live `passwordHash` as well, in both paths, with one unit test per path. Backlog SMELL-117. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-164** — the Admin portal's refresh never rotates its refresh token: the portal refreshes through `POST /admin/auth/refresh` (`apps/admin/app/api/auth/refresh/route.ts:46`, `apps/admin/app/api/backend/[...path]/route.ts:29`), which `AdminAuthService.refreshToken` serves by comparing the stored hash (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/AdminAuthService.ts:239`), updating only `lastActivityAt` (`:260-263`) and returning the same refresh token (`:269`), so a stolen token stays valid for the session's whole life and a replay is not detected. The single-statement rotation claim that fitness #41 guards lives in `authServiceSession.refreshTokens` (`apps/api/src/auth/authServiceSession.ts:174-198`), behind `POST /auth/refresh`, which neither portal calls. Proposed fix: give this flow the same claim (the new hash in the `update`, the presented hash in its `where`) and revoke the session on a replay, moving fitness #41's site floor and its "out of class" note with it. Backlog SMELL-118. 🔗 related: DEF-153 (the family revocation of the other Admin refresh flow). It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-165** — share of voice depends on the order in which the database returns tracked terms. The mention worker attributes each mention to the first term whose text it contains (`apps/workers/src/mentionIngestWorker.ts:237-241`), and `Mention.trackedTermId` holds a single term (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma:2203`). The terms reach the worker in no defined order: the query has no `orderBy` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaTrackedTermQuery.ts:18-30`) and `packages/core/listening/src/DispatchMentionSearchUseCase.ts:112` passes them on in that order. Share of voice counts a mention as brand or market by that one term's kind (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaMentionQueryRepository.ts:25-28`, `:51-55`, `sov` at `:106`). A mention that names the brand and a market term is therefore counted on whichever side came first, and a search hit whose term is not in the body counts on neither side. Proposed fix: store every matched term per mention and count brand and market from that set, with a test where one mention matches both. Backlog SMELL-25. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-166** — the CQRS bus does not deduplicate commands by id, although the canon says it does (`docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE_CANON.md:216`: «The CQRS bus dedupes by command ID»). `executeCommand` (`apps/api/src/cqrs/CQRSBus.ts:91-121`) looks up the handler and runs it, and keeps no record of the ids it has seen. The saga mints the deterministic id `cmd-${sagaId}-create-post` (`packages/shared/src/saga.ts:621`), but `PostAggregate.create` generates a fresh post id on every call (`packages/core/domain/src/aggregates/PostAggregate.ts:149`). So a saga replayed from before its create step, by a manual resume or by any future automatic one, persists a second draft post. Proposed fix: a durable store of seen command ids, which the bus consults before it calls the handler, with a test that sends the same create command twice and finds one post; the canon sentence stays only if that lands. Backlog SMELL-71. 🔗 related: N-COR-15 (the cross-replica row claim, which names this gap). It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-167** — `POST /sagas/:sagaId/continue` can answer success when it resumed nothing. The route (`apps/api/src/saga/SagaIntegration.ts:596-611`) returns `success: true` after `continueSaga` (`apps/api/src/saga/SagaManagerLifecycle.ts:911`) dispatches with the `operator` trigger (`:950`). When this process already holds the saga, the engine merges only an `event` trigger into the running pass and drops any other trigger with a DEBUG log, uncounted (`apps/api/src/saga/SagaManagerExecution.ts:344-369`). In the same situation, the sibling `compensateSaga` answers 409 (`SagaManagerLifecycle.ts:989`). Proposed fix: decide together with the compensate sibling so the two endpoints answer alike: either a dropped operator dispatch returns a conflict (or an "already advancing" flag), or `operator` is merged like `event`; a route test covers each case. Backlog SMELL-77. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-168** — the customer analytics dashboard and export report wrong numbers. The dashboard groups analytics rows by provider and then maps over channels (`apps/api/src/analytics/analyticsRoutes.ts:592-628`), so each of two channels on one provider reports the whole provider's totals. It reads at most 500 rows (`:578-582`) and reports their sums as totals, and `dataPoints` as their count (`:658`), with no truncation flag. The export does the same with 1000 posts and 5000 analytics rows (`:704-716`, summary at `:740`). `totalClicks`, `followerCount` and `growthRate` (`:624-626`) and `growthThisWeek` (`:650`) are always 0. The CSV columns `postStatus` and `publishedAt` stay blank when posts are not loaded (`:852`, `:863`). The export also re-reads the project its access check already proved, and that re-read's 404 branch can never run (`:686-699`). Proposed fix: once the Product Owner signs off on what each number means, aggregate per channel, report the caps or remove them, feed the constant fields or drop them, and re-author the characterization goldens. Backlog SMELL-101. Latent while DEF-32 ingests no analytics rows; 🔗 related: ARCH-4, which moves these handlers, so this lands before it or with it. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-169** — boolean query flags cannot be turned off. `z.coerce.boolean()` is `Boolean(raw)`: `"false"` and `"0"` parse as `true`, and only an empty value parses as `false`. Nine query flags use it: `byChannel` and `includeCompetitive` (`apps/api/src/analytics/analyticsRoutes.ts:98`, `:106`); `includeThreads`, `includePosts` and `includeAnalytics` of the export (`:122-124`); `hasMedia` and `includeArchived` of the post list (`apps/api/src/posts/postRoutes.ts:105`, `:115`), so `includeArchived=false` lists archived posts; the Admin audit-log `success` filter (`apps/api/src/admin/analyticsSchemas.ts:18`), so `success=false` returns the successes; and the scheduling rules' `isActive` filter (`apps/api/src/admin/schedulingSchemas.ts:68`). Proposed fix: one shared boolean query schema that accepts `"true"` and `"false"` and refuses anything else, applied to all nine flags, with a test per flag that `false` filters. Backlog SMELL-102. 🔗 related: ARCH-4, which moves the analytics handlers. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-170** — a channel whose credentials cannot be decrypted blocks the promotion of a post whose publication already completed. `CompletePostPublishingUseCase.resolveProviders` needs only each channel's provider type (`packages/core/posts/src/CompletePostPublishingUseCase.ts:326-347`), but it reads it through `ChannelRepository.findById` (`:338`). That adapter decrypts the credentials while it maps the row (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaChannelRepository.ts:123-133`, decryption at `:82`) and throws on an invalid provider (`:79`). Decryption throws on an AAD mismatch, an unknown key version or a bad tag (`apps/api/src/security/ChannelCredentialsCrypto.ts:86-99`; `apps/api/src/security/EncryptionService.ts:186`, `:198`, `:239`). The throw bypasses the unresolved-channel path the method was written for (`CompletePostPublishingUseCase.ts:339-342`) and fails the promotion after the providers have published. Every promotion also decrypts every channel's credentials for a value it never reads. Proposed fix: a `ChannelRepository` method that returns the provider as a `Result` without reading credentials, used by `resolveProviders`, with a test where an undecryptable envelope lands in `unresolvedChannelIds`. Backlog SMELL-121. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-171** — the API process creates a dead-letter manager that nothing consumes. `apps/api/src/index.ts:387-391` calls `createDeadLetterQueue` (`packages/adapters/dead-letter-queue/src/index.ts:590-595`) and stores the result in the unused `_deadLetterQueue`: the call only sets the module's global instance. The breaker's default is `deadLetterEnabled: true` (`packages/adapters/external-apis/src/circuitBreaker.ts:105`). So in that process, every call whose retries run out goes to `addFailedOperation` (`circuitBreaker.ts:920-946`), for example S3 upload signing (`packages/adapters/storage-s3/src/index.ts:128-131`). That method adds a job to the `dead-letter-queue` BullMQ queue (`dead-letter-queue/src/index.ts:268`), and the breaker logs "Operation queued for later retry" (`circuitBreaker.ts:946`). No code calls `startProcessing` (`:294`), `registerRetryTarget` (`:151`) or `cleanup` (`:530`). The callers pass `args` as `[]` (e.g. `packages/providers/x/src/apiClient.ts:135`), so nothing could replay the call: each entry stays in Redis with its error stack, and the log promises a retry that never happens. The canonical `BullMQDeadLetterQueueAdapter` writes a different payload into the same queue name (`packages/adapters/queue-bullmq/src/dead-letter-queue-adapter.ts:28`). The composition root registers it (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/setupServices.ts:698-704`), but no code resolves it, and its `list` and `retry` return `NOT_IMPLEMENTED` (`dead-letter-queue-adapter.ts:53,66`). Proposed fix: delete `@adapters/dead-letter-queue` and the breaker's dead-letter branch, or send that branch through `DeadLetterQueuePort` with a consumer, so that no log promises a retry nobody runs. Backlog FN-005. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.
> - [ ] **DEF-172** — Admin's queue health panel shows a per-queue status that nothing measures. `apps/admin/components/maintenance/QueueHealthPanel.tsx:15-31` copies 15 queue names by hand, and the panel paints every one with the single status it derives from `stats` (`:43-49`, `:80`). `stats` comes from `GET /admin/queue/stats`, which counts the publish queue alone (`apps/api/src/admin/queueRoutes.ts:68-80`; registered with `{ queue: publishQueue }` at `apps/api/src/index.ts:598`). So a failed `triage-inbox` job shows green, and one failed publish job turns all 15 red. The list includes three queues with no producer and no consumer: `recurring-posts` and `report-generation` (their constants claim a cron at `packages/adapters/queue-bullmq/src/constants.ts:35-39`, while recurring posts run in-process through `RecurrenceScheduler`, `apps/api/src/index.ts:919-926`, and no code runs scheduled reports) and `failed-operations-dlq` (`constants.ts:24-30`). It also leaves out four live queues: `mention-ingest`, `bulk-schedule`, `bulk-schedule-dead-letter` and `gateway-switch`. Proposed fix: the API serves counts for every live `QUEUE_NAMES` entry, the panel renders that response, and the orphan constants are deleted, with the two pending support candidates they leave (`docs/support/classification/non-features.json:118-119`) and fitness #46's `pendingBaseline` lowered by two in the same diff. Backlog FN-017, and the orphan-constant half of FN-021. It waits its turn in this queue, like the rest of §5.11.

> **Added on 2026-10-29, by ALR-U6-9's own implementation** — DEF-173 and DEF-174, the first found while editing in a linked worktree, the second while checking the §7 ledger's FN provenance; recorded here per decision F2 (§5.17): a finding outside a task's scope is a row in the same pull request.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-173** — the post-edit hook reports a false secret leak on every edit of an ignored file in a linked worktree that has no `node_modules`. `.claude/hooks-py/post_edit.py` `scan_target()` (`:68-97`) scans from the file's own checkout only when that checkout holds `node_modules/.bin/secretlint`; otherwise it falls back to `PROJECT_ROOT` "with the path as given", an absolute path, and secretlint matches `.secretlintignore` (`docs/**` at `:42`, `CLAUDE.md` at `:43`, `**/*.md` at `:53`) against paths relative to its working directory, so no pattern can match. Measured on 2026-10-29 in `alr-u6-9`: each Edit of `CLAUDE.md` printed `BLOCKED [post-edit]: secretlint detected a possible leak … PostgreSQL connection string` for the documented dev database URL at `CLAUDE.md:87`, and the edit applied anyway, so the message trains agents to ignore the hook. **DoD:** when the file's checkout holds `.secretlintrc.json` and `.secretlintignore` but not the binary, the hook runs `PROJECT_ROOT`'s `node_modules/.bin/secretlint` by absolute path with that checkout as its working directory and the path relative to it (never `pnpm exec`, which installs), and a test under `.claude/hooks-py/tests/` pins an ignored file edited in a second checkout.
> - [ ] **DEF-174** — the Admin webhook subscription form's project selector is always empty (the inventory's FN-029, never a backlog row). `fetchProjectsForSubscriptionForm` (`apps/admin/hooks/api/useWebhooks/api.ts:79-96`) fetches `/api/backend/projects`, a path the Admin proxy forwards to an API route that does not exist, as its own docstring states (`:81-84`); the `!res.ok` and `catch` branches return `[]`, so production shows an empty selector and no error. The inventory left the permission open: which projects an Admin may list. **DoD:** the Product Owner decides that scope; the API serves that list to the Admin principal, the selector reads it, and a component test pins a populated selector and a surfaced error.

> **Added on 2026-10-29, by COV-FLOOR-LAG's verification** — DEF-175, found by the unit's independent verifier while replaying `autoUpdate`'s rewrite of the coverage floors; recorded here per decision F2 (§5.17): a finding outside a task's scope is a row in the same pull request.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-175** — a local coverage run can lower a committed coverage floor of `apps/api/vitest.config.ts`. vitest's `autoUpdate` rewrites the config whenever a run measures above a floor, by any amount, and the formatter `(Math.floor(n * 10) - 1) / 10` writes the measurement's decimal minus one, so a measurement less than 0.1 pp above a floor writes that floor minus 0.1: floor 60 measured at 60.05 writes 59.9, floor 50.3 measured at 50.39 writes 50.2. With the margins `PR cvlag` leaves (0.13 to 0.18 pp), a drop of 0.04 pp (functions) to 0.09 pp (lines) or more, up to each floor's margin, in a local run lowers a literal; fitness #37 refuses the lowered literal in a pull request, but CI's "Surface a risen coverage floor" step (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:277-311`) would report such a fall as a rise. The config's own comment states the case since `PR cvlag`. **DoD:** the formatter never writes below the committed floor (it clamps to the current literal, or formats only a measurement at least 0.1 pp above it), pinned by a test on its edge values, and the CI step's message tells a fall from a rise.
>
> **Added on 2026-10-30, by WU-2.8.1b's deletion** — DEF-176, found by the unit's writer when the existence-only block that reached the branch left, and recorded in the slice's own pull request as §Findings outside a task's scope asks.
>
> - [ ] **DEF-176** — `getActionFromRequest` never returns `ACCOUNT_CREATED` or `POST_CREATED`: `apps/api/src/audit/auditMiddleware.ts:142` and `:156` test `method === "POST" && !path.includes("/")` inside branches entered only by a path that starts with `/accounts` or `/posts`, so the second condition is always false and `POST /accounts` and `POST /posts` fall through to no audit action (the project branch at `:148` reads `includes("/projects")` and works). Hidden until WU-2.8.1b (2026-10-30) deleted the existence-only block `should detect POST_CREATED action from /posts POST` (`apps/api/tests/unit/auditMiddleware.test.ts:235` at `f774b048`), which asserted only that `request.auditLog` was truthy. **DoD:** both branches test the collection path (`path === "/accounts"`, `path === "/posts"`, or the matched route pattern), and one behavioural test per action `getActionFromRequest` returns pins it, the two above first: the deleted `Action Detection from Request` suite inspected no action, so login, logout, the project and post actions, publish and cache-clear are untested as well; found by WU-2.8.1b.

### 5.12 Routes → use cases and the composition root (ADR-0033) — queued after N-TEST-1

> **ℹ️ CARD [ROUTES-ROOT] — decided and documented (2026-10-05); nothing implemented. Queued: it does not start until N-TEST-1 and the legal register (§5.10), which goes first, have closed; its place relative to §5.9 and §5.11 is set at that point.**
> Item (v) of Phase 0 left the architecture gate reading the whole import graph (ADR-0032) and checked against six primary sources the 509 edges the previous policy rejected: the canon allows 460, 43 are real violations and 6 needed a decision. The Maintainer decided on 2026-10-05 that route modules receive their dependencies as parameters from the single composition root, instead of resolving them from the container (ADR-0033). On 2026-10-05 item (v) delivered the gate (PR v, #408), the baseline that only shrinks (PR v-b, #411), the core's `LoggerPort` (PR v-d1, #413 and PR v-d2, #414) and the repairs that need no design (`cacheStatsRoutes`, `queueRoutes` and `healthRoutes`, in PR v-e1, #415 to PR v-e3, #417; see ARCH-19); this card is the rest: each route module moves to use cases with injection from the root, area by area. Each slice is a PR of up to about 400 CODE lines (a moved line counts twice), with its tests and its documentation, and it removes its baseline entries in the same change; its DoD is the §0.2 bar plus the architecture gate green, with no stale entries.
>
> **Decisions:** [ADR-0032](../technical/ADR-0032-architecture-gate-dependency-cruiser.md) (one architecture gate, dependency-cruiser 18.x over the whole resolved graph, with a baseline that only shrinks; `eslint-plugin-boundaries` retires, SMELL-183) and [ADR-0033](../technical/ADR-0033-layer-boundaries-and-composition-root-injection.md) (D-P1 = B, the guards, D-R10, P2b and application-owned ports).
>
> **Deviation status, stated plainly:** until its area migrates, 74 of the 78 route modules still resolve from the container, 23 still touch Prisma, a repository or the email port, and five construct value objects (76 and 24 when this was decided; PR v-e1 and PR v-e3 took `cacheStatsRoutes` and `healthRoutes` off the container, and the latter stopped touching Prisma). Those edges are admitted only as entries of the ADR-0032 baseline; a new module, or a new edge in an existing one, fails the gate.
>
> **Totals:** the 25 sized route files add up to ~15 200 CODE lines (11 400–19 000) in 49 slices (55 if the six marked ⚠ are split), of which item (v) delivered `healthRoutes` (~190) (PR v-e3, #417, `workstream/item-v-cheap-health`, `783aeebe`); the other 51 route modules, ~1670 in about 5 slices; the convention for route dependencies at the root, ~40; the guards, unsized. Uncertainty of ±25 % per file, which the verbosity of the repository's tests pushes upward.
>
> **First proposed slice: TSK-1 (ARCH-15, tasks)**, about 300 lines: 260 plus the convention for route dependencies at the root (~40), which is born with it. It is the smallest area that sets the whole pattern in a single slice:
>
> - a write use case with Unit of Work (`DeleteTaskUseCase`, on `TaskRepository.findById` and `softDelete`, which already exist), the shape the write areas repeat;
> - the plugin starts receiving its dependencies from the root, with the class-based handler (`BaseRouteHandler`) that 17 of the 25 files use;
> - when it is done, the whole module sits on use cases: the other six handlers already resolve them and its only R2 edge (`TaskRepository`) disappears;
> - `taskRoutes.ts` has no suite that registers it today, so the slice writes the first route suite with fake dependencies passed to the plugin: the harness template;
> - it does not touch fitness #40, waits for no decision and does not mix a defect into the same diff.
>
> The smaller areas do not set the pattern: operations (~40) creates no use case, custom reports (150) only has R11 and SSO (230) reuses existing queries. Notifications (380) would set it too, but with the convention it goes over 400 and drags in DEF-10. The `accountId` that `taskRoutes.ts` takes from the query string or the body is the class of N-AUTH-3 (the file appears in its proposal's baseline, `docs/features/principal-derived-authority/proposal.md`) and does not change in this slice.
>
> **Findings → §5.11** (DEF-7 to DEF-15, which carry SMELL-194 to SMELL-202): nine defects read statically during the sizing and its verification; the rows below name the ones that live in their code.
>
> **Sources:** the sizing `boundaries-repair-sizing.md` and the classification `boundaries-violations-509.classified.json` (`/root/.claude/omnipost-tools/research-2026-10-04/`, outside the repository; engram 1213 and 1211), measured on 2026-10-05 on `main` `4ffe981b`; the code of `apps/api/src`, `packages/` and `infra/` did not change up to `0e51032a`.

**Route slices** (estimates in CODE lines from the sizing, with their interval; slice ids in parentheses; ⚠ = a slice at the 400 limit, split if it exceeds it when measured):

- [ ] **ARCH-1** **API+PKG** — accounts, `accounts/accountRoutes.ts` (7 handlers): `CreateAccountUseCase` (normalizes the email inside), `GetAccountQuery`, `ListAccountsQuery` (with a port of live projects: `findWithProjects` includes the deleted ones) and `UpdateAccountUseCase` (with a `maxProjects` mutator); R11 `AdminActorId` and `EmailAddress` move to the use case. ~1030 (850–1300), 3 slices (ACC-1 to ACC-3). N-AUTH-1 (§1, critical) changes the authorization of these same routes and BILL-21 (§5.9) retires `maxProjects`: the order with both is set at planning. ADR-0033. **Note 2026-10-18:** N-AUTH-1 closed; `POST /accounts` and `GET /accounts` no longer exist and `PUT` writes only `name`, so the file has 5 handlers and `CreateAccountUseCase`, `ListAccountsQuery`, the `maxProjects` mutator and the route's `EmailAddress` leave the scope; the ~1030-line estimate predates that closure.
- [ ] **ARCH-2** **API+PKG** — projects, `projects/projectRoutes.ts` (7): `CreateProjectUseCase` (with a port that counts the occupied slots, deleted ones included), `ListProjectsQuery`, `GetProjectQuery` and `GetProjectPublishLogsQuery`; the pre-deletion check goes, since `DeleteProjectUseCase` already makes it; R11 `AdminActorId` in three places. ~1010 (850–1300), 3 slices (PRJ-1 ⚠, PRJ-2, PRJ-3). The quota check PRJ-1 moves into the use case is the one BILL-21 (§5.9) retires: if BILL-21 goes first, PRJ-1 shrinks. ADR-0033.
- [ ] **ARCH-3** **API+PKG** — channels, `channels/channelRoutes.ts` (8): seven use cases and queries (create, get, list with its usage, update, delete, delete permanently and what is left of mark as primary, whose `SetPrimaryChannelUseCase` already exists), an ownership policy in the core and a credential verification port; R1 and R7 in the Bluesky connection (`new BlueskyClient` at `:462`); R11 in the construction of the ids. ~1760 (1400–2200), 5 slices (CH-1 to CH-5; CH-3 ⚠). CH-5 waits for a decision: the Bluesky credential (`{identifier, appPassword}`) is encrypted today by the route itself (`:483`), because `ChannelRepository.save` only encrypts the OAuth shape, and its shape in the port is still to be decided. ADR-0033.

Four endpoints answer 501 behind the gate (`:442,476,511,541`). ~1290 (1000–1600), 4 slices (AN-1 to AN-4). ADR-0033.

AA-5 moves the code of DEF-8. ADR-0033.

Moves the code of DEF-13. ADR-0033.

Takes two sites out of fitness #40 (ARCH-22). ADR-0033.

Takes one site out of fitness #40 (ARCH-22). ADR-0033.

With that, this route loses R4, R5 and its R1: it no longer resolves `PrismaClient`, the root constructs the single `db-prisma` adapter and its single connection monitor, and the route has no container resolution left. R5 in `queueRoutes` was delivered in PR v-e2 (#416, `workstream/item-v-cheap-queue`, `33476917`: it receives the publish queue the root constructs) and R6 in PR v-e1 (#415, `workstream/item-v-cheap-repairs`, `05f1d28f`: `cacheStatsRoutes` receives the `CacheAdminPort`). `trends/trendRoutes.ts` (5) remains: `TrendAnalysisService` is constructed at the root, without the `prisma` parameter it does not use. ~40 after item (v) (the sizing measured ~230 with `healthRoutes`), 1 slice (OPS-1). Moves the code of DEF-12. ADR-0033.

With work of their own and unsized: `reports/reportRoutes.ts:270`, one of the 51, resolves `ScheduledReportRepository` per request (an R2 class the classification did not see), and `auth/providerOAuth.ts:25-35`, which does not resolve from the container, receives repositories as parameters and constructs `OAuthFlowStore` (`:32`). ADR-0033. 🔗 dep:ARCH-15.

They are the prerequisite for retiring Fastify's `container` decoration (`index.ts:280`, `types/fastify.d.ts:62`), which also waits for ARCH-1 to ARCH-20. Unsized. ADR-0033.

The transaction `outbox/outboxAdminRoutes.ts:73` opens moves to a use case with Unit of Work in OBX-2 (ARCH-11), not in item (v), because it moves fitness #40: its Part B counts 13 sites today against a floor of 10, and four leave when their operations use the Unit of Work, whose single site is `tenantTransaction.ts:55` — `outboxAdminRoutes.ts:73` (OBX-2), `SchedulingPostHandlers.ts:249` and `:349` (SA-2, SA-3) and `SchedulingSlotHandlers.ts:418` (SC-7). The slice that leaves the count below 10 lowers the floor deliberately, with its red proven, and each slice that takes a file out of the seam updates `tenantTransactionNesting.test.ts` in the same change: its `INDEPENDENT_BY_DESIGN` entry and `SEAM_FILE_POPULATION` (16 today, a floor equal to the measured population). ADR-0033.

### 5.13 Dead code — decisions of 2026-10-05

> **ℹ️ CARD [DEAD-CODE] — twelve cards on the inventory of unused code; nothing has been deleted yet. No deletion unit starts before this section is on `main`, and those of C8, C9, C11 and C12 also wait for the explicit approval of the Product Owner and the Maintainer, given on this section.**
>
> **Where the decisions live:** [ADR-0041](../technical/ADR-0041-dead-code-cards-and-retired-intentions.md) records the condition of 2026-10-05 that keeps each intention, the cards C1, C2, C3, C4, C5, C6, C7, C8, C9, C10, C11 and C12 with what each retires (Decision 2, the card table: "the table" of DC-4, DC-11 and DC-12 below is that table), the ledger of each retired intention and where it lives (including the per-network scheduler and accessors the canon declines under §0.4, the audit retention of GDPR-3 (§5.2, roadmap §4.3.d), and FN-010/FN-027, closed by AUTH-RL-1), the product questions PREG-1 to PREG-11 decided on 2026-10-06, and what stays open outside that mapping (FN-023 and FN-025, the quota figures of AUTH-RL-1 stage 2, the KPI set of BI-KPI-1); the runtime findings are DEF-30 to DEF-38 (§5.11).

**Deletion units** (provisional ids, one per card, plus DC-13 for decision D4 of the credential model and DC-14 for the correction of Tailwind's `@source` directive, DEF-49; DC-1 to DC-7 and DC-13 are decided, DC-8, DC-9, DC-11 and DC-12 wait for the approval of the Product Owner and the Maintainer, and DC-14 for the Maintainer's). Proposed order, the order of the decision sessions: DC-1 → DC-2 → DC-3 → DC-4 → DC-5 → DC-6 → DC-7 and, with that approval, DC-8 → DC-9 → DC-11 → DC-12; DC-13, decided on 2026-10-06, goes last, and DC-14, added that day, when DEF-49 is on `main` and the Maintainer approves it. Units with disjoint files can run in parallel; the measured constraints: DC-1 and DC-6 edit `apps/api/src/index.ts` and go in series; DC-13 edits that same file, as DC-2 does, and the schema, as DC-7, DC-8 and DC-9 do, and goes in series with them; `validator` and `@types/validator` (catalog, override and their CVE-floor row in SECURITY_CANON) leave only when DC-3 and DC-4 are both on `main`, because their only two importers are `secureSchemas.ts` and `enhancedValidator.ts`; the decisions of C1 and C2 place their units after the chain of slice 0.22 where they share files, and that of C2 also after DEF-29. They run in parallel with what is left of N-TEST-1, one certified PR per unit and disjoint files between simultaneous units, without displacing the route; deleting C1, C4, C6 and C11 before their phases 2 and S saves work already planned on tests of dead code (WU-2.8.1a, 2.8.1b, 2.8.2, 2.8.4 and S.4; `docs/development/TESTING_REFOUNDATION_PLAN.md:949-961,999-1005`). Each unit is a certified PR of about 400 CODE lines as a heuristic, split by module group if it exceeds them; it updates its rows of `docs/reports/UNUSED_CODE_INVENTORY.md` in the same PR and reruns `pnpm lint` with no warnings, `pnpm exec tsc -b --force`, `node scripts/knip-ratchet.mjs` (`--write` to shrink the ledger), `pnpm check:duplicates` (stale jscpd fingerprints fail), `apps/api/scripts/typecheck-tests-ratchet.mjs`, `pnpm check:architecture` and the suites of each package touched; if it touches the schema, `pnpm db:up` first, the migration and the RLS coverage gate. `apps/api` has 773 test files against `TEST_FILE_FLOOR = 700` (`apps/api/scripts/typecheck-tests-ratchet.mjs:31`): DC-1 to DC-6 and DC-11 remove about 45, DC-8 another 15 and DC-13 another 3, and lowering the floor requires its written reason.

The three were written because "the Tailwind scanner cannot follow the symlinks of pnpm workspace packages"; it was not so: the `@source` path of `packages/ui` was the wrong one (DEF-49), and with that path corrected Tailwind's oxide scanner extracts from `packages/ui/src` the 124 classes of the list (measured on 2026-10-06), so the list is redundant. **Condition before deleting:** a visual review of Dialog, AlertDialog and Toast —and of any Popover Admin comes to render: the content of Radix's Popover carries `role="dialog"`, and Admin renders none today—, because the override block cancels the directional slide of every element with `role="dialog"` or `role="alertdialog"`: deleting it changes the animation of those dialogs, and the unit decides whether the rule goes or stays with a comment that says why. The comments the DEF-49 correction leaves in Admin's stylesheet already name this unit as their retirement condition. Added on 2026-10-06 by that correction. 🔗 dep:DEF-49.

It collects the intention of seven methods of `BusinessKPITracker` (`packages/observability/opentelemetry/src/businessMetrics.ts`, 2026-03), which were never called and go with card C6 (DC-6). 🔗 dep: BILL-19.

Today the portals only run Sentry, and three attempts to measure usage never ran: `UserJourneyContext` (`packages/observability/opentelemetry/src/correlationTracking.ts:26`), `feature_adoption_total` and `user_retention_rate` of `BusinessKPITracker` (`businessMetrics.ts:35-44`) and `AnalyticsInstrumentation` (`customInstrumentation.ts:338`), which go with cards C4 and C6 (§5.13). **DoD:** the use cases, or the outbox and audit events that already exist, emit product events —feature adoption, journeys at route and use-case level, usage retention— to an in-house store, with their dashboards in Admin; no third-party SDK in `apps/client` or in `apps/admin`; the marketing site, if it is measured, uses cookieless analytics. Before the first event, LEGAL-1 sets the purpose and the legal basis —that events emitted from the server need no consent is a reading of the EDPB guidelines 2/2023, not a regulator's confirmation, and it is decided there— and the LEGAL-3 gate covers the event schema as a personal-data surface. It feeds BI-KPI-1 with adoption and retention. 🔗 dep: LEGAL-1, LEGAL-3.

### 5.14 Credential model — decisions of 2026-10-06

> **ℹ️ CARD [CREDENTIAL-MODEL] — decided and documented in [ADR-0034](../technical/ADR-0034-credential-model.md) (2026-10-06, #433). Unit B (DEF-37) merged on 2026-10-06 (#434, with its complement B.1, #435); only PR 2 of DEF-29 is authorized now; the rest enters the queue by the route (Testing Refoundation → legal register → this plan in order).**
> On 2026-10-05, faced with DEF-36 to DEF-38, the Product Owner and the Maintainer chose option B: a surgical unit now (the repository stops projecting `Channel.credentials`, DEF-37) and an ADR of the credential model, with the per-provider redesign as slice 1 of §2F; before building, they asked for the inventory of every credential in the environment and in the database, so that the unit would not be isolated from the rest. The inventory, measured on `main` `8710327d` (39 live secrets in the environment; 28 columns with secrets in the database: 5 encrypted families, 9 hashed, 8 recoverable secrets and 6 plaintext verifiers; 8 Redis namespaces and an in-memory store), found the core well solved and the inconsistency around it. On 2026-10-06 the Maintainer closed four decisions, which ADR-0034 brings together in a single model:
>
> **D1+D2, D3 and D4:** recorded as one model in [ADR-0034](../technical/ADR-0034-credential-model.md) — D1+D2 as decision 2 (final version under ADR-0025 and ADR-0028), D3 as decisions 3 to 5 and 9, D4 as decision 11 (BYOK, `IntegrationApiKey`, the CRM connections, and the customer `ApiKey` slice deleted by DC-13, §5.13); the public API's credential is PREG-1 (PUBAPI-1 and PUBAPI-2, §3).
>
> **State of the engine, measured in execution (§5.11):** before #429 no channel published (DEF-29); since that merge only Bluesky publishes, because the OAuth flow stores three generic fields and each adapter requires fields of its own network (DEF-36); saving a Bluesky channel destroyed its credentials until unit B (DEF-37, #434 and #435); comments are neither synced nor answered (DEF-38), and analytics ingestion persists nothing (DEF-32).
>
> **Order of the sequence:** #430 (merged) → unit B (DEF-37, about 215 CODE lines; #434 and #435, merged on 2026-10-06) → PR 2 of DEF-29, which cites ADR-0034 → CRED-4 → CRED-5 → CRED-6 and CRED-7 → CRED-8a to CRED-8c → CRED-14 → CRED-15a to CRED-15d → CRED-9a to CRED-9c and CRED-10. CRED-1 goes after PR 2 and DC-3; CRED-2, CRED-3, CRED-11 and CRED-12 depend on nothing and, being cheap, can go at any point; the three gates of CRED-13 arrive in parts (the cipher one after PR 2, the environment-list one right after, the column-inventory one after CRED-5); each row names its dependencies. STOR-3 and BILL-5 (§5.9) are still the owners of the storage and gateway moves to Admin; CRED-8a to CRED-8c are their siblings, not their duplicates.
>
> **Findings → §5.11:** DEF-39 to DEF-45 (TOTP seeds, CRM tokens and reset and invitation tokens in plaintext; the workers' logger with no redaction; the AI pool key read from two sources; a dead key required at boot; rotation on paper). The in-memory OIDC PKCE state was already DEF-9.
>
> **Sources:** the inventory `credential-inventory.md` and the plan of the ADR and of unit B, `credential-model-adr-and-b-unit-plan.md` (`/root/.claude/omnipost-tools/research-2026-10-05/`, outside the repository; engram 1300 and 1308), and the decisions in engram 1298 and 1303 to 1306.

**Credential model slices** (detail in [ADR-0034](../technical/ADR-0034-credential-model.md); sizes in CODE lines according to the unit's plan; a slice of more than about 400 is split):

- [ ] **CRED-1** **API+DOCS** — the dead secrets DC-3 does not cover: `JWT_SECRET` (`apps/api/src/config/env.ts:105`, optional and with no reader) leaves the schema, and `SHADOW_DATABASE_URL` (`:83`) becomes optional in the API, because only `infra/prisma/prisma.config.ts` uses it; `OAUTH_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, `ADMIN_EMAIL` and `BCRYPT_ROUNDS` are deleted by DC-3 (§5.13), which closes DEF-44. ~10 CODE. ADR-0034 (decision 13). 🔗 dep:DEF-29, DC-3.
- [ ] **CRED-2** **API+WRK+PKG** — a single redaction list: `REDACT_PATHS` moves to `@observability/logger` as an exported constant consumed by `apps/api/src/lib/logger.ts` and by the packages' base logger, which gains `redact`; the list adds `appPassword`, `clientSecret`, `appSecret`, `botToken`, `secretKey`, `webhookSecret`, `mfaSecret`, `backupCodes`, `codeVerifier`, `webhookUrl`, `targetUrl`, `privateKey`, `accessTokenSecret` and the nested forms; tests that write a credentials object through each logger and read the masks; the LOGGING_CANON row is amended (ADR-0013). Closes DEF-42. ~120 CODE. ADR-0034 (decision 8).
- [ ] **CRED-3** **API+PKG** — the OIDC PKCE state and `state` move from the process `Map` to the `OAuthFlowStore` pattern in Redis (600 s TTL), and `CachePort` gains `take(key)` (`GETDEL` in Redis; the in-memory adapter imitates it), so both stores consume atomically. Closes DEF-9. It shares `auth/oidcRoutes.ts` with ARCH-17 (§5.12): the order between the two is set at planning. ~130 CODE. ADR-0034 (decision 7).
- [ ] **CRED-4** **API+WRK+PKG+INFRA** — the encryption seam becomes asynchronous: `EncryptionPort.encrypt` and `decrypt` return promises and all their callers await them (`EncryptionService`, `ChannelCredentialsCrypto`, the five repositories —`PrismaChannelRepository.toDomain` maps synchronously today—, `PlatformCredentialService`, the workers' `ChannelCredentialsDecryptor` and the seed), with their tests. ~350–450 CODE; two PRs if it exceeds that (core and API; workers and seed). ADR-0034 (decision 4). 🔗 dep:DEF-29.
- [ ] **CRED-5** **PKG+API+WRK+INFRA** — the port and the data-key layer: `KeyEncryptionProvider` in `packages/ports`; `@adapters/field-encryption` (`FieldEncryptionService`, an in-process data-key cache with a TTL of 300 s at most, the `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS` registry, reading in both modes); `@adapters/key-provider-env`; the `KEY_PROVIDER` fragment with its interlock in both environment schemas; the migration that adds `wrappedDek`, `keyRef` and `wrapAlgorithm`, with their index, to the five encrypted families; `EncryptionService` as a wrapper that audits, and the real contract suite against `env`. Two PRs (port, package, adapter and suite, ~350; schema, roots and wrapper, ~300). ADR-0034 (decisions 3 to 5). 🔗 dep:CRED-4.
- [ ] **CRED-6** **PKG+API+INFRA+DOCS** — the re-encryption job and a single rotation log: `RotateEncryptedColumnsUseCase` on an `EncryptedColumnRepository` port with its adapter in `packages/adapters/db-prisma`, run as a command from the API root under `withSystemContext`, idempotent and resumable, which rewrites in envelope mode the rows in direct mode or under another key and normalizes the AAD of the two legacy contexts of `PlatformCredential` and `AccountCredential` (what was left of normalizing `PlatformCredentialService`: PR 2 of DEF-29 already puts it on the single cipher); the job writes `SecretRotationLog`; `PlatformEncryptionKey`, its Admin rotation endpoint and `logEncryptionKeyRotation` go; the message of `EncryptionService.ts:241` names the job and the rotation runbook is rewritten; tested against the real shape of each family before a column is deleted. Closes DEF-45 together with CRED-11. ~300 CODE. ADR-0034 (decision 9). 🔗 dep:CRED-5.
- [ ] **CRED-7** **PKG+INFRA+CI** — the Vault adapter: `@adapters/key-provider-vault` on `fetch`, with no SDK (`datakey/plaintext`, `decrypt`, `rewrap`, rotation and `min_decryption_version`); a Vault service in `docker-compose.yml` beside MinIO (image pinned per ADR-0018, dev mode, transit engine enabled by a startup step), its `services:` entry in the CI integration job and the real contract suite as a batch of `apps/api/scripts/run-tests.sh` with `TIER=pr-integration`, which fails without the server; a step in the battery and a row in `docs/development/TESTING_REFOUNDATION.md`. Should Vault's BSL license ever matter, OpenBao (MPL-2.0, transit-compatible) is the fallback image, which the unit verifies before relying on it. ~300 CODE. ADR-0034 (decision 5). 🔗 dep:CRED-5.
- [ ] **CRED-8a** **API+WRK+ADM+PKG** — the networks' app registrations from Admin: the `SOCIAL_*` groups store only app registrations (the class T keys of the single-tenant era go); `providerOAuthConfigs.ts` resolves them from `PlatformCredentialService`; the Prisma repository of `PlatformCredential` moves to `packages/adapters/db-prisma` so the workers' root can construct the service; the eight `*_CLIENT_ID`/`*_CLIENT_SECRET` pairs and their `*_REDIRECT_URI` leave the environment (the redirect is derived from the base URL of the `PLATFORM` group), with `probe()` and write-only fields in Admin. Closes DEF-45 together with CRED-6. ~250 CODE. ADR-0034 (decision 9).

(a) The cipher gate: `createCipheriv` and `createDecipheriv` only in the canonical module, with a floor on its own calls (task T-3 of DEF-29 reserved it as #45; named exceptions: `credentialManager.ts` until DC-4 and `video/uploadPipeline.ts:638` until DC-8), after PR 2 of DEF-29.
